# UI conformance register

Phase C of [`spec/ui-conformance-plan.md`](../../spec/ui-conformance-plan.md). Every finding is
one row; every row is assigned to exactly one work package. This is the only place findings live.
Builders close rows; validators add rows; the lead confirms or rejects.

**Evidence.** Emulator screenshots taken 2026-09-07 on a Pixel 6 / API 34 AVD (`ort_audit`,
port 5554) of the app at `6aaa608`, and the source under `app/src/main/kotlin/org/ort/app/` at
`bff688b` (which includes audit fixes F-002, F-004, F-017, F-018, F-019 merged during phase A).
Each row names the artboard it was compared against; the artboards are under
[`design/canvas/`](../../design/canvas/) and the tokens in [`design/design-guide.md`](../../design/design-guide.md).

**Severity.** `halt` â€” wrong or misleading to the operator (constitution I) Â· `spec` â€” an
FR / P / F not met Â· `design` â€” the artboard not matched Â· `polish`.

**Status.** `open` Â· `building` (a builder owns it) Â· `fixed` (builder reports it closed, lead
not yet confirmed) Â· `closed` (confirmed on the emulator) Â· `rejected` (lead disagrees; reason
in the row).

---

| R-005 | every | `Type` | `OrtType.typography` maps M3 `bodyMedium` to the 12.5px caption and `bodyLarge` to 15px, so every screen that asks for `MaterialTheme.typography.bodyMedium` gets caption size for body copy. | R-006 | every | `Tokens` | `OrtTheme` sets a colour scheme but no root `Surface`; the window ground is whatever `DeviceDefault` gives. Screens must sit on `bg/screen` `oklch(0.155 0.008 250)` (â‰ˆ `#202329`) from the theme, not from the OS. | design | closed |
| R-007 | every | â€” | `ReaderActivity` is `exported="false"` and `MainActivity` `finish()`es after launching it; the back stack after a cold start is `Reader` alone, which is right â€” but a process death mid-session relaunches into `MainActivity`'s permission flow rather than the reader. Verify and route. Sweep @669628a: cannot judge from a capture â€” process-death back-stack routing is lifecycle behaviour; covered by the real-Activity smoke tests instead. Sweep @669628a: lifecycle behaviour, not visual; covered by the real-Activity smoke tests. | polish | closed |
| R-008 | every | guide Â§12 | Found on the emulator after WP1 merged (`8ad7cc1`): status-bar clock and icons render dark on the dark ground on both activities â€” `enableEdgeToEdge()` with no arguments chooses icon colour from the OS night-mode setting, and this app is dark-only. Both activities must pass `SystemBarStyle.dark(TRANSPARENT)` for status and navigation bars, and `themes.xml` must set `windowLightStatusBar`/`windowLightNavigationBar` false. WP7 (diagnose) â†’ `:data`. DIAGNOSED (WP7): the API 34 emulator's platform SQLite has no fts5 module ("no such module: fts5" at CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE); `OrtDatabase` assumes fts5 on minSdk 26 and only builds the index in onCreate. Lead decision: bundle a SQLite with FTS5 in `:data` (also fixes Robolectric's missing fts5). :data bc9bc51/c009d7e: bundled `androidx.sqlite:sqlite-bundled` with FTS5 behind `BundledSQLiteDriver` (Room callbacks now run via `inWriteTransaction`/`execRaw`), 10 s busy timeout, schema v4. Text search works on API 34 and under Robolectric. V5 search half confirms on device. ## Field session 1 - device report (2026-09-12) - R-1000 base

**Evidence.** The operator's own run of the rolling `latest-build` debug APK at `680dc15d` on the
reference phone (Oppo Find X9 Ultra, 1440x3168 @ 480 dpi, ~480 dp wide, ColorOS): full onboarding,
then ~30 minutes of local-microphone capture. Nineteen overs detected; every one failed Pass B.
Screenshot supplied by the operator (transmission detail, `not transcribed`, five ASR attempts).
Diagnosis by the lead against the source at `680dc15d` and against the built APKs under
`app/build/outputs/apk/`. Session plan: `docs/debug-fix-session-prompt.md` loop.

| id | screen / surface | artboard / requirement | what is wrong | sev | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R-1001 | Pass B, every transmission | FR-ASR-1, constitution VII | **No Android build of sherpa-onnx exists in the project.** `gradle/libs.versions.toml:18,69,70` declares only `sherpa-onnx-jvm` and `sherpa-onnx-native-lib-win-x64`; `asr-sherpa/build.gradle.kts:14-16` says so in its own comment. `:app` -> `:pipeline` -> `:asr-sherpa` therefore dexes the JNI wrapper classes with **Windows DLLs beside them**: both built APKs carry `sherpa-onnx/native/win-x64/onnxruntime.dll` (17,378,304 B) and `sherpa-onnx-jni.dll` (4,610,560 B) as inert Java resources and **no `lib/<abi>/libsherpa-onnx-jni.so`** (12 `lib/` entries, all from androidx/MediaPipe/sqlite). The upstream jar's `LibraryUtils.load()` has an explicit `isAndroid()` branch that skips DLL extraction and calls `System.loadLibrary("sherpa-onnx-jni")`, so the bindings are Android-capable and only the `.so` is absent. Build-plan P12 wired `RealAsrEngineProvider` into the live service (`RealCaptureService.kt:624-631`), promoting a Windows-desktop-only binding into the Android runtime path; no gate could catch it because the classes compile. Error surfaced honestly at `AsrEngineProvisioning.kt:80-87`. **WPJ.** | halt | fixed - WPJ `06c12772`, merged `e844e350`, pushed `91fb7824`, **CI `34681750721` and Release `34681750720` both green**, `latest-build` republished at that commit (602,967,042 B). Symbol compatibility proven before the load path was written: no NDK on this machine, so `pyelftools` read the real arm64-v8a `.so` - 133 exports, every one `Java_com_k2fsa_sherpa_onnx_*`, `DT_NEEDED` naming only `libonnxruntime.so` beyond system libs - and `javap -p` matched every native method `RealSherpaDecoder` calls. `FetchSherpaNativeTask` (mirroring `FetchBundledAssetsTask`) fetches upstream's public release tarball into gitignored `app/src/main/jniLibs/<abi>/`, checksummed from a real download; arm64-v8a for the phone, x86_64 so the audit AVDs can exercise ASR for the first time; the 22 MB of win-x64 DLLs are gone. **Lead verified the packaged APK directly** on the no-escape-hatch `main` build: `lib/arm64-v8a/libsherpa-onnx-jni.so` 4,761,536 B, `libonnxruntime.so` 21,684,880 B, x86_64 likewise, zero `sherpa-onnx/*` entries. New `PlatformGuards.missingNativeLibraryViolations` + `NativeLibraryPackagingGuardTask` read the **real packaged artifact** - the first guard in that family to do so, because a declared-coordinate check structurally cannot see a build-time-fetched binary - and it immediately caught a bug in itself: `getOrElse` on a managed `ListProperty` never fires (Gradle pre-populates it empty, not absent), so the guard silently checked **zero** libraries and pronounced a broken APK OK. On an x86_64 AVD the new instrumented test **reproduces the operator's exact `UnsatisfiedLinkError` with the fetch disabled** and returns a real transcript with it restored. **CLOSED on the operator's own device** - `debug-dump-2026-09-12.ndjson` from `111e761` on a CPH2841 (Oppo Find X9 Ultra), Android 16 / SDK 36: **six of eight overs reached `processingState: COMPLETE`**, and one was rejected with `REPETITION: 4-gram "North, North, North, North," repeats 4 times (>= 3)` - **that rejection reason is a real transcript**, so Whisper ran, produced text, and the hallucination guard fired on it. A guard cannot fire on output that does not exist. The eighth was `too_short` at 1392 ms. First row in this programme to close on hardware rather than an emulator. |

| R-1013 | S10b link probe | FR-RIG-3, FR-RIG-14, FR-RIG-15, constitution I + IV | **`DefaultRigLinkBridge.probe()` has no timeout anywhere** (`pipeline/.../rig/RigLinkBridge.kt:148-223`). If the SPP socket opens but the radio never answers the descriptor's identify command - wrong framing, an unexpected line terminator, a radio mode that needs setup before it will talk CAT, or simply not the rig the descriptor expects - `observe()` never emits and the transport is never *lost*, so nothing terminal ever arrives. **The flow sits at `Open` indefinitely**, which S10b renders as "Serial link open OK / Identify the rig (spinner) / Verify the command set (empty)" with `Continue` gated on `Verified`. Timeouts derived from the descriptor's own poll interval (x3 identify, x6 verify, floored 2 s, default 5 s) through two overridable constructor functions, never a bare constant. **The signal existed all along and nothing read it**: `DescriptorRigModule.readLine` already returned null after its 1 s `readTimeoutMs` and emitted `RigHealth.Degraded(TIMEOUT)`, which `probe()` never inspected - it backed off 50 ms and retried forever. Same hole one layer up is **R-1015**. **S10b rendering confirmed by capture** (`<scratch>/ort-tour-r1016/setup-rig-bluetooth/S10b-verify-timed-out.png`): an amber banner reading "Identified, but not every capability answered" over "Continuing will log this session with the command set only partially confirmed - see the checklist below for exactly what was not seen"; the checklist's third row is a **shape-distinct** amber ring-around-a-dot (not colour-only, so it survives greyscale) reading "1 of 3 seen - missing squelch, per band"; `Continue` renders in the enabled primary style and the escape hatch remains. **What the capture does NOT establish, and a correction to avoid a false record:** a `uiautomator dump` of this state could not be taken - the scenario broadcast alone does not reach it (the tour forces it through `DebugRigLinkPortOverride`), so an `am broadcast` plus a launcher start lands on the default screen where `Continue` is correctly disabled. The lead read `enabled=false` there and nearly filed it as a halt - a primary action styled tappable but inert - before checking that the dump contained none of the partial-state text at all. **The `Continue`-is-enabled claim therefore rests on the capture plus WPD3's own discriminating test, not on a device dump.** |
| R-1014 | S10b `Verified` | FR-RIG-3, constitution I | `Verified` requires `seenCapabilities.containsAll(declaredCapabilities)`, where `seenCapabilities` is derived from populated `RigState` fields by `capabilitiesPresentIn` (`RigLinkBridge.kt:268`). That function's own kdoc concedes `RigCapability.TIME` "has no corresponding `RigState` field today... a descriptor that declares only `TIME` would never reach `Verified`, which is an honest reflection of that gap rather than a defect introduced by this function." Honest in isolation - but wired to a `Continue` enabled only on `Verified`, **an underivable declared capability is a permanent lock**, and the same holds for any capability a real rig legitimately does not report in a given state (a quiet band reports no `SIGNAL_STRENGTH`; a VFO reports no `MEMORY_CHANNEL`). Whether it bites the TH-D75A specifically is for WPRIG to establish and may be theoretical; the general trap is fixed regardless. **WPRIG.** | halt | fixed - and **it does not bite the TH-D75A**, established before any code was written: `kenwood-thd75a.json` declares `{FREQUENCY, SQUELCH_STATE, SUB_BAND}` on both transports and `generic-ascii-cat.json` declares `{FREQUENCY, MODE}` - every one of which `capabilitiesPresentIn` derives directly. The trap is real only for a future descriptor declaring `TIME` (no `RigState` field exists for it at all) or a capability a rig legitimately never reports in a given session. Fixed generally by `VerifyTimedOut` **without loosening `Verified`'s meaning**, which stays "every declared capability was observed" - a useful, honest claim worth keeping. Lead's product decision: `VerifyTimedOut` enables `Continue` (the rig spoke; partial capability observation is normal operation - a quiet band reports no `SIGNAL_STRENGTH`) and must **never** render as `Verified` anywhere; `IdentifyTimedOut` does not (nothing answered, so there is no link). Android's accessibility delegate pads a clickable node's reported bounds to the 48 dp minimum touch target; the row is a deliberate 44 dp flush against the bottom edge, so the deficit went upward past the column's top and `Rect.contains()` rejected correct geometry. ## Field session 2 - the operator's own logs (2026-09-12, later)

**Evidence.** Three artefacts the operator saved from the shipped build at `111e761` and put in
Drive: `diagnostics-2026-09-12.zip`, `debug-dump-2026-09-12.ndjson`, `ort-export-all-20260912-183854.adi`.
Device **CPH2841** (Oppo Find X9 Ultra), Android 16 / SDK 36. Two sessions: one of 12 minutes
producing 7 overs, one of 30 seconds producing 1. All four observations below were traced to source
before being filed.

| id | surface | requirement | what is wrong | sev | status |

The operator''s entry *is* stored (`SetupActivity.kt:855-861` -> `SetupStore.kt:130`) but `CaptureConfiguration` (`pipeline/.../rig/CaptureConfiguration.kt:24-30`) **carries no frequency field at all**, so the setup-to-capture bridge drops it; it survives only as UI text. | R-1036 | archive default-on disclosure | D39, FR-STO-3d, constitution I | Raised by the spec drafter rather than left implicit, and worth acting on: D39 flips the continuous archive **on by default**, and unlike the gated-over budget - which the operator watches because it holds the actual product - **the archive is write-mostly training material nobody looks at day to day.** It can consume 60 GB over roughly four months without anyone noticing until pruning starts. FR-STO-3d makes the pruning itself safe (oldest first, gated overs protected, nothing vanishes silently), but *safe pruning is not the same as the operator knowing their disk is filling for a purpose they may have forgotten they enabled.* Needs a plain disclosure where the default is flipped - onboarding and Settings > Storage - with the monthly rate stated. Lands with the Recordings work. | spec | **specified** - spec3 merged `8129d2fb`: **FR-STO-3f** requires the archive's default-on state and its monthly rate at setup and wherever storage is shown, **beside the off control** rather than a tap away, with the *measured* rate replacing D39's estimate once one exists and the estimate visibly labelled until then (constitution VI); AC-158, AC-159. Drawn on RC01 `Recordings.dc.html` (the storage card, with Turn off beside the statement) and N08 `Capture.dc.html` (Storage row). Builds with the Recordings UI package; the measured-vs-estimated flag is part of the archive builder's read API. |
| R-1037 | gated-audio pruning order | FR-STO-3, FR-STO-3d | The spec drafter declined to invent an answer and said so, which is right. FR-STO-3 says budgets exist for **(a) gated transmission audio and (b) the continuous archive**, but only (b)''s pruning order is specified anywhere - D39 gave a decision for the archive and nobody has ever been asked about (a). A careful reader will ask what happens when the **gated-audio** budget is reached, and neither FR-STO-3, FR-STO-3a nor the new FR-STO-3d answers it. Needs a product decision, then a clause. | spec | **decided by the product owner** - when the over-audio budget is reached the app **warns loudly and deletes nothing**; capture continues until the phone is genuinely out of space and then stops loudly (constitution IV), and the operator frees space by deleting sessions themselves in Recordings. Over audio is the evidence behind every transcript and correction. This **withdraws** FR-STO-3's opt-in automatic pruning for the over-audio budget (it stays for the archive, per FR-STO-3d) and makes today's `SettingsStore.autoPruneEnabled` / `StorageAccounting.computeNextDeletion` whole-session pruning wrong for over audio. Specified in spec3, merged `8129d2fb`: decision **D40**, **FR-STO-3e** - no setting turns pruning on for over audio; FR-STO-3a's opt-in pruning amended in the open to apply to the archive only. The drafter added, and the lead accepted, that the warning **persists for as long as the budget stays exceeded and survives restarts** (AC-156, AC-157), and then flagged that a persistent warning on a Settings sub-page satisfies the letter of "warns loudly" while failing the operator - so **AC-160** binds it to the Capture screen and the bar's state label, **visible without a tap**, reusing the `Low storage` state already in `LiveBarPolling`'s priority ladder. The code change is in the archive and budget package (WPARC), told to model the condition as a derivable persistent state rather than a one-time event. |
| R-1038 | continuous archive attachment | constitution IV, R5, FR-SEG-9, FR-STO-3d | Found reviewing WPARC `7db41a16` before merge; **blocks that merge.** `ContinuousArchiveAttachment` feeds the archive writer through `Channel(Channel.UNLIMITED)` - chosen so a slow writer never drops a frame, and a `Channel(1)` revert did prove small buffers lose data. But a writer that **stalls** rather than runs slow (a wedged filesystem, a hung codec, storage I/O starved by the OEM) makes `trySend` succeed forever while the queue grows on the heap at the pipeline rate: **16 kHz mono PCM16 is 115.2 MB per hour.** A few hours of stall overnight exhausts the process heap, the OS kills it, and **capture dies with it** - the most secondary component in the pipeline taking down the primary one. "Never blocks" holds; "never dies" does not. Fix in flight: a bounded channel with a derived capacity, overflow dropping **archive** frames only and recording a coalesced hole in `archive_gap`, and `resegmentable` made honest across a hole. **Side finding, recorded because the pattern keeps recurring:** WPARC established that `SettingsStore.autoPruneEnabled` **never had a wired deletion path** - `StorageAccounting.computeNextDeletion` only ever produced a preview. The whole-session over-audio auto-prune D40 "withdraws" had never deleted anything; D40 now guarantees it never will. **Fixed at `7ca223bb`** (round 2: bounded at a derived 10 s, overflow drops archive frames only with one coalesced `archive_gap` reason `archive_queue_overflow`, `finishAndAwait` times out at 5 s, a session with an archive hole is not re-segmentable), merged `81b4e87f`. Closes when the gate, CI and Release are green on the pushed merge. | halt **CLOSED on evidence** - CI run 34729418415 and Release run 34729418424 both exit 0 on `8cef8f8b`, which contains the merge 81b4e87f, after the full local gate. | closed |

WPPOL guarded the *preview* with `runCatching`, so the screen shows an honest absence - but **the Save file write path reaches the identical code and is not guarded**, so any operator whose data contains a confirmed attribution whose station has no resolvable callsign crashes the export. The guard must not become the fix: a CONFIRMED record that cannot name its callsign is a data inconsistency to be exported honestly - named, with its state and the reason its callsign is missing - never thrown on and never invented (FR-EXP-4''s structural type already forbids inventing). Also establish whether the scenario data is itself inconsistent or whether real data can reach this state. | halt **Fixed at `4aec9531`**, merged `a268e38b`, gate green: `ExportCoordinator` falls back to `stationId` as every other reader does (a CONFIRMED over with no catalog row is ordinary real state - `CallsignResolver` sets `stationId` to the heard callsign and the catalog is populated lazily); a corrected INFERRED over carries an honestly empty confidence rather than throwing; a resolved state with no `stationId` at all becomes `UnresolvedCallsign(state, reason)`, named in the ADIF header, a trailing CSV `attribution_note` column and a JSON `note`, never readable as a callsign and never folded into AMBIGUOUS/UNKNOWN. | R-1043 | `CaptureStatusContentTest` R_232 | constitution II | **Intermittent, not a merge regression.** The full gate on `b6acd316`+ (after the WPARC and WPNAV merges) failed 1 of 163 in `:app:smokeTestDebugUnitTest`: `java.lang.NullPointerException at Preconditions.checkNotNull` inside `ActivityController.windowFocusChanged`, thrown from Robolectric's own `startActivitySyncInternal`, before the test body runs. The same class re-run alone passed 9/9 in 40 s; neither merge touched the test or `CaptureStatusScreen`. A launch that can fail inside the framework is a flake in the gate, and a gate that flakes teaches people to re-run rather than read. Watch for recurrence; if it reappears, the fix is test isolation (activity scenario lifecycle), not a retry. **Second instance, next gate run:** `SettingsContentTest` AC_144 failed 1 of 2125 in `:app:testDebugUnitTest` with `ComposeTimeoutException: Condition still not satisfied after 5000 ms` at the fixed wall-clock `waitUntil(5_000)` after tapping Send field report; the class re-run alone passed. WPARC's only settings change was 41 added lines in `SettingsStore.kt`, off that path. Both failures are **wall-clock waits that fail under load**: each gate ran beside three builders compiling in their own worktrees (14 JVMs, CPU 45 %). That is a real defect in the tests, not noise - a gate whose verdict depends on what else the machine is doing is not a gate. Route a test-robustness package: replace wall-clock waits in Robolectric UI tests with idling on the composition (`waitForIdle`, `mainClock` advance) or a generous shared timeout constant, and find the other fixed `waitUntil(N)` sites. **A second, environmental cause, found by WPDATA:** its `:data` `MigrationTest` failed intermittently with `SQLITE_CANTOPEN`, root-caused to **17,731 leftover `robolectric-*` directories** in this machine's shared `%TEMP%`, the failure rate tracking the count as it grew through the session - never a data mismatch. Robolectric does not clean these up after forked workers, and every builder worktree adds more. So a gate failure on this machine can be load (wall-clock waits) or temp exhaustion (file opens); both are environment, and both must be removed before a flaky gate can be read as a code defect. Action: clear stale `robolectric-*` temp directories when no build is running, and add that step to the gate script. **Correction, same day: the temp-directory cause is NOT established for WPDATA's failure.** After clearing 17,638 stale directories (0 failures, 88 recent ones left), `MigrationTest.migration_from_v12_to_v13_preserves_existing_rows_and_adds_the_over_audio_and_label_columns` still failed with `SQLITE_CANTOPEN` **deterministically** - run alone (1 of 16) and in the full gate (1 of 120) - so it is a defect in that merge (`156d38ac`, unpushed), not the environment, and is routed back to WPDATA. **Root cause, WPDATA `a7dddcae`:** Windows `MAX_PATH`. The test's database path under Robolectric's per-test temp directory (`robolectric-<test method name><20 digits>\org.ort.data.test-dataDir\databases\migration-test-db-v13-over-audio-and-label`) was 253 characters; opening the `-journal` sidecar pushed it to 261, one past the limit, which is why it threw exactly at `setJournalMode`. A long test name plus a long database name, deterministic, not temp exhaustion. Fixed by shortening the database name; alone 3/3 and the `:data` suite 3/3 pass. Production is unaffected (`ort.db`, and Android has no such limit). The stale-directory cleanup stays in the gate script as hygiene; the load-sensitive wall-clock waits above remain this row's substance. **Further instance (WPIMPROVE, 2026-09-13):** `NavSeedTest` > `searchFiltersOpen...` in `:app:smokeTestDebugUnitTest` failed once in a full gate and passed 3/3 alone, with other builders compiling. **Partly fixed, merged `ab18b480`** (WPTESTROBUST `3e7cbf5f`): `SettingsContentTest` fixed five-second waits now use a shared `ORT_COMPOSE_ASYNC_WAIT_TIMEOUT_MILLIS` (15 s) in `OrtComposeTestRule.kt`; `NavSeedTest` search-filters waits for its tag before asserting. `CaptureStatusContentTest` R_232 did not reproduce (8 of 8 alone, 6 of 6 under load), left open. About 15 other test files still carry literal `waitUntil(5_000)`/`15_000` waits with no reported flake. Remaining: R-1053's residual `SlotTable` teardown race, and the same state-write shape in `ModelsContent.kt` and `TransmissionDetailScreen.kt`. **Further instance (WPREPLIFE, 2026-09-13):** `CaptureStatusContentTest` R_172 failed once in a full gate with a Robolectric `ActivityController` NPE - the R_232 launch NPE class in a second test - and passed alone; the next full gate was green. | process **2026-09-20 (overnight session):** this row cost two full gate runs. `TransmissionDetailContentTest > R_052` failed under load with `performScrollTo() failed ... could not find any node ... ContentDescription = 'Typed callsign'` and passed in isolation on an unchanged tree. Diagnosed rather than re-run: three flows in that file clicked *Type a callsign* and then looked for the field with one implicit idle-wait instead of the file's own bounded `waitUntilDescriptionExists` poll, so a contended machine loses the race. Fixed with the deterministic wait, not a longer timeout (merged this session). **Two mechanisms remain unproven and are deliberately unfixed:** `SettingsCaptureScreenTest`/`BluetoothPermissionScreenTest`'s `AppNotIdleException` (suspected cross-class Compose recomposer poisoning in a shared fork - neither class owns async state) and `:data`'s `SQLITE_CANTOPEN` when two on-disk databases open back to back in one Robolectric JVM on Windows (`forkEvery` is set only in `app/build.gradle.kts`, nowhere else). Neither could be forced red under synthetic CPU load across 5 runs each, so no fork-policy or timeout change was guessed at - the constitution's own rule after two blind fixes cost more than one instrumented run. Next occurrence: capture `jstack` rather than re-running. **2026-09-22, a new signature in this family, recorded rather than absorbed.** The final gate failed on `NavSeedTest > openThreadId lands on the thread detail drill-in` with a `NullPointerException` from `Preconditions.checkNotNull` inside **Robolectric's own** `ActivityController.windowFocusChanged`, during `ActivityScenario.launch` - **no product code anywhere in the stack**. That is a different signature from this row's two recorded mechanisms (`AppNotIdleException`, and `:data`'s `SQLITE_CANTOPEN`), so it is a third, not a recurrence. Diagnosed rather than re-run blindly: the class passes in isolation with `--rerun-tasks` forcing real execution, and the whole gate then passed at `--max-workers=4` where it had failed at 6 - `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 20m 55s` against a failure at 35m 56s. That is consistent with contention during activity launch, and it is **one observation, not a proven cause** - the same discipline this row already records applies, and no fork policy or timeout was changed on the strength of it. Worth noting alongside R-1157, which found the tour degrading late in a long run: both are 'the harness gets less reliable the more work is in flight', and if a common cause exists it is probably worth one instrumented run rather than two more guesses. | partly fixed |

Matched by voice.* **It was not matched by voice - the operator typed it.** Cause, read in code: `DetailViewState.kt:290` uses *Matched by voice.* as the fallback for **every** INFERRED attribution without a source over, and `Attribution.withCorrection` produces exactly that shape (INFERRED, null confidence, no source) for every human correction. So every typed correction in the app currently tells the operator a voice match made it - a provenance lie on the one screen that exists to explain why a callsign is shown. The propagated screen already knows the truth (*typed, unverified*). The explanation must come from the real correction record, never be inferred from state alone; a genuine voice-matched INFERRED without a resolved source keeps its own honest text. **Fixed on the WPLINK branch, not merged** (`9369fbbc`): the explanation now comes from the correction record - `DetailBodyViewState.Inferred` carries structured `corrected`/`correctionTyped` from `CorrectionPolling.correctionTyped()`, which reads the latest correction audit row. Lead viewed `scratchpad\wplink-round2-captures\``corrected\D02-inferred-corrected.png` on device: *Not heard in this over. | R-1052 | Model loading on launch; debug scenarios with the real app | constitution IV, I; R-873 | Found by validator VALEXP on `90a87d75`, fresh `install.ps1 -Clear`, real bundled models: seeding any scenario (`overnight`) and then opening the **real** app (`MainActivity` -> `ReaderActivity`) **kills the process with SIGABRT, every time**. `Scenarios.load` calls `ScenarioFixtures.installEveryModelFixture()`, writing 64-byte stubs over every model (VAD, ASR encoder/decoder/tokens, LLM) for the tour; the reader's eager pipeline warm-up then loads them through sherpa-onnx JNI, which throws `Ort::Exception: Protobuf parsing failed` as an uncaught C++ exception -> `std::terminate`. `RealVadProvider.provide()`'s Kotlin `try/catch` (`RealVadProvider.kt:43`) cannot catch it. `ActiveScenarioRepublishProvider` (R-873) re-runs the seeding at every process start while a scenario marker is set, so the crash re-stomps the models and **loops**. First crash on VAD; with VAD restored by hand, the next on the ASR decoder. **Debug half:** no seeded session can be driven in the real app. **The half that matters:** nothing validates a model file before handing it to native code, so any path that leaves a truncated or corrupt model at the final path on a real phone - an interrupted first-run copy, a race between the async `BundledAssetInstaller` and the eager warm-up, storage corruption - would abort the process, possibly in a loop, taking capture down with it (constitution IV: capture never dies). Not yet established whether production can reach it; routed to find out first, then validate integrity before any native load and fail into an honest unavailable state. **Round 1 (WPMODEL `477a5fc1`, unmerged) - sent back.** Reachability, answered with file:line: the bundled installer is atomic (`.part`, hash, rename), so a release install from bundled assets hands native code either no file or a verified one; the corrupt file reached native code through `ScenarioFixtures.installModelFixture` writing stubs straight over real paths, and nothing re-verified at load. Crash reproduced before the fix and absent after on device. **But the fix would regress real installs:** the new `ModelFileVerifier` requires a `.size` sidecar that only `BundledAssetInstaller` writes, while `ModelAcquisition.fetch` and `sideload` (the Download and Install-from-a-file paths, `ModelsViewData.kt:775/796`) write only `.sha256` - so every downloaded or sideloaded model would read Failed. And `ModelAcquisition.sideload` copies straight onto the final path (`ModelAcquisition.kt:79`), a real production route to a partial model file. The unavailable state was never reached on device (the installer repaired both truncations before load), and what capture does with VAD unavailable was not answered. Asked for one sidecar helper across all three install paths, atomic sideload, upgrade safety for `.sha256`-only installs, the VAD-unavailable answer, and device proof. **Round 2 fixed** (`bb3dd013`, merged `0b2c1b84`): one `ModelFileVerifier.recordVerifiedInstall` writes the sha256 and size records for all three install paths (bundled installer, `ModelAcquisition.fetch`, `sideload`); the size record is a fast path, not a requirement - without it the file is hashed once and backfilled, so models installed by older builds still load, while a fixture stub carrying the real hash text is still refused. `sideload` is now atomic (`.part`, hash, rename). Tests failing first: a downloaded and a sideloaded model pass verification; an upgrade install with only the sha256 record loads; an interrupted sideload leaves nothing at the final path. Round 1's same-size self-heal was a sequencing mistake in that session, confirmed with temporary instrumentation. Device (`ort_audit_wpmodel`): VAD genuinely unavailable, no crash, Capture screen reads *Capturing*, heartbeat live, tier *energy VAD (not Silero)*. The VAD-unavailable behaviour is filed as R-1054. **Device evidence, kept and viewed by the lead** (`scratchpad\wpmodel-r1052-evidence\`, branch head `5c8bdcb9`, the merged code): `01-logcat-overnight.txt` - install -Clear, `overnight`, launch, 30 s, relaunch, filtered for SIGABRT|Fatal signal|tombstone|Ort::Exception|AndroidRuntime: no matches. `02-capture-status.png` - with `silero_vad.onnx` same-size corrupt: *Capturing*, heartbeat live, Tier *3 of 3 - whisper-tiny-en-int8@1 Â· energy VAD (not Silero)*. `03-picker.png`, `03-models-after.png`, `03-sidecars.txt` - a real Settings > Models install from a file through the system picker staged *Silero VAD 9e2449e1*, with the 643,854-byte file beside its `.sha256`, `.size` and `.verified` records. `03-after-relaunch.png` is byte-for-byte the same size as `03-models-after.png` and is not relied on. **Load paths, read on main:** each native constructor has exactly one caller and each runs after `ModelFileVerifier.verify` - `RealSileroVad` only at `RealVadProvider.kt:45` (verify `:60`), `RealSherpaDecoder` only at `AsrEngineProvisioning.kt:87` (verify `:101`), `MediaPipeLlmEngine` only at `ProseDigestRunner.kt:64` (verify `:58`); a 64-byte stub fails the size record when one exists, and the one-time hash when it does not. So the SIGABRT in `Vad_newFromFile` at 00:21:59 logged during this run, from another agent's `ScreenshotTourActivity` sharing emulator-5566, came from that agent's pre-fix APK, not a hole in this fix. Follow-ups filed: R-1057, R-1058. **CLOSED on evidence** - CI run 34744925285 and Release run 34744925223 both `success` on `849be96d`, which contains merge `0b2c1b84`, after the full local gate; device evidence and load paths as recorded above. | halt | closed |
| R-1053 | CI, `SettingsContentExportAndDebugDumpTest` | constitution II; R-1043 | CI run 34738522611 attempt 1 on register-only `2f4b3f8c` failed 1 of 2,189 in `:app:testDebugUnitTest`: *WPDUMP Save launches a real SAF CreateDocument intent naming one unified zip*, `CalledFromWrongThreadException` on `DefaultDispatcher-worker-2`. The same code was CI-green on `c4246961` and `eefac837`. **The stack has no app frame**: `TestMonotonicFrameClock.performFrame` -> `Recomposer.applyChanges` -> `LayoutNode.removeAt` -> `requestLayout` on the worker. The Compose test clock ran a pending frame on the thread that resumed the save handler's `withContext(Dispatchers.IO)` (`SettingsContent.kt` export and local-save launchers). Production recomposes on the main thread, so this is **not an app crash** but a harness timing defect of R-1043's class. Evidence it is timing: the class alone locally passed 3/3 (1m 19s, 1m 7s, 55s), and attempt 2 of the same CI run passed. Still a defect: a hosted run went red on it and would again. Fold into R-1043's test-robustness package: inject the IO dispatcher into these save handlers for tests, or idle the compose rule across the IO hop; never a retry. **Recurring, now blocking (2026-09-13):** the same failure, same stack with no app frame, failed a builder's branch 2 of 3 times alone (WPRC02), the lead's full gate on the WPRC02 merge `c12263f0` (1 of 2,425; the rerun was green in 13m 25s), and **hosted Release run 34762090241 on `71636cb0`** (`DefaultDispatcher-worker-1`), so latest-build was not republished; the failed job was re-run. Routed to WPTESTROBUST as its first commit: measure the failure rate under load, make the save launchers' IO dispatcher injectable, fix the same pattern elsewhere, two consecutive green gates. **Fixed, merged `ab18b480`** (WPTESTROBUST `5bb93147`). Root cause proved with thread-name instrumentation (since removed): the hop is not the launcher's own `withContext(Dispatchers.IO)` but two calls deeper, inside `ExportCoordinator.build`, `LocalSaveBundleBuilder.write`/`preview` and `FieldReportBundleBuilder.preview`; the following state write resumed on an IO worker, and the Compose test clock ran a pending frame there. An outer dispatcher seam alone still failed 9 of 20. Fix: every state write in `SettingsContent.kt` runs inside an entered `withContext(Dispatchers.Main.immediate)` - a no-op on a device, where resumption is already on the main thread. Rates on this machine: **7 of 23 failing before, 1 of 25 after**; the one remaining failure has a different signature (`SlotTable` `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` racing `ActivityScenario` teardown) and is residual. Under `:pipeline` test load 15 of 15 passed. The same bare state-write-after-IO shape exists at six sites in `ModelsContent.kt` and `TransmissionDetailScreen.kt`, not fixed here (ownership). Closes when pushed and CI and Release are green; the residual is watched under R-1043. **CLOSED on evidence** - CI run 34769228303 `success` on attempt 2 (attempt 1 failed before any test on a GitHub HTTP 500 fetching the sherpa-onnx native libraries, R-1075) and Release run 34769228314 `success` on `745e01b7`, which contains merge `ab18b480`, after the full local gate (21m 43s). The residual SlotTable teardown race stays under R-1043. | process | closed |
| R-1054 | Capture with VAD unavailable | constitution I, VI; FR-OBS-1; D41 | From R-1052 round 2 (WPMODEL), with file:line: when the Silero VAD model is unavailable, `RealCaptureService.buildSegmenter` (`RealCaptureService.kt:674-684`) records `VadAvailability.stub(reason)` and substitutes `EnergyVadModel` (`:1560-1571`, an RMS-energy threshold). Capture never stops and never drops an over - constitution IV holds. But (a) the only operator-facing trace is one diagnostic tier-facts line, *energy VAD (not Silero)* (`CaptureStatusViewState.kt:508-510`), not a banner like ASR's, so an operator can capture a whole night segmented by a far cruder detector without being told; and (b) segmentation is the one decision reprocessing cannot undo, so whether each segment records which VAD cut it is a provenance question (constitution VI) - D41's `vad_stats` line names no model. No FR-RUN or FR-CAP requirement names VAD degraded-mode disclosure. Needs: check whether the segment, transmission or session records the VAD in use; if not, record it and carry it into `vad_stats` and the debug dump; and decide the operator-facing disclosure (spec first, then UI). **Spec, lead:** the fallback contradicts the spec, not only the UI - FR-SEG-1 names Silero or TEN-VAD only, and FR-SEG-7 with CON-SEG-1 says segmentation must not vary because boundaries cannot be reprocessed; against that, constitution IV says capture never dies. Recorded as **FR-SEG-10** (every session and transmission records which detector cut it, marked when it is not an FR-SEG-1 detector, carried in `vad_stats` and the dump; AC-162) and open question **Q22** (continue or stop, and how loudly; recommended: continue, disclosed without a tap like D40, and re-segment from the continuous archive when the specified VAD is back). Next: route FR-SEG-10 to a builder; Q22 is the operator's. **FR-SEG-10 built** (WPSEGPROV `8cebe3cd`, merged `a7ac6259`): `VadDetectorKind` (UNKNOWN, SILERO, TEN_VAD, ENERGY) on session and transmission, schema v14 with honest UNKNOWN for existing rows, `conformsToFrSeg1` answered once at the data layer, carried in `vad_stats` and the dump; resolved once per session before its row is written, so it cannot change mid-session; AC-162 driven both ways through the real capture service. **Still open:** the operator-facing disclosure and whether capture should continue on the fallback at all - Q22. | spec | FR-SEG-10 merged, Q22 open |
| R-1055 | Log opened from Improve's Review changes | constitution I; R-1041, R-1047 | Lead viewed `scratchpad\valr04b-evidence\``02-log-filtered.png` (VALR04B, pushed `ba3a66da`): the Log reads **Filtered to 12 overs** with Clear, and directly beneath it **No overs yet. Listening since 07:19. The first one appears here the moment squelch opens.** The screen states twelve overs and shows none; same at 2.0 (`06-log-filtered@2x.png`). Cause, read in code by the validator: the Log is scoped to the live capture session, `MainActivity` starts one on launch, and Review changes applies the transmission-id filter without moving the Log to the session - or sessions - those overs belong to, so it filters a session that holds none of them. On a real phone this is the ordinary case: capture runs overnight while the operator improves earlier nights. The same mismatch can hit any `transmissionIds` or station filter opened from outside the live session (D11 affected overs, DG02, Recordings). Fix: a transmission-id filter shows those overs wherever they are, regardless of the live-session scope; the statement's count is the count of rows actually shown, and if some are out of scope it says so rather than claiming them. Also back should restore the Done summary it came from, not Improve's starting board. Routed to WPINIT, which already owns Log content and polling. **Fixed, merged `43ec9a18`** (WPINIT `706d1a74`, evidence scenario `cf5a327d`): a curated `transmissionIds` or station filter resolves across sessions (`TransmissionDao.listByIds`, `listByStationId`), and the statement counts the rows found. Lead viewed `scratchpad\wpinit-evidence\``r1055-log-cross-session-1x.png` and `-2x.png`: a live session running (*5:00*, green dot), Log reads *Filtered to 3 overs* with Clear and shows exactly three rows from the earlier session (02:12-02:14: K7LWH, KE7QRS or KE7QRF, unknown station) at both scales. Back to Improve's Done summary needs `OrtNavHost` - left for the navigation package. R04's own recheck follows the push. Closes on hosted green. **CLOSED on evidence** - CI run 34752376449 and Release run 34752376340 both `success` on `5ee189d6`, which contains merge `43ec9a18`, after the full local gate; lead viewed the cross-session captures above and VALR04C's `02-log-filtered.png` (R-1041). | R-1058 | Model verification failures are not logged | constitution I, VI; FR-OBS-1; R-1052 | Found by WPMODEL while producing R-1052's evidence (`02-logcat.txt`): when `ModelFileVerifier` refuses a model, the typed `Failed(reason)` - which file, and whether size or hash failed - reaches `VadAvailability` / the ASR availability state in memory only. Nothing writes it to `capture.log`, the diagnostics bundle or logcat, and the one operator-facing trace for the VAD, *energy VAD (not Silero)*, says what fell back but not why. A field report from a phone where a model was refused would therefore show a degraded tier and no cause. Fix: log each verification failure once per launch as a closed-vocabulary diagnostics event (asset id, failure kind: missing file, missing record, size mismatch, hash mismatch; no free text, no path beyond the asset id), carried into the debug dump, for VAD, ASR and the LLM alike. **Fixed** (WPMODLOG `e7be30ac`): closed `ModelAssetId` (VAD, ASR_ENCODER, ASR_DECODER, ASR_TOKENS, LLM) and `ModelVerificationFailureKind` (MISSING_FILE, MISSING_RECORD, SIZE_MISMATCH, HASH_MISMATCH, PLACEHOLDER); `DiagnosticsLog.logModelVerificationFailed` writes one `model_verification_failed assetId=... kind=...` line per asset per launch to `pipeline.log`, read into the debug dump. Tests through `RealVadProvider` and `AsrEngineProvisioning` fail with the log call reverted. **Not seen on a device** - no real capture session configured the log during WPMODLOG's device run; proven at unit level only. Closes when pushed and CI and Release are green; a field dump with a refused model would be the device proof.  **CLOSED on evidence** - CI run 34754729089 and Release run 34754729078 both `success` on `86e1c336`, which contains merge `962b4a5d`, after the full local gate; lead viewed the device evidence cited above. | spec | closed |

| R-1060 | Local builds without bundled assets, the real app | R-1052; AGENTS escape hatch | Reported by WPREC while capturing RC01: on a build made with `-PortAllowMissingBundledAssets=true`, opening the real app (`MainActivity`) fails in `RealCaptureService`'s VAD load on the **placeholder** ONNX model, even with R-1052's verifier merged. Reading the fix explains it: the verifier proves a file is what the manifest says, and in a no-token build the manifest entry *is* the placeholder, so the check passes and native code is handed a file it cannot parse. Release builds and CI refuse missing assets, so the operator's phone never has a placeholder - production is unaffected. But every builder's and validator's manual check of the real app on a local build hits it, which is why tonight's validators needed workarounds. Fix: mark placeholder assets as placeholders (in the manifest or an installed record) and have the providers treat them as unavailable without calling native code - the same typed state R-1052 uses - so a no-token build runs the real app with ASR, VAD and the LLM honestly unavailable. **Fixed** (WPMODLOG `858f1fb2`): `BundledAssetInstaller` writes a `.placeholder` record for every manifest entry marked missing and clears stale verification records; `ModelFileVerifier.verify` checks the placeholder record first, so it wins over any bytes at that path; a real install clears it. Only the escape hatch can mark an entry missing - with it off, one unfetchable asset fails the whole fetch (test). Lead viewed `scratchpad\wpmodlog-evidence\``14-notoken-models.png` (no-token build: Settings > Models reads the digest model *marker present, bytes missing - 0 KB of 555 MB*) and `16-ondevice-models-listing.txt` (`gemma3-1b-it-int4.task.placeholder` beside a stale `.sha256`, still refused); `11-notoken-launch.png` with `12-logcat-launch-raw.txt` shows the real app launching with no native abort. Closes when pushed and CI and Release are green.  **CLOSED on evidence** - CI run 34754729089 and Release run 34754729078 both `success` on `86e1c336`, which contains merge `962b4a5d`, after the full local gate; lead viewed the device evidence cited above. | process | closed |

| R-1062 | FR-SEG-5 squelch fusion was never built | FR-SEG-5 (M), CON-SEG-1; failure mode F4 | Found by WPSEGPROV while wiring FR-SEG-10's fusion flag: nothing in `:pipeline` or `:segment` gates a segment boundary on rig squelch. The only reader of squelch state is frequency attribution (`RigSupervisor.bandAtTransmissionStart`). FR-SEG-5 is a Must - *rig squelch is authoritative for boundaries, VAD is authoritative for whether there is speech inside them* - and the spec calls it the single highest-value use of the rig connection after frequency, because it turns segmentation from an inference into a measurement and addresses squelch-tail hallucination, the #1 failure mode. The TH-D75A reports squelch via `BY` (FR-RIG-3, `docs/reference/th-d75a-cat.md`). The build plan reads as landed and the coverage matrix already listed FR-SEG-5 uncovered, but no row tracked it - the pattern this session keeps finding: a specified mechanism nobody built. The flag FR-SEG-10 records, `rigSquelchFusionApplied`, is honestly false everywhere today. Needs: build fusion behind the Rig Module squelch signal with a behavioural fake, keeping FR-SEG-7 (no tier dependence) and the capture-never-blocks rule; set the FR-SEG-10 flag from the real decision. Closing evidence needs the operator's radio (hardware protocol, `results/e2e-audit/`). **Round 1 (WPSQUELCH `71ec04ec`, unmerged) - sent back, halt-class hole.** Built: squelch decides boundaries and VAD decides speech once the first transition arrives; a no-speech squelch interval is a retained `REJECTED_NO_SPEECH`; squelch receipt time maps to sample position through `SampleClock`; eligibility only for push rigs or polls within FR-RUN-17's 250 ms; union of bands for the one mixed stream; FR-SEG-5 now covered by 13 tests. **Lead read the code:** `Segmenter.squelchOpen` is set only by transitions and never returns to unknown, `SquelchGate` has no loss signal, and `RigSupervisor` clears only its own dedupe state on disconnect. So a rig lost while squelch is closed - the idle state - leaves segmentation blocked for the rest of the session: every later over is captured as audio and never becomes a transmission (constitution IV). Lost while open, the rest of the night becomes back-to-back maximum-length segments. Its rig-drop test covered only the in-flight over. It also skipped the full local gate. Round 2 asked for a loss and staleness signal that returns segmentation to VAD-only, a `RIG_LOST` close reason, resumption on reconnect, and the full gate. Two design questions recorded as **Q23**. **Round 2 (WPSQUELCH `a3d4eb25`, unmerged) - sent back.** The loss path is right: `SquelchGate.markUnknown` queues a loss in arrival order, `Segmenter.handleSquelchLoss` returns to VAD-only and closes an open segment as `RIG_LOST`, and fusion resumes on reconnect; losses are emitted on transport lost, disconnect or rig change, and a staleness watchdog; its own full gate was green. **But the staleness bound would split real overs on the TH-D75A.** `squelchStalenessBoundMillis` gives every push descriptor a flat 2000 ms, restarted by any fresh reading. `kenwood-thd75a.json` is push (AI 1) *and* re-polls every 2000 ms, and `DescriptorRigModule` sends the cycle's four commands and then waits the interval, so replies arrive in bursts about 2000 ms plus round-trip apart - the gap between heartbeats equals the bound, and on Bluetooth routinely exceeds it. Each trip mid-over closes the transmission as RIG_LOST and the next `BY` reply opens a new one: one over cut into two-second pieces, which reprocessing cannot undo (CON-SEG-1). A push-only descriptor with no poll would be cut after 2 s of every over. Round 3 asked for failing tests holding a 30 s over on the real descriptor with realistic reply latency and jitter, a bound clearly above a full poll cycle, no silence watchdog for push-only rigs, and a hardware step holding a 20 s over on each band. **Round 3 fixed** (WPSQUELCH `025ec8b0`, merged `e3402ef5`): the squelch staleness watchdog runs only when the descriptor polls, at twice the poll interval (4000 ms for the TH-D75A); a push-only descriptor with no poll has no silence watchdog and loses squelch authority only on transport loss, disconnect or rig change; the generic link-silence STALE path still marks the rig stale for every descriptor but emits a squelch loss only for polled ones. `FakeRigTransport` gained reply latency and jitter. Tests drive the real `kenwood-thd75a.json` through `DescriptorRigModule` and `RigSupervisor` for a 30 s over at 40 ms latency and with up to 300 ms jitter: one uninterrupted open, zero losses - on round 2's code the flat-latency case failed with 29 events instead of 1; a push-only descriptor silent for 30 s gets no loss; a genuinely dead polled link still reverts to VAD-only. Its own full gate green (20m 42s). Lead read the diff. **Residual, recorded:** a push-only rig whose link hangs without the transport reporting loss keeps squelch fusion until reconnect - not the TH-D75A, which also polls. **Open until hardware:** on the operator's radio, a 20 s over on each band is one transmission, and squelch-bounded overs start and end at the squelch edges. Closes on hosted green plus that hardware check. **Hosted green:** CI run 34756994437 and Release run 34756994415 both `success` on `cae628b2`, which contains merge `e3402ef5`, after the full local gate (17m 24s). Only the hardware check on the operator's radio remains. | spec | hosted green, hardware check outstanding |

| R-1067 | Reprocess run lifecycle | FR-REP, constitution I | Disclosed by WPIMPROVE while fixing R-1063: the reprocess run started from Improve is cancelled when its Activity is destroyed (cooperative cancellation of `ReprocessRunner`), so rotating the phone or changing the font scale mid-run stops reprocessing; the restored Running board then shows a freshly restarted run. What the screen shows is honest, but an operator-started reprocess of a night's overs should not depend on the phone staying in one orientation, and on ColorOS the Activity can be destroyed in the background as well. Check what FR-REP requires of a run's lifetime; move the run to a lifecycle that outlives the screen (the processing service or WorkManager, as the pipeline's other long work does), with Running observing it rather than owning it. **Round 1 (WPREPLIFE `914ea5f4`, unmerged) - sent back.** Built: the operator-started run is unique WorkManager work (`ReprocessWorker`, KEEP, no constraints - FR-REP-9, FR-REP-11), with a durable checkpoint so a restarted attempt finishes only the remainder, pause carried in, and cancel only by the operator; a real `ReaderActivity` recreate keeps the same work id running. Lead viewed `scratchpad\wpreplife-evidence\02-running-1x.png` (*Improving - All groups - 0 of 12*, Pause, Cancel). Not met: leaving Improve and returning shows Root rather than the running job, although Root is in the package; nothing handles WorkManager's 10-minute execution limit (foreground work, or an honest waiting-to-resume state); no device capture of a run in progress, because the fixture overs finish in under a second. Round 2 asked for all three. **Rounds 2-4, fixed, merged `105ea41f`** (WPREPLIFE `773d5c3b`, `4fe78086`, `ead5bcb5`). Improve reattaches to the unique work on every composition (Running, Paused, or Waiting when the system has stopped it), and a run finished while the screen was gone is reported from WorkInfo output. Stop-and-reschedule was chosen over a foreground service: the checkpoint makes a stop and resume as correct as an uninterrupted run. A reattached board carries its group headline, and progress is published with the real total before any work (no *0 of 0*). A processed over's tier and candidate flag are now written in one `TransmissionDao.markProcessedAtTier` statement, and a resumed worker re-checks checkpointed ids against the database. **Honest note on the round-2 contradiction** (*12 of 12 processed* above *3 overs can get better*): the builder attributed it to a torn two-statement write, but its own clean before-dump shows unprocessed seed rows already reading candidate flag 0 with no tier, and it found the debug `ActiveScenarioRepublishProvider` re-seeding the database on every restart (R-1076); the contradiction is better explained by that re-seed, and the atomic write is kept as defence in depth. Lead viewed `scratchpad\wpreplife-evidence\round2\a2-running-2x.png` (*Paused - All groups - 3 of 12* at 2.0 after a font change), `b-after-kill.png` (Running resumed at *11 of 12* after force-stop), `scratchpad\wpreplife-evidence\round4\reattach-real-group.png` (*Paused - All groups - 6 of 12* after the composition was disposed), `kill-mid-run-root-clean.png` (*A run finished while you were away - 12 of 12 overs processed* above *Nothing can get better right now*) and `db-before-after-clean.txt` (six overs at T3 before the kill, all twelve at T3 after). Its own full gate green (18m 29s). | R-1075 | Build: native library and bundled asset downloads | constitution II; CI | CI run 34769228303 on `745e01b7` failed 17 s in, before any test: `:app:fetchSherpaNativeLibraries` got **HTTP 500** from GitHub for `sherpa-onnx-v1.13.7-android.tar.bz2`; Release on the same commit fetched it fine, and the failed job was re-run. `FetchSherpaNativeTask.kt:197/203` and `FetchBundledAssetsTask.kt:217/224` throw on the first non-200 or I/O error - no retry, no backoff - so one transient server error turns a green commit red. Needs bounded retries with backoff on 5xx and connection errors only (never on 404 or a checksum mismatch, which must still fail loudly), each attempt logged, tested in `buildSrc` with a fake server. **Fixed, merged `5b952034`** (WPFETCHRETRY `2f2fe262`): shared `RetryingDownload` in `buildSrc` - 4 attempts, backoff 2 s / 5 s / 15 s or a `Retry-After` of at most 120 s; retries HTTP 5xx, 429 and `IOException` only; any other 4xx fails on attempt 1; checksum and size checks stay in each fetcher after the download and never retry; a `.part` file per attempt; request headers are never logged. Tests against a local `HttpServer`: 500 then 200 and a connection reset then 200 both failed on the old single-attempt code; 503 four times names four attempts; 404/401/403 do not retry; a checksum mismatch does not retry and leaves no file; the token appears in no log line. Both tasks reported UP-TO-DATE after the build. Lead read the helper. **Noted, not in scope:** the final step copies the part file over the destination rather than an atomic move, and `instanceFollowRedirects` may resend request headers to a redirect host (unchanged from before). Its own full gate green (24m 5s). | R-1089 | Four design tokens fail WCAG AA contrast | constitution VII (the accessibility floor); FR-A11Y; design-guide | Measured by the P32 pass, computed rather than eyeballed (OKLCH to linear sRGB to WCAG relative luminance) against `design/design-guide.md`'s own tokens: `marker/unknown` on `bg/screen` **2.63:1** (needs 3:1 - this is the UNKNOWN attribution dot itself, the very mark that tells the operator the app does not know); `line/control` on `bg/screen` **2.12:1** (needs 3:1 - the unselected checkbox/radio border); `text/disabled` on `bg/screen` **2.74:1** (needs 4.5:1, and this token is used for column headers, not only for disabled text); `text/figure` on `bg/score` **3.39:1** (needs 4.5:1 - score-chip values and list counts). Two more are size-dependent: `text/low` (3.55:1) and `text/signal` (3.26:1) pass as large text or a glyph and fail at 13sp, which `AttributionRow` uses. Deliberately not overridden on one screen: these are token-level failures and belong to the design guide, or every screen drifts separately. | spec **FIXED, awaiting the batch capture** (`476f6b6d`, merged 2026-09-20): all six tokens raised to clear their floor, derived rather than eyeballed - OKLCH to linear sRGB by Ottosson's published matrices, checked against the existing hex values before the method was trusted, then WCAG relative luminance. `marker/unknown` 2.63 -> 3.17, `line/control` 2.12 -> 3.12, `text/disabled` 2.75 -> 4.63, `text/figure` 3.39 -> 4.71, `text/low` 3.55 -> 5.03, `text/signal` 3.27 -> 4.83. Every change is lightness only: hue (250) and chroma (0.008) are untouched, so `marker/unknown` stays a neutral grey far from `accent/green` (hue 150) and `accent/amber` (hue 75), and UNKNOWN keeps its independent size cue (5/9 scale) as well - the four attribution states remain mutually distinguishable, which was the way this fix could have gone wrong. `text/low`/`text/signal` were raised rather than resized: reaching WCAG's large-text threshold from 13sp needs about a 45% size increase, reflowing every row that uses `AttributionRow`, and doubly so at font scale 2.0 - a colour change carries no layout risk and fixes text and graphic uses at once. `OrtColorsContrastTest` now asserts the floor for every pair the guide defines, not only the six; discriminated by reverting `OrtColors.kt` to HEAD, which turned it red naming exactly the same seven pairs at the same ratios. **Open until the batch tour captures it**: this changes almost every screen in the app, which is why the row was held until one full canonical run could settle it. **CLOSED on capture evidence** (lead, 2026-09-21). Judged on real screens rather than on the arithmetic alone: `stations-14-nights/ST01-stations.png` shows the `STATION / TONIGHT / LAST` column headers - the `text/disabled` token, and the use this row specifically warned was not merely disabled text - now plainly legible, with the *last heard* figures beside them in the raised `text/figure`. `llm-disabled/DG01-digest.png` settles the question this fix could have failed on: the amber half-filled AMBIGUOUS marker on *1 over could not be attributed with confidence* reads clearly apart from the green CONFIRMED dots above it, so raising `marker/unknown`'s lightness did not collapse the states into each other. | closed |
| R-1090 | The accessibility sweep is half done | constitution VII, VIII; FR-A11Y | P32 fixed two shared-component defects that reach most of the app (`ScreenHeader`'s drawer and search icons plus `DrillInHeader`'s kebab were clickable at 19-21 dp, the same shape R-1073 fixed on the back chevron three lines away; `CheckboxRow`/`ToggleRow` carried **no content description at all**, confirmed live by `uiautomator` showing `content-desc=""` on all three analytics tier toggles), then stopped and said so. **Inspected and clean:** Now, Log, drawer, transmission detail, Settings root, Settings-Analytics, Settings-Alerts; the four-state attribution marker already distinguishes by shape, not colour. **Not reached:** correction sheet, Search, Threads, Stations, Digest, Recordings, Capture, every setup step, and Settings' Licences/Backup/Export/Contribute/Diagnostics/About/Storage/Rig/Tier/Capture sub-screens. Also open: whether `uiautomator`'s bounds for a small icon inside a clickable box are ground truth at all - the dump shows an empty-label clickable parent and a labelled non-clickable child on both the new fix and the already-verified R-1073 chevron, so a live TalkBack pass is what would settle it. | spec **2026-09-20, second pass (`b9d916e8`):** the screens the first pass never reached were inventoried on a device and fixed - the correction sheet, Search's recent row, Threads (card and meanwhile row), Thread detail, Stations (`StationRow` dumped an empty `content-desc` **and** measured 33.5 dp, under the floor), Station identity, Digest, Sessions, Recordings (the budgets card had no semantics at all), Capture's own back chevron (the R-1073 shape again, on a screen that draws its own header), Settings Export and Backup, and `Feedback.kt`'s `BannerAction`, which every banner in the app uses. Settings root, Storage and Licences confirmed clean. **Still open:** `ImproveScreens.kt` and `FrequencyScreen.kt` carry the identical defect and were outside the sweep's list; Search's `WidenRow`/`StruckThroughQueryField`/`UnappliedTextCountLine` use a different shape that was not device-verified; and no live TalkBack pass has been run, which is what would settle whether `uiautomator`'s split-node dump is ground truth for a small icon inside a clickable box. **2026-09-21, narrowed to the one thing left** (lead). `ImproveScreens.kt` and `FrequencyScreen.kt` were fixed with the established `clearAndSetSemantics` + multi-entry `Text` shape (`edc22979`). Search's `WidenRow`, `StruckThroughQueryField` and `UnappliedTextCountLine` were **inspected and deliberately not changed**: each sets `contentDescription` through a plain non-merging `semantics {}` on the node that carries the fact, which is structurally unlike the confirmed defect - that one is specifically a merge-from-descendants step failing on a device. So what is left is **a live TalkBack pass on a real device**, and only that. It is the one question none of this can settle from a JVM: whether `uiautomator`'s split-node dump is ground truth for a small icon inside a clickable box, or an artefact of how the dump flattens the tree. Robolectric demonstrably passes the broken shape too, so no test added here can stand in for it. **Operator/device work, not a builder's.** | open |

One of the two is wrong and nobody has decided which - a permanently denied microphone arguably *is* a halt, since capture cannot start, in which case the code is wrong and not the drawing. Decide, then make them agree and re-capture the board. | spec **STALE - code and artboard already agree** (verified at HEAD by the R-1127 builder, 2026-09-20): `SetupStep.isHalted()` already returns `true` for `MICROPHONE_DENIED`, with `SetupStepTest`'s own `R_1091` cases passing, and `design/canvas/Setup-Mic-Denied.dc.html` already draws segment 2 halted. No code change was needed or made. Rolled into the batch tour, which now has a `setup-mic-denied` step for the first time, so this closes on that capture rather than on a code claim. **CLOSED on capture evidence** (lead, 2026-09-21 full tour, run `results/ui-audit/setup-mic-denied/S02b-mic-denied.png`): the first capture this screen has ever had. Segment 2 draws in the halted red, the step counter reads *2 of 10*, the banner states *Android will not ask again* with the three system-settings steps beneath it, and the pinned *Open app settings* / *Check again* pair sits at the foot. Fix the screen, fix the artboard, re-capture `setup-models/S11a-models` at 1.0 and 2.0. | polish **CLOSED on evidence** (lead capture 2026-09-20): fixed by `e1f5873c`. Lead viewed `scratchpad\wavel-evidence\setup-models\S11a-models.png`: the decoder row's *checksum did not match the published digest - try again* now renders in `accent/amber-text`, the guide's own failure colour, beside a green *Retry*; the artboard's deviation comment is deleted. The same capture re-confirms R-1087's numbering (**8 of 10**) and the READY gate (Continue disabled). | closed |
| R-1093 | CF13 Settings-Analytics has an artboard and a capture, but no judgement | NFR-6d-adjacent; D42, FR-ANL-11, AC-180 | `design/canvas/Settings-Analytics.dc.html` was drawn 2026-09-20 and `overnight/CF13-settings-analytics` captures at 1.0 and 2.0 exist under `scratchpad\waveh-evidence\`, but no row records the comparison, so by constitution VIII the screen is not verified. The lead judged S11b and closed R-1085 on it; CF13 never got the same treatment. Compare and close, or file what differs. | spec **CLOSED on capture evidence** (lead judgement 2026-09-21, `results/ui-audit/overnight/CF13-settings-analytics.png` plus `@2x` and `@2x-end`). The screen states the operator's own decisions back to them accurately and in the right order: **ON BY DEFAULT** carries tier 1 (*usage and quality*) with its toggle on, **OPT-IN** carries tier 2 (*transcripts and callsigns*) and tier 3 (*audio*) with both toggles off. Tier 1's description names what it sends - crashes, screens and actions used, per-pass speed, capture uptime, the setup funnel, aggregate quality rates - and then names what it never sends, in the same sentence: *never a transcript, callsign, name, station knowledge or location*. Three things this screen gets right that it would have been easy to leave out. It **discloses that nothing is sent at all**: *no destination is configured in this build - events queue on this phone and nothing is ever sent*, which is the honest state today and exactly the disclosure whose absence this row was worried about. It carries a **NEVER IN ANY TIER** section stating that a user-supplied name, station knowledge or location finer than a grid square leaves through this channel in no combination of tiers - the constitutional rule, on the screen, not only in the spec. And it shows the install id with a **Reset install id** action that *starts a new, unlinked id and asks the destination to erase every row tied to the old one*, which is the erasure path GDPR needs and the only place it is visible to an operator. Note the tier 1 description reads *screens and actions used*, which became true only today when action-level events landed (R-1100); before that it would have overstated. It is correct as captured. | closed |
| R-1094 | Backup carries four tables, not the record | FR-STO-9; AC-170; constitution III | P30's backup writes sessions, current transcripts, transmissions and corrections plus audio. It does **not** carry the station catalog, voiceprints, threads, or superseded-transcript history. The screen says so honestly, which is why this is a scoped gap and not a lie - but a restore onto a new phone still loses the station knowledge the operator built up, and superseded transcripts are exactly what constitution III says stays reachable. Decide the real scope for 1.0 and carry it. | spec **FIXED, awaiting the capture** (`f8c48d5b`, merged 2026-09-21): the bundle now carries the station catalog, voiceprints, threads, and **every transcript version rather than only the current one** - which was the constitutional half of this row, since Principle III's *never delete quietly* is exactly what a restore returning only current transcripts had quietly undone. **The station codec gets the subtle part right**: it omits `overCountsByAttributionState` entirely and writes the honest `EMPTY` placeholder on restore, because that column is D56/R-1134's *derived* value - carrying it would have recreated the second source of truth R-1134 had just removed. The next read re-derives it from the transmissions the same restore carries. Voiceprints are carried only because FR-SPK-20 permits it for the operator's own device-to-device transfer, and both the code and the screen now say so, so the bundle cannot be mistaken for a contribution or a field report. What is still not carried is named on the screen rather than silently dropped: digest summaries, lattice detail, calibration, assets, location, the contribution queue, and the identity/binding histories. | fixed |
| R-1095 | Restore's conflict rule is all-or-nothing | FR-STO-9; AC-170 | `BackupRestoreCoordinator.apply` treats any record whose id already exists on the device as a conflict, never inserts or overwrites it, and shows the operator counts per category before they confirm. Device-always-wins is a defensible default and it never destroys anything - but there is no per-record choice, so an operator restoring a mostly-overlapping bundle cannot take the one corrected callsign they wanted back. A picker is real work; file it rather than pretend the coarse rule is finished. | spec **A defect was found inside what was filed as a limitation** (`f8c48d5b`, merged 2026-09-21). The verdict asked for is *safe but limited* - device-always-wins never overwrites or deletes an existing record - **except in one case it did not cover**: a *new* correction, with a fresh id, for a transmission that itself conflicted still slipped past the filter and was inserted as an orphaned audit row, without ever updating that transmission's own `attributionState`/`stationId`. The restored record then **looks corrected without being corrected**, which is a Principle I breach at the data layer, not a missing convenience. Fixed by folding a transmission-conflicting correction into `correctionConflicts`, mirroring the scoping `transcriptsToAdd` already had, and discriminated by reverting that filter. The remaining limitation - no per-record picker - is genuinely safe-but-limited and stays open as feature work, per bugs-before-features. | fixed |
| R-1096 | The share sheet always shares the most recent thing | FR-EXP-7; AC-171 | `ShareCoordinator` resolves the most recent digest, thread transcript or over clip, because P30 wired sharing from Settings rather than from an open screen. The operator reading a particular over and tapping share does not get that over. The closed field list and the attribution it carries are correct; only the selection is wrong. Wire share from the surface the operator is actually looking at. | spec **FIXED, awaiting the batch capture** (`948cfde9`, merged 2026-09-20): `ShareCoordinator`'s three builders take the real subject id and look it up (`SessionDao.getById`, a `threadId` filter, `TransmissionDao.getById`) instead of scanning for the newest row, and share moved off Settings - which had no open screen to take a subject from - onto Digest, Thread Detail and Transmission Detail, behind the `DrillInHeader` kebab those screens already use. The `ACTION_SEND`/FileProvider intent now has exactly one builder (`ShareIntentLauncher`) rather than three hand-rolled copies, and Settings' three *share the most recent* rows are deleted rather than left as a second way in. **Privacy checked rather than assumed**: the frame-capture derivation flags `TRANSMISSION_DETAIL` unsafe because the correction sheet can render an operator-typed name - but the share payload never reads `StationEntity` at all, confirmed against its own closed-field-list tests, so wiring share there reopens nothing. See R-1143 for the artboard consequence. **CLOSED on capture evidence** (lead, 2026-09-21): `llm-disabled/DG01-digest.png` shows the share kebab in the Digest header, the affordance that lets the operator share *this* digest rather than the most recent thing the app happened to write. | closed |
| R-1097 | Reprocessing never fires an alert, and a delivery failure is discarded | FR-ALR-1..6; AC-191..197 | Two ends of the same subsystem. `ReprocessRunner` still passes `NoOpAlertEvaluationTrigger` (it has no `Context`), so a watched callsign that only resolves on a reprocess pass never alerts - defensible, but undecided and unwritten. And `AlertEvaluationCoordinator.dispatchCoalesced` discards the `Boolean` the dispatcher now returns, so a firing that the platform refused (notifications denied) is indistinguishable from one that reached the operator. Decide the first; act on the second. | spec **CLOSED** (`82eaebed`, merged 2026-09-20). Both halves settled, one by acting and one by deciding. `AlertEvaluationCoordinator.dispatchCoalesced` now captures the dispatcher's `Boolean` and logs `logAlertDeliveryFailed(watchId)` when the platform refused, so a firing nobody received is no longer indistinguishable from one that landed - `watchId` is an opaque Ulid, never a callsign. On reprocess alerts the builder checked the row's own reasoning and found it wrong: `ReprocessWorker` is a `CoroutineWorker` and does have a `Context`. It still declined to wire them, for a better reason now recorded in `ReprocessRunner`'s KDoc - the notification says *Heard now*, which would be a false claim about timing when fired from a pass closing a session that ended hours ago. | closed |

Also open: no tour scenario seeds a corrected transmission on Live Monitor, Recording Session or Threads, so the fix has tests but no capture on those three surfaces. | spec **CLOSED as stale** (lead reconciliation 2026-09-20). The R-1096 builder traced this row against real history rather than taking it at face value: commit `e69c5979`, **68 commits before** tonight's branch point, already unified `CorrectionPolling.currentAttribution` with `attributionFrom` behind the shared `correctedAttributionOrNull`, and already seeded a corrected transmission on `overnightLiveMonitor` and on `overnight`'s QSO thread - Recording Session needed no seed, since it reads every transmission unfiltered. No code change was needed or made. The live remnant it found while checking is filed separately as R-1142. | closed |
| R-1100 | Analytics records screens, not actions | FR-ANL-2; D42 | Only `FeatureUsageAnalytics.screenViewed` is instrumented; no action-level event exists, because there is no central action dispatch to hook. Tier 1 therefore answers 'which screens are opened' but not 'which features are used', which is most of what the tier is for. Related dead code from the same sweep: `DiagnosticsLog.logHeartbeatGap` has no production caller - `CaptureHeartbeatAnalytics` computes gaps itself. | spec **CLOSED** (`f10bddeb`, merged 2026-09-21): a closed 23-value `UsageAction` enum plus `FeatureUsageAnalytics.actionInvoked`, wired at the one real chokepoint - `NavHostCallbacks` - so every navigation-shaped action reports at its single origin rather than at the dozen screens that trigger it. It reuses the existing tier-1 `Usage(screen, action)` payload, so no schema changed and `action` can only ever be one of the 23 names, never a callsign, frequency or transcript. **Still open as its own gap**: settings toggles, corrections, exports and recording start/stop have no central dispatch to hook and remain uninstrumented. | closed |
| R-1101 | The analytics uploader's TLS path has never run | FR-ANL-9; D48 | `RealAnalyticsUploadClient` is implemented and unit-tested against a fake, but no test or run exercises a real HTTPS request, because no endpoint is configured anywhere yet. The first real upload will be the first execution of that code. Before any endpoint is deployed, stand one up locally (the reference ingest server in `tools/analytics/` is exactly that) and prove the client against it, including a refused certificate and a mid-upload failure. | spec **CLOSED** (`f10bddeb`, merged 2026-09-21): a real `SSLServerSocket` loopback fixture now drives `RealAnalyticsUploadClient` over actual TLS - trusted certificate succeeds, an untrusted one degrades safely to `PARTIAL_WRITE` rather than crashing, a connection killed just after handshake gives a mid-upload `PARTIAL_WRITE`, and `purge` is proven over TLS too. Additionally proven by hand against the real `tools/analytics/server.py` on loopback, including a refused certificate and a server killed mid-upload of a 7 MB body. Nothing was sent externally and no real destination is configured. The throwaway test certificate is labelled as compromised-by-construction in a README beside it. | closed |
| R-1102 | AC-193's network-silence proof for alerts has not been run | AC-193; FR-ALR-3; constitution V | The alert path is structurally barred from the network (`dependencyRules` proves `:pipeline` cannot reach `:net`), which is the strong form of the guarantee. AC-193 asks for the empirical half as well - a packet capture over a session in which alerts fire, showing nothing leaves the device. Needs a device and a capture tool; belongs with the H1-H15 hardware protocol rather than a builder session. | spec **2026-09-21, re-scoped on real evidence** (`a85e82d0`; transcript at `results/e2e-audit/R-1102-alert-silence-investigation.md`). The assumption in this row was wrong in a useful way: **packet capture does not need the reference phone**. `adb root` plus the emulator image's own `/system/bin/tcpdump` works, and the rig was validated with a positive control rather than trusted - 2 packets over an idle 8 s window against 182 packets over 8 s while a browser loaded a page, which is what makes a subsequent empty capture mean anything at all. **What actually blocks AC-193 is making an alert fire.** The one production path is `DataPassBResultSink.record` inside a live `RealCaptureService` session; `ReprocessRunner` deliberately never fires live alerts (R-1097); and no debug scenario touches the live coordinator - they seed historical Room rows only. A real capture could not be started either: every AVD in this fleet has a silent virtual microphone, already recorded by validator V8 as E2-E02. The builder confirmed the negative directly - zero sessions and transmissions in the database, no capture service ever running - rather than inferring it, and **offered no weaker proof in place of the one asked for**, which was the right call. Structural corroboration re-run: `dependencyRules` still shows no `:pipeline` or `:capture-android` edge to `:net` across 21 modules. So the strong form of the guarantee holds; the empirical half needs either a real microphone/rig or the injection seam filed as R-1152. | open |
| R-1103 | The tour script itself has no test | constitution II, VIII; R-1083 | R-1083's stale-manifest false green was a defect in `tools/ui-audit/tour.ps1`, the tool the constitution makes the arbiter of visual evidence, and it was fixed with no automated test because the repo has no PowerShell test harness. The run-id matching and the fewer-screenshots-than-steps check are covered only by a manual reproduction. Either add a harness (Pester) or move the logic somewhere testable, so the arbiter is itself checked. | process **FIXED** (branch `m-tooling-provenance`, commit `<pending>`): followed `diff.py`'s own same-night precedent (R-1116) rather than introducing Pester - the run-id-matching (`check_done_marker`) and fewer-results-than-requested (`verify_step_count`/`verify_pulled_count`) decisions, plus the manifest-line parsing both depended on (`summarize`), are now a plain Python module, `tools/ui-audit/tour_manifest.py`, with no device dependency. `tour.ps1` was rewired to shell out to it for all three decisions rather than keep its own inline copy - a builder that only extracted the logic without wiring it in would have reproduced exactly the "built but not connected" pattern this register is full of.
