New suite `tools/ui-audit/tests/test_tour_manifest.py` (19 cases) is picked up automatically by the existing `ui-audit-diff` CI job (`pytest tools/ui-audit -q`), no new job needed. The discriminating case this row names - a stale/foreign manifest reported as success - is `test_R_1103_a_foreign_run_ids_done_line_is_not_reported_done`, watched to fail against the pre-R-1083 shape (`return DoneResult(done=True)` unconditionally) before the fix, and restored. Wiring this in surfaced a genuine, previously unknown bug along the way: Windows PowerShell 5.1 prepends a UTF-8 BOM when piping a string to a native process's stdin, which would have silently corrupted every real tour run's first parsed line - fixed by decoding stdin as `utf-8-sig`, also test-covered and discriminated. **Open until a real device run exercises the wired-in calls** - this unit's own verification is 26 passing tests plus a fixture-text dry run of the exact PowerShell-to-Python call shapes, not a live tour. **FIXED** (`dd8d232a`, merged 2026-09-20): the tour's decisions moved out of PowerShell into a new pure `tools/ui-audit/tour_manifest.py` - run-id matching including R-1083's own stale/foreign-manifest case, manifest parsing (which had been duplicated inline twice), and the fewer-results-than-requested guards - and `tour.ps1` shells out to it for every decision, so the logic is wired in rather than parallel to the script. Python was chosen over Pester to follow the same night's `diff.py` precedent instead of standing up a second test framework; the existing `ui-audit-diff` CI job picks the suite up automatically. 19 cases, discriminated by restoring the pre-R-1083 `return DoneResult(done=True)`, which turned the named foreign-run-id case red. **A real bug was found by doing this**: Windows PowerShell 5.1 prepends a UTF-8 BOM when piping a string to a native process's stdin, which would have corrupted the first line of every real tour poll - fixed by decoding as `utf-8-sig`, with its own test. That defect was invisible while the logic lived in PowerShell, which is the argument for this row. Open only until a real emulator run exercises the rewired script. | fixed |
| R-1104 | Setup only rechecks overnight survival when it is reopened | AC-189; NFR-8; constitution IV | The Overnight step now returns until the heartbeat proves the app survived in the background, which is the right shape - but `MainActivity`'s fast path does not reopen Setup solely to recheck, so an operator who never revisits Setup is never re-asked. On ColorOS, where the battery-exemption API lies, that is the exact population the check exists for. | spec **CLOSED** (`edc22979`, merged 2026-09-20): `MainActivity.route()` now runs the same overnight-survival check `SetupActivity` already ran, so the operator who completed setup once and always takes the fast path is re-asked - which on ColorOS is the entire population the check exists for. It reuses the existing `:data` session-history `OvernightSurvivalChecker` and touches no heartbeat code, deliberately, since another builder was changing that in parallel. Four cases including the permission-revoked short-circuit; discriminated. Two doc comments that named this exact gap as open are corrected. | closed |
| R-1105 | Two shipped artifacts are unproven: the play APK's native libraries and the model mirror | FR-AST-13, FR-AST-14; D43, D44 | `verifySherpaNativeLibrariesPackaged` checks the `full` APK only, so nothing asserts the slim `play` build packages its native libraries correctly - and `play` is the variant intended for the store. Separately, `:app:publishModelMirror` runs only on a `v*.*.*` tag, so the `models-v1` mirror that every `play` install downloads from has never actually been published or downloaded from. Both are provable before the first store submission; neither is proven now. | spec **2026-09-21, half closed, half is the operator's** (lead). The play artifact's native libraries **are** now verified: `verifySherpaNativeLibrariesPackaged` has a `playRelease` sibling wired into `:app:check`, proven able to fail by deleting `lib/x86_64/libonnxruntime.so` from the real assembled APK and watching it name the missing library. What remains is the mirror, and it is not a code gap: **all five `mirrorUrl` entries in `bundled-assets.json` return 404 and the `models-v1` release has never been created**, because `:app:publishModelMirror` runs only on a `v*.*.*` tag. Every slim `play` install would therefore fail setup at the models step. Creating that release publishes content publicly under the operator's account, which is outward-facing and permanent, so the lead deliberately did not do it unprompted. **Blocked on the operator**: push a `v*.*.*` tag, or create `models-v1` by hand and run the publish task. Worth doing before any store submission, since it is the difference between a working slim build and a broken one. | open |
| R-1106 | AndroidManifest has no dataExtractionRules | technical-design Â§12.4; NFR-6a | The technical design calls for `android:dataExtractionRules`; the manifest has none, so Android's own backup and device-transfer defaults decide what leaves the phone - on an app whose first principle is that audio and its content stay on the device. Pre-existing, noticed while wiring P30's FileProvider. Decide the rules explicitly and declare them. | spec **CLOSED** (`edc22979`, merged; lead-verified 2026-09-20). `app/src/main/res/xml/data_extraction_rules.xml` excludes `domain="root"` from both `<cloud-backup>` and `<device-transfer>`, and the lead read `AndroidManifest.xml:48` directly to confirm `android:dataExtractionRules="@xml/data_extraction_rules"` is actually wired - the one thing no JVM test can prove, since `ApplicationInfo.dataExtractionRulesRes` is not in the public SDK 34 stub. Excluding everything is argued rather than defaulted: `allowBackup="false"` already refuses both routes, so this file is the belt-and-braces that still holds if that flag is ever flipped by mistake, and the four declared outbound channels are each their own consented path - none of them is *restore my phone onto a new one*. No `external` or `media` domain is declared, deliberately, because the app never writes there and an exclusion for a domain nothing is written to would document a false capability. | closed |
| R-1107 | The mic-denied setup screen has an artboard and no tour step | constitution VIII; R-770, R-1091 | Found by the lead while capturing Wave L: `design/canvas/Setup-Mic-Denied.dc.html` exists and R-1091 just changed what it draws (segment 2 now halted, because a permanently denied microphone genuinely halts capture), but `tools/ui-audit/tour.json` has **no step for it at all** - `-Only "setup-mic-denied/*"` matches nothing across 279 steps. A designed, shipped screen with no capture cannot be verified, which is R-770's own complaint in a specific instance. Add a scenario that reaches the permanently-denied state and tour steps at 1.0 and 2.0. | process **CLOSED** (lead, 2026-09-21): the `setup-mic-denied` scenario and its 1.0/2.0 steps exist and ran in the full tour, so the screen with an artboard and no capture now has both. The two blockers this row named were real and both had to be removed: `install.ps1` granting `RECORD_AUDIO` unconditionally, and `SetupActivity.shouldRefreshStepOnResume` re-deriving the live step on every resume. | closed |
| R-1108 | Two more screens carry the confirmed empty-label defect | FR-A11Y; R-1090 | Reported by the R-1090 sweep and deliberately left, being outside its screen list: `ImproveScreens.kt` and `FrequencyScreen.kt` use the same `clickable` + trailing `mergeDescendants` shape that a device dump proves exports an empty `content-desc` on the clickable node, with the real label stranded on a non-clickable child. The fix is mechanical and already established in `Rows.kt`/`Controls.kt`; what is missing is the device dump for those two screens. | spec **FIXED, awaiting a device dump** (`edc22979`, merged 2026-09-20): `ImproveGroupRow`, `FrequencyRow` and `RegularRow` now use `clearAndSetSemantics` with an explicit multi-entry `SemanticsProperties.Text` list beside the `contentDescription` - the shape `Rows.kt` and `Controls.kt` already establish, not a third invention. `FrequencyRow`'s label locals were hoisted so the exported list is identical to what renders, which `clearAndSetSemantics` otherwise erases. **Search's three components were inspected and deliberately not changed**: `WidenRow`, `StruckThroughQueryField` and `UnappliedTextCountLine` set `contentDescription` through a plain non-merging `semantics {}` on the node that carries the fact, which is structurally unlike the confirmed bug - that one is specifically a merge-from-descendants step failing on device. The builder was explicit that Robolectric passes the broken shape too, so these tests prove the tree carries a non-empty description and nothing more; only a device `uiautomator` pass settles it. | fixed |
| R-1109 | Production Pass B runs with every evidence prior dead | FR-LEX-9; FR-LEX-25..27, FR-LEX-31; constitution I | `PassBFactory.create` builds `PriorCombiner(emptyList())`, so frequency, band plausibility, database presence, recency, geography, conversation context and my-stations contribute nothing to any real capture. The seven priors exist, are tested, and are constructed only by `:eval`'s `Main.kt` and the on-device harness. Every callsign the app has ever resolved on a device was resolved by grammar and confusion distance alone. Filed by the 2026-09-20 roadmap audit; P33 owns the fix. | halt **2026-09-20, P33 (`aad0d2db`, merged):** `PassBFactory` now builds `PriorCombiner(defaultPriors(PropagationModel()))` instead of `PriorCombiner(emptyList())`, and `PassBFactoryCalibrationTest.R_1109` proves all seven priors contribute a non-zero term on a fixture over (discriminated: reverted to `emptyList()`, that case alone went red). **This row stays open, because the fix is structural only.** The lead verified at HEAD that `PassBFactory.create` constructs `PassB(...)` without passing `contextFor`, so production keeps `PassB.kt:104`'s default `{ RankingContext() }` - an empty context on every over. Frequency, band plausibility, database presence, recency, geography, conversation context and my-stations therefore all still read cold-start fields on a real device, and this row's own sentence - every callsign is resolved by grammar and confusion distance alone - remains true in production. Split out as R-1124, which is the half that changes a device result. **2026-09-21, the ground under this row changed** (lead). R-1132 landed, so stations are now created by production code for the first time - which means the **database-presence and recency priors, and conversation context, finally have real rows to read on a device**, where R-1124's note correctly said they would read nothing. This row's original sentence - every callsign resolved by grammar and confusion distance alone - is no longer true in production. What remains is the **four priors that are honestly cold** and cannot be made warm without reference data that does not exist anywhere in the app: *frequency* needs a repeater/memory-channel roster (FR-LEX-32's WWARA import is unbuilt), *band plausibility* and *geographic* both need a prefix-to-centroid table (`ItuPrefixTable` carries no coordinates), and *my-stations* (FR-LEX-14) has no storage and no Settings surface. Those are new work - reference data and a settings surface - rather than defects, and the operator has asked for bugs first. **Held deliberately, not overlooked.** | open |
| R-1110 | `CONFIRMED` is asserted on an uncalibrated score | FR-LEX-17..21; FR-SPK-10; constitution I, VI | The same factory sets `confirmThreshold = -1f`, and `CallsignResolver` then reports `CONFIRMED` with `confidence = totalScore.coerceIn(0f, 1f)` while the fingerprint carries `calibrationVersion = null`. A threshold below every possible score means the highest-scoring candidate is always confirmed. This is Principle I breached at the data layer, not in the UI, and it falsifies the listing sentence "every callsign says how sure it is". The fix is `AMBIGUOUS` until a calibrator exists - never a hand-picked constant, which would be a number without its fold. | halt **FIXED, awaiting device evidence** (P33 `aad0d2db`, merged): `confirmThreshold = -1f` is gone. `CallsignResolver` now takes a `Calibrator?` rather than a raw float, and with no calibrator present resolves to `AMBIGUOUS` - deliberately not `UNKNOWN`, since a real separated candidate exists and claiming nothing would be equally dishonest. No hand-picked constant was substituted, per constitution VI. `PassBFingerprintBuilder` hashes the threshold as `Float?` so *uncalibrated* is its own honest configuration, and a `FakeCalibrator` makes the rule testable in both directions. Discriminated: restoring `calibrator ?: CONFIRMED` turned exactly the four R_1110 cases red and left the rest green. **Open until a device dump shows no `CONFIRMED` without a calibration version** - the unit names that as its evidence, and a passing test is not it. Note the consequence recorded in R-1125. | fixed |
| R-1111 | Improve is unreachable on every real session | FR-REP-1..11; AC-39 | `ImprovePolling.root` keeps only sessions whose `deviceTier` is non-null; `RealCaptureService.buildSessionEntity` writes `deviceTier = null` unconditionally. The whole reprocessing surface - drawer entry, artboard, `RealImproveRunner`, `ReprocessRunner` - is therefore reachable only from seeded debug scenarios, and the class's own KDoc asserts the opposite. Either write a real tier or hide the destination; shipping a drawer entry that leads nowhere is the visible half. | halt **FIXED** (`457243ee`, merged 2026-09-20): `RealCaptureService.buildSessionEntity` now writes `Tier.entries[tierFromShedLevel()].name` instead of an unconditional `null`, so `ImprovePolling.root` stops filtering every real session out and the whole reprocessing surface becomes reachable outside a seeded debug scenario. The builder reused the class's **own existing** `tierFromShedLevel()` rather than inventing a second notion of tier, and checked the others rather than assuming: `ReprocessRunner`, `ImprovePolling` and `SettingsPolling` all compute the identical `(MAX_TIER - ShedStatus.currentLevel)` formula independently, and **no disagreement was found**. `ImprovePolling`'s KDoc, which asserted the opposite behaviour, is corrected. Two cases driven through the real service harness, discriminated. See R-1141 for the separate gap this surfaced. | closed |
| R-1112 | The field-report channel is absent from every signed build | FR-OBS-6..12; D55 | `FieldReportAppWiring` gates construction on `BuildConfig.DEBUG`, and the upload client factory hardcodes the repository. The channel built to answer field session 1's verbal-report problem is missing from exactly the release builds a beta ships, and its destination is not build-configurable as D49 requires. D55 makes both launch blockers. Note the visibility guard itself is wired and fail-closed - do not rebuild it. | halt **FIXED, awaiting device evidence** (P36 `0b486cec`, merged): `FieldReportAppWiring` and `FieldReportRecorder` no longer consult `BuildConfig.DEBUG` at all, and the builder correctly followed the gate into two files its brief did not name - `SettingsContent.kt`'s `FieldReportHost`, which nulled the whole section out in a release build, and `SettingsDiagnosticsScreen.kt`'s *(debug builds only)* header - without which the fix would have been invisible. `FieldReportUploadClientFactory.DESTINATION_REPOSITORY` is gone; `shouldCreate(token, repository)` requires both non-blank, so a build with no configured destination refuses rather than falling back, per D49. No URL was invented or committed. The visibility guard was not touched, as instructed. **Open until an upload from a signed build lands in the private repository** - which the operator has not created yet. | fixed |
| R-1113 | Route-change and interruption events never fire in production | FR-CAP-3; FR-RUN-11; constitution IV | `AndroidAudioIo.setEventListener` stores the callback and nothing ever invokes it; only `FakeAudioIo` emits route and interruption events, so every FR-CAP-3 recovery test passes against the fake while the real device has one signal, `io.read() < 0`. Nothing re-verifies the route after the first successful read either. A route that switches to the built-in microphone at 03:00 yields hours of room audio filed as radio audio - the highest-consequence silent failure in the system. | halt **FIXED, awaiting hardware** (P34 `a1cdd665`, merged 2026-09-20): `AndroidAudioIo` registers a real `AudioManager.registerAudioDeviceCallback` and fires `AudioIoEvent.Interrupted` when the OS reports the selected device gone. The API choice was made on evidence rather than preference: `AudioRecord.OnRoutingChangedListener` has no Robolectric shadow at all, so a test against it would have proved nothing, while `ShadowAudioManager` can genuinely fire `AudioDeviceCallback`. Because no Android API reliably reports a *silent* reroute, `AudioRecordSource` also polls `routeProbe` every 30 s - launched `UNDISPATCHED`, cancelled in a `finally` on every exit path, and never awaited by the read loop. Constitution IV was tested, not assumed: with the probe hung on a never-completed `Deferred`, six frames still deliver and no samples drop, and replacing the `launch` with an inline awaited call hangs that test - so it detects a blocking implementation rather than merely asserting about one. `AndroidAudioIoInterruptionGapTest` proves the recorded gap equals the true 7000 ms outage. **Open until H4 and the 8-hour run**: whether the callback fires promptly on ColorOS, whether 30 s is the right interval, and whether a real silent reroute is caught are all device questions. | fixed |
| R-1114 | Capture never negotiates the adapter's native sample rate | FR-CAP-2, FR-CAP-2a | `AndroidAudioIo` defaults to 48 000 Hz and `RealCaptureService` constructs it with no argument, so a USB adapter that offers only 44.1 kHz fails to open rather than being resampled deterministically. AGENTS.md advertises the opposite ("capture negotiates the device's native rate and resamples deterministically"), and most USB audio adapters do not offer 16 kHz. | spec **FIXED, awaiting hardware** (P34 `a1cdd665`, merged 2026-09-20): a new pure `SampleRateNegotiator.negotiate(preferred, supported)` is consulted inside `AndroidAudioIo.select()` against the selected device's real `AudioDeviceInfo.getSampleRates()`, so the 48 kHz-only default is gone and AGENTS.md's claim is true. Worth recording how the test was made honest: `AudioDeviceInfoBuilder.setProfiles()` does **not** feed `getSampleRates()` - the builder's own path writes `AudioPort.mSamplingRates` - which the builder established by decompiling the API 31 `android-all` jar rather than trusting the fixture, so the test reaches that field the same way Robolectric does internally. A fixture that silently did not exercise the real accessor would have been the exact defect this row is about. **Open until a real 44.1 kHz-only adapter opens end to end**: Robolectric's `AudioRecord` shadow is permissive and cannot fail the way hardware does. | fixed |
| R-1115 | The heartbeat keeps no history, so the 30-minute prove-it test cannot run | NFR-8; FR-SVC-5b; AC-189 | The heartbeat is written only inside the `CaptureEvent.Frames` branch into a one-line file overwritten each beat, so no trail survives on the device and a frames-starved stretch is indistinguishable from an OS kill. Consequently `ProveItAnalyzer` has no production caller, `DiagnosticsLog.logHeartbeatGap` has none, and D8 - the test `spec/test-plan.md` Â§7 says must predict the 8-hour gate - is unrunnable as built. On ColorOS, whose battery-exemption API lies, this is the one liveness evidence that matters. | spec **FIXED, awaiting the device runs it exists to enable** (`457243ee`, merged 2026-09-20): new `HeartbeatTrail` - append-only, bounded to 960 rows (8 hours at the 30 s cadence) - beside the unchanged single-record store, with `ProveItAnalyzer` and `DiagnosticsLog.logHeartbeatGap` wired to real callers for the first time. **The design point is that the beat no longer depends on frames arriving**: an independent ticker beats on its own schedule, and the frame path only sets a flag and dispatches through `scope.launch{}`, so no synchronous I/O ever reaches the frame-collecting coroutine (Principle IV, which this row's fix could easily have breached). That is what separates the two cases the row is about - a starved-but-alive stretch keeps landing beats carrying `framesObservedSinceLastBeat = false` with no gap, while an OS kill simply stops, and the first beat after relaunch reports the real elapsed gap against the dead process's last row. Rotation does not delete quietly (Principle III): a `.rotated` sidecar accumulates the dropped count and the wall-time span. Nine cases, discriminated. **Open until D8's 30-minute test and the 8-hour gate actually run on the device** - this change gives them a trail to read, which they never had, but is not a substitute for running them. The ticker's real-time behaviour has no automated test, following this file's own established convention of testing the decoupled pieces rather than running a 30 s loop under Robolectric. | fixed |
| R-1116 | `diff.py` cannot fail, and cannot see a changed line of text | constitution VIII; R-1083, R-1103 | The tour's stale-manifest false green was fixed and the run-id plumbing is connected end to end - but the comparison step still returns 0 except when a manifest file is missing, never reads `runId` or `apkHash`, and compares 4x4 mean-RGB signatures at threshold 6.0, where a one-line text difference moves the signature by about 1.0. The R-1046-class defects this register is full of are structurally invisible to it. Until this is fixed, a `diff.py` "unchanged" is not evidence of anything. | process | open |
| R-1117 | The strongest built differentiator has never been captured | constitution VIII; FR-UI-8; R-770 | `Detail-Why` has an artboard and no tour step - `tour.json`'s own comment says it has no seed seam, and `TourSpec` has `revisionsOpen` but no `whyOpen`. It is reachable only from the detail screen's "Full lattice" action: `AttributionMarker`, `AttributionRow` and `TitleAttributionRow` carry no `onClick`, so no marker anywhere opens the screen that explains it. Against its artboard the built screen is also missing the per-candidate reasons and the bottom action bar, and renders the runner-up candidate twice. | design **FIXED in the shipped app, partly** (`2b53110e`, merged 2026-09-20). This row's real complaint was not the missing capture but that **no operator could reach the screen at all**: `AttributionMarker`/`AttributionRow`/`TitleAttributionRow` now take a nullable `onClick` (default `null`, so no existing caller gains even an inert `clickable`), and the detail screen wires the title row to `onOpenWhy` - so tapping the callsign at the top of an over now opens Detail-Why, a far larger target than the pre-existing *Full lattice* text action. Proved with no seed at all. One artboard deviation also fixed: the screen rendered the runner-up candidate twice, once in the list and again as a separate *Runner-up* line the board never had. **Still open**: the per-candidate reasons need derived data no current API returns, and the bottom action bar needs the correction callbacks `MainDestination` builds inline - both reported rather than bodged. **CLOSED on capture evidence** (lead, 2026-09-21, `results/ui-audit/overnight/D05-why.png` plus `@2x` and `@2x-end`). The screen renders its four numbered sections - phonetic lattice with per-unit scores, grammar, candidates that survived, and priors for the chosen candidate - and **the duplicated runner-up line is gone**, which was this row's one concrete artboard deviation. The caption that makes the screen worth having is present and correct: *a prior that argued against is negative and amber; one with no data yet reads "cold start", not zero - the two are different facts*. The drill-in itself is the real fix: tapping the callsign at the top of an over now opens this screen, where before nothing in the shipped app could reach it. **Still open as R-1144**: the per-candidate reasons and the bottom action bar the artboard also draws. | closed |
| R-1118 | A thread's participant order drops every unattributed over | FR-SPK-29, FR-SPK-30; AC-164 | `RoomThreadRepository`'s `participantOrder` filters to overs with a resolved callsign, so an `UNKNOWN` over is absent from the order a net roster would be built from. The roster surface Teams is meant to sell (D51) would silently omit exactly the check-ins that need an operator's attention. | spec **CLOSED** (`82eaebed`, merged 2026-09-20): `participantOrderKey` gives every over a roster slot - station id, else voiceprint id, else `UNKNOWN` - so an unattributed over is never dropped from the order a net roster is built from. The builder measured the real magnitude and it was worse than filed: since `CONFIRMED` became unreachable without a calibration, `stationId` is null for essentially every freshly-resolved over, so the old filter left `participantOrder` close to permanently empty in production, not merely missing an unknown minority. The consequence it surfaced is filed as R-1137. | closed |
| R-1119 | Stale work-queue leases are never recovered | FR-RUN-8; AC-47 | `WorkQueue.recoverStaleLeases` is implemented and tested, and nothing calls it at service start. ColorOS kills the app mid-Pass-B routinely, and the item it was working stays `LEASED` forever - so a killed night loses the overs it was mid-way through, silently, which is the pattern P9's own rationale describes. | spec **FIXED, awaiting the device run** (`0b10bf56`, merged 2026-09-20): `WorkQueue.recoverStaleLeasesAtLaunch()` is called from `OrtApplication.onCreate` on a background dispatcher after a successful database open. It generates a fresh run id, since a launching process has by construction leased nothing, so every still-LEASED row is unconditionally stale. Open until a real kill mid-Pass-B on a device shows the row returning to READY and the over completing - this unit's own Done-when, and Robolectric is not it. | fixed |
| R-1120 | An unguarded index build can brick launch permanently | FR-STO-7; constitution III | `applyHandWrittenSchema` creates the partial unique indices without guarding against pre-existing violating rows, and a throw from `create()` has no recovery path, so an upgrade over a database that violates one fails on every subsequent launch with no in-app route out. A tester who installed an earlier build is the population at risk. | halt **FIXED, awaiting the device run** (`0b10bf56`, merged 2026-09-20): three guard functions run immediately before their `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX`, each resolving a violation by keeping the highest-rowid row and **demoting** the rest - `isCurrent = 0`, the same shape `TranscriptDao.supersede` already produces, or a terminal FAILED with a recorded reason for the work queue. Nothing is deleted, which is what makes it legal under Principle III. A startup wrapper catches anything the guard does not anticipate and routes to the existing migration failure screen instead of a crash loop. Open until an upgrade over a genuinely violating database is observed opening on a device. | fixed |
| R-1121 | Hallucination control 3 is inert, and no acoustic confidence exists anywhere | FR-ASR-5, FR-ASR-6; AC-6 | `RealSherpaDecoder` returns `noSpeechProb = null` and `NoSpeechProbRule` accepts on null, so AC-6 rests on five controls rather than six, with the Whisper-default ceilings never refitted. More broadly every `Hypothesis`/`TokenScore` logProb is `0f` and the text-derived lattice writes `UnitScore(unit, 0f)`, so there is no acoustic confidence in the system - which is why calibration (R-1110) is not simply "fit Platt to what we have". | spec **FIXED as far as the binding allows** (`412f68b0`, merged 2026-09-20). The builder answered the question this row really asks by decompiling the resolved `sherpa-onnx-jvm-1.13.7.jar` with `javap -p`, because upstream publishes no source for the binding: `OfflineRecognizerResult` - the only decode result `RealSherpaDecoder` ever sees - carries `text`, `tokens`, `timestamps`, `durations`, `lang`, `emotion`, `event` **and nothing else**. No `no_speech_prob`, no per-token or per-hypothesis log probability, no n-best list. By contrast `OnlineRecognizerResult`, the streaming Pass A path this module does not use, does carry per-token `ysProbs`. So this is a binding gap specific to the offline recognizer, not a wiring gap - there was nothing to connect. **What changed is the honesty:** `RejectionVerdict` gains `Indeterminate(rule, reason)`; `NoSpeechProbRule` returns it on a null probability instead of silently accepting; `RejectionPipeline` accumulates inert rule ids into a new `PassBOutcome.inertControls`; and `Hypothesis.logProb`/`TokenScore.logProb` become `Float?` with `RealSherpaDecoder` writing `null` rather than a fabricated `0f`, which had been asserting absolute certainty. AC-6 therefore still rests on five controls, but the sixth now *says* it is inert instead of looking healthy - which is the defect pattern this register exists to catch. No threshold was invented: Whisper's unrefitted `0.60` ceiling stands and is labelled as unrefitted, since refitting needs a labelled dataset that does not exist. `decodeTokens` was extracted to an internal top-level function so it is testable against a directly constructed result with no native call. Discriminated both ways. **Left open as R-1133.** | fixed |
| R-1122 | Every number the corpus harness emits lacks machine and provider | constitution VI | `corpus/src/corpus/fingerprint.py` records manifest, fold, config, git revision and Python version - but not machine, execution provider or thread count, which constitution VI names explicitly ("every reported figure carries fold, machine, execution provider, thread count, model version and run fingerprint"). Every accuracy number produced today would therefore be unreportable under the project's own rule; fix before the first dev-fold measurement, not after. | spec **FIXED** (`dd8d232a`, merged 2026-09-20): `run_fingerprint` now hashes `machine`, `provider` and `threads` beside manifest, fold, config, git revision and Python version, and `report.py` gained the missing `thread_count`. Two choices worth recording. `machine_descriptor()` deliberately **excludes `platform.node()`** - a hostname identifies the operator and this file's output is meant to be publishable - and records OS, release, architecture, processor and core count instead. `execution_provider()` returns `"none (harness v0, no model runs)"` rather than a plausible provider name, because the harness runs no model yet and naming one would be the kind of number-without-its-provenance this row exists to prevent. `harness.py` computes all three once and feeds the same values to both the report and the fingerprint, so the two can never disagree. Nine new cases plus three in the harness; discriminated by reverting all three files, which made the new suites fail to import. | closed |
| R-1123 | Native aborts, ANRs and OOM are unreportable | FR-ANL-3; R-1052 | `CrashCaptureHandler` chains a `Thread.UncaughtExceptionHandler`, which never fires for a native abort - so the crash class most likely to end a beta session (an uncaught `Ort::Exception` from the sherpa JNI reaching `std::terminate`) produces nothing at all. There is no ANR watchdog (`isAnr` is permanently null) and no `OutOfMemoryError` handler anywhere in `src/main`; the slim `play` variant, which downloads models at setup, is the likeliest OOM candidate. | spec **CLOSED as far as an in-process handler can reach** (`f10bddeb`, merged 2026-09-21): a new `AnrWatchdog` pings the main thread every 5 s and reports a stall once per episode - the first place in this codebase where `isAnr` is genuinely `true` rather than permanently null - and `ProcessExitReasonReporter` reads `getHistoricalProcessExitReasons` on the next launch to classify NATIVE_CRASH/ANR/LOW_MEMORY, with the `ApplicationExitInfo` reason values verified against `android.jar` by `javap` rather than assumed. Below API 30 it returns empty, stated rather than silent. **A native abort still cannot be caught live** - that needs a signal handler - and the builder said so plainly instead of implying coverage it does not have. Its own discrimination caught a real bug in the fix: the first watchdog reported 9 false stalls in 10 healthy cycles from a wall-clock-diff error. | closed |
| R-1124 | Pass B's ranking context is empty in production, so the wired priors read nothing | FR-LEX-9; FR-LEX-25..27, FR-LEX-31; constitution I; R-1109 | The half of R-1109 that P33 did not reach, reported by its builder and confirmed by the lead at HEAD. `PassB`'s `contextFor` parameter defaults to `{ RankingContext() }` and `PassBFactory.create` never passes one, so every prior the same unit just wired evaluates against an empty context on every real over. The combiner is connected and its input is not: the same built-but-not-connected shape as the original finding, one level down. Populating the context means reading frequency and band from the session, database presence and recency from `:data`, geography and my-stations from settings, and conversation context from the thread - real work against `:data`, and the unit that does it is what finally makes the priors change a device result. | halt **PARTLY FIXED** (`2c627ea8`, merged 2026-09-20): `PassB`'s `contextFor` lambda is replaced by a `RankingContextSource` suspend interface, and `PassBFactory` wires the real `DataRankingContextSource(db)`, called after `CallsignGrammar.parse` produces candidates - there is nothing to scope a lookup to before that. Three of the seven priors now read real `:data`: **database presence** and **recency** from `CatalogDao.getStation`, and **conversation context** from `RoomThreadRepository`. The other four are left **explicitly cold with named reasons** rather than handed a plausible empty value, which is the distinction this row was filed over: *frequency* needs a repeater/memory-channel roster that exists nowhere on the device (FR-LEX-32's WWARA import is unbuilt); *band plausibility* and *geographic* both need a prefix-to-centroid table that does not exist (`ItuPrefixTable` carries no coordinates), and geographic was left `null` outright rather than given a function that always returns `null`; *my-stations* (FR-LEX-14) has no storage and no Settings surface. Constitution IV was tested, not assumed - `PassBTest.R_1124` pins that context assembly suspends rather than blocking, discriminated by swapping in a real `Thread.sleep`. **Stays open** for the four cold priors, and see R-1132, which means even the three wired ones read nothing on a device today. **2026-09-21 update** (lead): the caveat this row ended on is resolved. It said *even the three wired ones read nothing on a device today* because no production path ever created a station; R-1132 fixed that, so database presence, recency and conversation context now read real rows. The four cold priors remain, blocked on reference data that does not exist - see R-1109 for the itemised list and why they are held rather than forgotten. | open |
| R-1125 | A callsign alert watch can no longer fire in production at all | FR-ALR-3; AC-194; R-1110 | A true consequence of P33, surfaced by its builder rather than hidden: `AlertMatcher.matches` resolves `AlertWatch.Callsign` as `input.stationId?.equals(watch.callsign)`, and `AlertMatchInput.stationId` is populated only when the attribution carries a station - which, correctly, no longer happens without a calibrator. So the headline of the live-alerts feature, *tell me when W7ABC is on*, is inert in every production build, while keyword and frequency watches still work. This is not an argument for putting the uncalibrated `CONFIRMED` back; it is an argument that a callsign watch must match on the resolved candidate and its state, and say in the notification how sure it is. `RealCaptureServiceTest`'s FR_ALR_3 case was moved to a keyword watch to keep proving the dispatch wiring - that test no longer covers the callsign path, which is why this row exists. | halt **FIXED** (`2c627ea8`, merged 2026-09-20): `AlertMatchInput` gains `resolvedCallsign` - the top-ranked candidate, independent of attribution state - and `AlertMatcher` matches a `Callsign` watch against it rather than against `stationId`. The uncalibrated `CONFIRMED` was not restored: the notification states the real `AttributionState` for every non-`CONFIRMED` firing, so a watch fires on an `AMBIGUOUS` candidate and says so. `RealCaptureServiceTest.FR_ALR_3` regains real callsign-watch coverage through the full capture composition, beside the keyword watch P33 had to fall back to. Discriminated on the match target. Device evidence for the alert path as a whole remains AC-193's business (R-1102). | fixed |
| R-1126 | A calibration re-fit will not mark Pass B rows for reprocessing | FR-REP-4; constitution VI; R-1110 | Noticed by P33's builder while adding the calibration seam, and out of that unit's scope (`:core`). `Materiality` carries a `CALIBRATION_VERSION` entry for `PassId.D_RESOLVE` but none for `PassId.B_OFFLINE`, so when a fitted calibrator eventually ships, the existing Pass B rows resolved without one will not be offered as reprocess candidates. Every over captured before calibration would keep its `AMBIGUOUS` state permanently unless the operator knew to force a pass by hand. Small change, easy to lose - file it now while the reason is legible. | spec **FIXED** (`5519ed12`, merged 2026-09-20): `Materiality.table[PassId.B_OFFLINE]` now includes `FingerprintField.CALIBRATION_VERSION`, so when a fitted calibrator ships, the overs resolved without one become reprocess candidates instead of keeping `AMBIGUOUS` forever. Pinned by `PassFingerprintTest.R_1126 a calibration re-fit makes a Pass B row a reprocess candidate too`, discriminated. | closed |
| R-1127 | Seven designed screens can never be captured without a src/main seed seam | constitution VIII; R-770, R-1107, R-1117, R-085 | The tour-coverage unit added 18 steps and then reported, precisely, what it could not reach from `app/src/debug` and `tour.json` alone. Each of these is a screen that exists, is designed, and has no path a capture can take, so by constitution VIII none of them is verified and the register rows that depend on them cannot close: **D08-D11 the correction sheets** (R-052) and **detail > label** (R-056) are interaction-only sheets with no `NavSeed`; **setup > mic denied** (R-085, R-1107) is doubly blocked - `install.ps1` grants `RECORD_AUDIO` unconditionally, and `SetupActivity.shouldRefreshStepOnResume` re-derives the live step on every `onResume` while `step == MICROPHONE_DENIED`, stomping a debug-forced `EXTRA_STEP` before it can be captured; **S06 Setup-Route-Mismatch** (R-282) gates on a private in-memory `verifyState`; **R04 Improve-Done** (R-350) holds unseeded local state in `ImproveContent`; and **D05 Detail-Why** (R-180, R-320) has no seam at all, which R-1117 records separately as the drill-in that no marker opens. The shape is one unit, not seven: give each of these screens a seed seam of the kind the rest of the app already has. Note `FQ02`'s caption (R-431) is a different problem - a missing fixture with zero not-listening hours, inside the tour's own ownership. | process **FIXED - all seven screens are now reachable** (`2b53110e`, merged 2026-09-20), awaiting the batch tour that captures them. D08/D09/D10 via `NavSeed.correctionStep`; detail > label via `openTransmissionLabel`; mic-denied via a new `DebugMicPermissionOverride`; S06 route-mismatch by backfilling `RenderRouteMismatch` from `DebugRouteCheckOverride` on a cold launch; D05 Detail-Why via `openTransmissionWhy`. D11 needed nothing - it was already reachable through the real `onChooseCandidate` flow. Still uncapturable and reported rather than glossed: R02/R03 Improve-Select/Improve-Running remain unseeded local state, outside this row's own named list. | fixed |
| R-1128 | The field-report redaction allowlist misses the stations list, and fails open by default | constitution V; FR-OBS-7, FR-SPK-25, FR-DIG-13; D55; R-1112 | Found by the lead reading the code after P36 merged, prompted by that unit's own honest note that a destination-level allowlist cannot see a name rendered somewhere it does not name. It cannot, and one already is. `RecorderDestination.mayRenderUserSuppliedContent()` names only `STATION_DETAIL`, but `StationsAndFrequencies.kt:428` sets `givenName = station.userName` on the **list** entry, and `StationScreen.kt:220-222` draws it as visible text (`Text(text = " - $it")`) on every station row, with the same value in the row's `contentDescription` (`:172`) and `Text` semantics (`:198`). So a field-report frame captured on the `STATIONS` destination renders an operator-typed station name, and constitution V admits no exception: user-supplied names leave the device in no channel and no tier. **Two things are wrong, not one.** The allowlist is under-inclusive, and its default direction is unsafe - a destination that is not named is capturable, so every screen added from now on leaks until someone remembers. The fix asked for is a derivation, not another guess one screen later: work from the fields the constitution names to the view states that carry them to the destinations that render them, and leave that derivation written in the code. Whether a corrected callsign counts as *station knowledge* under the same rule is the open question inside this row, and it must be argued rather than assumed either way. | halt **FIXED** (`18312bf2`, merged 2026-09-20), and fixed the way the row asked - by derivation, not by adding one more screen to a guess. The table (field -> view state -> destination) is written into `ScreenFrameCapturer.kt` as the durable artefact, and it turned up a third leak nobody had named: `StationSearchRow.userName` reaches `TRANSMISSION_DETAIL` through `CorrectionSheet`, composed inline inside `TransmissionDetailContent`. The builder pointedly did **not** rest on the fact that today's one-capture-per-arrival timing happens to predate that sheet opening, since a future trigger firing twice would start leaking silently - the destination is excluded on the derivation's own terms. **The default direction is inverted**: `CLEARED_FOR_CAPTURE` is now an allowlist, so an unnamed destination is not capturable, matching the fail-closed shape the upload client's visibility guard already uses. The cost is real and was stated rather than glossed - every future screen captures nothing until someone adds it and extends the table - and it is the right trade, because a shipped frame cannot be unshipped and the deny-list direction already produced one proven leak. **The lead checked the corrected-callsign argument against the spec rather than accepting it**: FR-DIG-13's *station knowledge* is scoped to FR-DIG-7's facts column, which lists frequencies and times heard, activity pattern, POTA/SOTA references, grid squares spoken, ITU region and over counts by attribution state - the callsign is the record's key, not a fact in it - and FR-SPK-25 governs user-supplied **names**. So a corrected callsign is capturable, and the argument stands. Residual question left open deliberately, not overlooked: whether a frequency's own activity pattern on `FREQUENCY_DETAIL` is station knowledge by another route. Device evidence for the redaction is still outstanding, as with the rest of P36. | fixed |
| R-1129 | No tour step opens an individual licence notice, so the Gemma terms are never captured | NFR-6d; constitution VIII; R-1127 | Reported by P36 and confirmed against `tour.json`: `overnight/CF12-settings-licenses` captures the licence *list*, and nothing drills into a notice's own detail screen. P36 just grew `gemma.txt` to the full Terms of Use and Prohibited Use Policy, which Gemma's licence requires the app to display - so the one screen carrying a legal obligation has no capture proving it renders, and its text is now long enough that whether it scrolls and ends correctly at font scale 2.0 is a real question, not a formality. Same family as R-1127 but a much cheaper fix: the detail screen is reachable by an ordinary tap, so this needs a tour step rather than a `src/main` seam. | process **FIXED, awaiting the batch capture** (`2b53110e`, merged 2026-09-20): new `overnight/CF12-settings-licenses-gemma` steps at 1.0, 2.0 and scrolled-to-end reach the Gemma notice through an ordinary tap (a tour-only `tapLicenseNotice` drill-in on a new `license-row-<file>` tag), with no `src/main` seam needed - correctly, since the screen is reachable in production already. The scrolled capture matters here: that text grew to the full Terms of Use and Prohibited Use Policy today, and whether it ends correctly at font scale 2.0 is a real question. | fixed |
| R-1130 | Some artboards were drawn with the wrong token's value, so the comparison validated a drawing that never matched the code | constitution VIII; design-guide; R-1089 | Found by the R-1089 builder while propagating the new token values across `design/canvas/`, and it undermines the evidence chain rather than any one screen. `States.dc.html` drew the UNKNOWN attribution dot in **`text/signal`'s** value rather than `marker/unknown`'s (fixed in place); several *N unidentified voices* lines and `Charts.dc.html`'s axis labels use `text/figure`'s or `text/signal`'s value where the built code uses `textLow` (not fixed - a blanket replace would have propagated the wrong colour further). The consequence is the one that matters: for those elements, a capture compared against its artboard was being judged against a drawing that did not encode the token system the code implements, so agreement proved nothing and disagreement would have been blamed on the screen. Needs a value-by-value audit of the three tokens across every board, not a find-and-replace. Note this is pre-existing drift that the token change merely exposed - it was equally wrong before. | design **FIXED** (`2e0afdae`, merged 2026-09-20), and done the slow way on purpose: every occurrence was justified against the Kotlin call site that renders that element, not swapped by value. 21 boards corrected - chart hour axes, level and timeline axes, the *N unidentified voices* lines, thread disclosure chevrons and the recent-search icon were all drawn in `text/signal` or `text/figure` where the code reads `textLow`; `Now-First.dc.html`'s flat baseline turned out to use `text/faint`, because `FirstSessionChartBaseline` deliberately does not reuse `ActivityPatternChart`. `design/design-guide.md` also had *recent-search icon* and *search-field placeholder* swapped between the two tokens against what the code does. The builder explicitly **left alone** every element that already used the right token but a pre-R-1089 value - that is a value refresh, not this row's wrong-token defect - and left `Editorial.dc.html` (superseded) and `Station-Pattern.dc.html`'s heat-map labels (no built code to justify against). Two findings split out as R-1135 and R-1136. | closed |
| R-1131 | A builder ran `gradlew --stop` and it cost another builder a full test run | AGENTS.md working agreement; constitution II | Disclosed by P34's builder without being asked, and the cost is measurable rather than theoretical: it ran `gradlew --stop` once while chasing a hung test process, and the R-1089 builder - working in a different worktree at the same time - independently reported one of its full-module runs dying with *Gradle build daemon has been stopped: stop command received*, then having to start over. That is the entire reason AGENTS.md forbids it, now with evidence attached rather than as a bare rule. The correct move, which the same builder used for every later case, is to kill the specific stuck `java.exe` by PID. Two improvements worth making so the rule does not depend on memory under pressure: say **why** in AGENTS.md rather than only *never*, and give builders the scoped recipe for killing one hung process, since the rule is most likely to be broken by someone who has a hung process and no sanctioned way out of it. Filed as process, not blame - the disclosure is what made it fixable. | process **CLOSED** (`1df20a8e`, merged 2026-09-20): AGENTS.md now says why the daemon must not be stopped - it is shared across every worktree, and this instance cost another builder a completed full-module run - and gives the scoped alternative of killing one PID. A rule with a stated reason and a sanctioned way out is the actual fix; *never* on its own is what gets reasoned around under pressure. | closed |
| R-1132 | No production code path ever creates a station, so the catalog is permanently empty on a device | FR-SPK-1, FR-SPK-10, FR-LEX-25..27; constitution I; R-1109, R-1124 | Disclosed by the R-1124 builder and verified independently by the lead: every `StationEntity(...)` construction in the tree is in `src/test` or `src/debug` - `Scenarios.kt`, `StationsFixtures.kt`, `TestFixtures.kt` and their peers - and nothing under any module's `src/main` inserts one. `StationIdentityDao` exists, is tested, and is referenced in `src/main` only by `OrtDatabase`'s own import. **This is the deepest instance yet of the pattern this register keeps recording**, and it invalidates more than the priors: the Stations screen and every station detail surface can only ever be populated by a seeded debug scenario, which is why they have always looked right in captures and would be empty in an operator's hands; `CatalogDao.getStation` - the read R-1124 just wired for the database-presence and recency priors - returns nothing on a real device; and a voiceprint has nothing to bind to. Compounding it, `conversation`'s real source `TransmissionEntity.stationId` is written only from `Attribution.stationId`, which is `null` for every `AMBIGUOUS` over, so after R-1110 that field is empty too. The unit is: decide where a station is born - first resolution above some bar, first correction, or an explicit operator action - and write it, with the same inspectability rule as everything else. Note this is not a regression introduced by P33 or R-1124; both merely made it visible. | halt **FIXED, awaiting device evidence** (`5519ed12`, merged 2026-09-20). Decision **D56** recorded in the spec with its traceability row, per the session lead's ruling while the product owner was unavailable: a station is born at Pass B closure for the top-ranked resolved callsign of any over that parsed a callsign and survived rejection (`AMBIGUOUS` or better, never a rejected or failed over), and by an operator correction, which always creates or rebinds. New `CatalogDao.recordStationObservation` is the first production write path `StationEntity` has ever had - it sets `firstHeardAt` once and never moves it, updates `lastHeardAt`, the count and the new `overCountsByAttributionState`, and **never touches `userName` or `notes`**, so an operator's own words cannot be overwritten by a machine observation. `DataPassBResultSink.record` calls it, gated on `state != UNKNOWN`, which already excludes rejected and failed overs since both resolve to `Attribution.unknown()`. `CorrectionDao.recordCorrection` calls an equivalent path unconditionally. Thirteen tests, each discriminated by hand. **Open until a device shows the Stations screen populated after a real unseeded session** - this is the row's whole point, and no device evidence exists yet. Two sub-paths deliberately left, see R-1134. | fixed |
| R-1133 | An inert hallucination control is computed but never persisted, so no dump can show it | FR-ASR-5, FR-ASR-6; AC-6; constitution I, VI; R-1121 | Reported by the R-1121 builder against its own work, and correctly left alone as outside its file ownership. `PassBOutcome.inertControls` now names every hallucination control that could not evaluate on a given pass, but nothing in `:pipeline` writes it: `PassBFingerprintBuilder` does not hash it and `DataPassBResultSink` does not store it. So the fix that made control 3's inertness *representable* has not yet made it *inspectable* - an operator or a debug dump still cannot tell that AC-6 ran on five controls rather than six for a particular over. Constitution VI wants the configuration a number was produced under to travel with it, and 'which controls were live' is exactly that. Small, well-bounded seam: hash it into the fingerprint and persist it beside the other pass provenance. | spec **CLOSED** (`f2398c38`, merged 2026-09-21), and the builder pushed back on this row's own wording, correctly. *Hash it into the fingerprint* cannot be taken literally: `configHash` is computed once at `PassBFactory.create()`, before any outcome exists, and is shared by every over that instance processes, so an outcome-varying value cannot live there - and hashing would defeat the row's own goal of a dump showing *which* controls were live. `inertControlsSignature` renders them as deterministic sorted plain text, persisted as `TransmissionEntity.inertControls` (schema v16, with a migration test) beside the rest of the pass provenance, none of which is hashed either. | closed |
| R-1134 | An undone correction leaves the station's over counts inflated | FR-SPK-10; constitution I, III; D56, R-1132 | Reported by the R-1132 builder against its own work and deliberately left rather than guessed at, which was the right call. A correction now always creates or rebinds a station and increments its counts, but `restoreAttribution` (undo) and `StationIdentityDao.splitVoiceprint` do not call the inverse - because the obvious inverse, decrementing, is a quiet deletion and constitution III forbids it. So after an operator corrects a callsign and then undoes it, the station keeps an observation it no longer has evidence for, and the counts FR-DIG-7 calls facts stop being re-derivable from the log (FR-DIG-8 says a fact that cannot be recomputed is not a fact). The likely answer is that the counts should be derived from the transmission rows rather than incremented in place, which makes undo automatic and satisfies FR-DIG-8 directly - but that is a design change worth doing deliberately, not a patch. | spec **CLOSED** (`f2398c38`, merged 2026-09-21) by removing the cause rather than patching the symptom: `overCountsByAttributionState` is now **derived** from the current transmission and candidate rows on every read, not incremented in place. So `restoreAttribution` and `splitVoiceprint` correct themselves with zero code changes to either, and FR-DIG-8 - a fact that cannot be recomputed from the log is not a fact - is satisfied directly. It uses `EXISTS` rather than `JOIN`, because a transmission can carry more than one historical rank-0 candidate row and a join fanned them out; discrimination caught that. Cost was measured before choosing, not guessed: ~2.6 ms at 5,000 transmissions, ~22 ms at 50,000, left uncached with the indices to add written down. **Known gap, documented in the entity's own KDoc**: `ActivityDao.listStations()` does not go through `getStation()` and still returns the raw placeholder for that column. | closed |
| R-1135 | A token's own doc comment disagrees with the call site it names | design-guide; R-1130 | Found while justifying every artboard element against its Kotlin call site. `OrtColors.kt`'s doc comment for `textSignal` claims it covers the *recent-search icon*, but `SearchScreen.kt:582` renders that icon with `textLow`. The board and the design guide were corrected to follow the executable code, as instructed, and the comment was deliberately not touched by a builder who owned only the boards. It is small, but it is the same class of defect as R-1130 itself: a stated reference that does not match what runs, which is what sends the next person looking in the wrong place. | design **CLOSED** (`65b3a9d2`, merged 2026-09-21): `OrtColors.kt`'s `textSignal` comment no longer claims the recent-search icon, and `textLow`'s does, matching `SearchScreen.kt:582` and the design guide. | closed |
| R-1136 | An unnamed legacy colour is still used for *last heard* figures | design-guide; R-1089, R-1130 | Also found during the R-1130 audit and correctly not folded into it. `oklch(0.56 0.008 250)` appears on the *last heard* figures in `Stations.dc.html` and `Frequency.dc.html`, and in `Rows.dc.html`'s S-meter column, but it is not the current or any prior value of `text/figure` or any other named token - it belongs to no token at all. Real drift against `text/figure`, but a different defect from drawing one token in another's value, so it was flagged rather than swept in. Note `Rows.dc.html`'s S-meter column has no built code either, which may mean the answer is to delete that part of the board rather than recolour it. | design **CLOSED** (`65b3a9d2`, merged 2026-09-21): the unnamed `oklch(0.56 0.008 250)` is retired from the *last heard* figures in `Stations.dc.html` and `Frequency.dc.html` (now `text/figure`, justified against `StationScreen.kt:232` and `FrequencyScreen.kt:389`) and from `Rows.dc.html`'s group-header icon (now `text/time`, per `Rows.kt:1211-1216`). The row's own description was corrected in passing: the S-meter column carries a *different* unnamed value (`0.50`), and it has real built code (`LogRowSignalText` -> `textLow`), so it was recoloured rather than deleted as the row had speculated. | closed |
| R-1137 | Voice matching is not wired in production, so a voice-scoped correction has no voice signal | FR-SPK-10; constitution I; R-1118 | Surfaced by the R-1118 builder while measuring how much the participant filter really dropped, and it is the wider finding: `RealCaptureService`'s segment-persist writes `voiceprintId = null` unconditionally, so no over ever carries a voiceprint and `:identity` remains an empty stub. Two consequences. The correction scope the UI offers as *every over by the same voice* has no voice signal behind it and falls back to matching the callsign text - a screen naming a mechanism the data cannot support, which is the pattern this register keeps recording. And a thread's roster, now that every over gets a slot, will mostly read as a run of `UNKNOWN` entries, because `voiceKey` falls back through a null station id **and** a null voiceprint id. The original 1.0 proposal already recommended killing `:identity` for 1.0 and relabelling the scope to *same callsign*, which is what it actually does; that relabelling is the cheap honest fix and is not yet done. | spec **CLOSED as stale** (lead reconciliation 2026-09-21). The builder dated the row against real history rather than reading the current code and guessing: this row was filed at `2078db52` on 2026-09-20 at 22:38, and commit `c9d2a599` (*P29: D45 relabel*) had already renamed `CorrectionScope.EVERY_OVER_SAME_VOICE` to `EVERY_OVER_SAME_CALLSIGN` everywhere and relabelled the Tier C radio to *Every over with the same callsign* at 00:00 that same day. `CorrectionSheetTest` already asserts the old string is gone. Every other place the voice-scoped wording could have survived - the correction sheet, `CorrectionPolling`, `TransmissionDetailContent`, `PropagatedScreen`, and the INFERRED-explanation sentences in `TransmissionDetailScreen`/`DetailViewState`/`ThreadViewData` - was checked and is already honestly gated. The underlying fact the row also states remains true and is not a defect of wording: `voiceprintId` is still unconditionally null and `:identity` is still an empty stub. Two adjacent overclaims it found are filed as R-1147. | closed |
| R-1138 | A builder used `git stash` after being told not to | AGENTS.md working agreement; R-1131 | Disclosed rather than hidden, and no harm resulted - the stash was tagged, applied by SHA and dropped - but the rule is unconditional and was restated in the brief. This is the second instruction-discipline finding of the day after R-1131's `gradlew --stop`, and the same remedy applies: AGENTS.md says *never `git stash`* without saying why or what to do instead, and a rule with no stated reason and no sanctioned alternative is the kind that gets reasoned around under pressure. Write the reason (a stash is invisible to the lead and to every other worktree, and a lost stash is unrecoverable work) and give the alternative (commit on the branch; it is cheap and it is visible). Filed as process, not blame. | process **CLOSED** (merged 2026-09-21): AGENTS.md now says why not to stash - a stash is invisible to the lead and to every other worktree, and one dropped by mistake is unrecoverable - and gives the alternative: commit on the branch, which is cheap and visible. Same remedy as R-1131, for the same reason. | closed |
| R-1139 | The new toast push channel has no producer | constitution I; R-1008 | Filed by the lead the moment R-1008 landed, because this is the exact shape the register keeps recording and it is cheapest to catch on the day it is created: `PushedToastChannel` is built, tested four ways and collected by `FailureHost` - and **nothing in the app calls `push()`**. The builder was right not to invent a call site: wiring the obvious one (`PropagatedScreen`'s *Undo all*) needs `CorrectionPolling.kt` and `TransmissionDetailContent.kt`, outside its ownership, and a closure built against a guessed result shape is precisely the stale-undo failure the unit was warned about. But an unused channel is indistinguishable from a missing feature to an operator, and R-1008's own complaint - no way to surface the result of an action taken on a screen you have left - remains true until a real action pushes one. Wire the first producer, and take the undo-by-value rule from R-1008's conclusion with it. | spec **CLOSED** (`0e99eab1`, merged 2026-09-21): `PropagatedScreen`'s *Done* is the first real caller of `PushedToastChannel.push()`, pushing *Corrected to <callsign> - N over(s) updated* with a working undo. **The undo is built by value, as R-1008's conclusion required**: the closure captures `outcome` - the immutable `PropagationOutcome` that this exact correction produced - and the application context, and never touches `transmissionId`, `destination` or `refresh`, all of which are bound to the screen being left. The builder also found the trap that would have made this fail silently in production: the producing composable's own `rememberCoroutineScope()` is cancelled the instant it leaves composition, which is exactly when the undo would be tapped, so a process-lifetime `undoScope` was added for the undo to launch on. Discriminated, and the undo-after-navigation case is driven through a real state toggle rather than a second `setContent`. | closed |
| R-1140 | `testPlayDebugUnitTest` silently excludes a test class | constitution II; R-1043 | Noticed by the R-1008 builder and worth keeping. `FailureHostTest` is isolated into its own `smokeTestPlayDebugUnitTest` task by `app/build.gradle.kts`, a deliberate mitigation for the Compose idle-poisoning described in R-1043 - but the consequence is that the ordinary `testPlayDebugUnitTest` a builder or a reviewer runs **silently excludes that class and says nothing**. Someone verifying a change to `FailureHost` with the obvious command gets a green result that never exercised the host's own test. The isolation is justified; its invisibility is not. Either name the smoke task in the ordinary task's output, or make the module's check depend on both so no one can run one without the other. | spec **CLOSED** (`a5268be2`, merged 2026-09-21): `testFullDebugUnitTest` now prints the 18 Compose-idle-poisoning classes it excluded and names the task that does run them, so a green result can no longer quietly mean *I did not look there*. The notice reads the smoke task's **own includes** rather than restating the list, because a second copy is how it would drift out of step with reality - the same failure as the silence it replaces. Verified by running an unrelated class and reading the output. | closed |
| R-1141 | The tier override in Settings has no effect on anything | FR-AST-3a; constitution I; R-1111 | Found by the R-1111 builder while confirming that the four independent tier computations agree - they do, and none of them can see this. `SettingsStore.tierOverrideName` is written by the Settings surface and **never read by `ShedController` or `AndroidShedSignals`**, so the operator's explicit choice of tier changes nothing: not the session row, not reprocessing, not the models screen. A control that appears to work and does not is worse than an absent one, and this is the same shape as the register's recurring built-but-not-connected finding, on a surface the operator touches directly. Pre-existing and out of R-1111's scope; note the fix has to decide what an override means against a shed level the device computes from real thermal and backlog pressure - an override that silently loses to shedding is no better than one that is ignored. | spec **FIXED** (`61bb724b`, merged 2026-09-21), and fixed at the one shared source of truth rather than in the four places that each compute tier independently: `ShedSignals.tierCapOrdinal()` is read by `AndroidShedSignals` from the same on-disk preference `SharedPreferencesSettingsStore` writes, and `ShedController.sample()` combines it with `maxOf`. **The design question this row posed is answered and argued, not assumed.** An override is a *ceiling on tier*, which is a *floor on shed level* - per FR-TIER-3's own wording, *override downward, to save battery or heat*. Combining with `maxOf` means it can add shedding but can never remove the automatic degradation FR-TIER-4 makes mandatory, so an operator cannot override their way past thermal or backlog pressure. FR-TIER-3's *upward, accepting the risk* half is not built - the UI offers only T0-T2 holds - and was left as an open question rather than quietly treated as decided. The existing *N of 3 - held at T1* line in Settings needed no change and is now honest, because `N` is finally fed by the capped level. Open only until a scenario confirms that line changes on a device. | fixed |
| R-1142 | A revision card claims a transmission was corrected whenever it has a station | FR-SPK-10; constitution I; R-1099 | Found by the R-1096 builder while verifying that R-1099 was stale, and correctly left alone as outside its owned files. `DetailRevisionsScreen.kt` builds the `withCorrection`/`INFERRED` attribution shape whenever a version carries a `stationId`, **without gating on `version.corrected`** - so an uncorrected transmission's revision cards misrepresent it as operator-corrected. That is the same duplicated-rule defect R-1099 was filed over, surviving in a fourth place after the other three were unified, and it is a Principle I breach in the one surface whose whole job is to show how a conclusion changed over time. Use the shared `correctedAttributionOrNull` here too rather than a fifth reconstruction. | spec **FIXED** (`61bb724b`, merged 2026-09-21): `VersionCard` was building `Attribution.unknown().withCorrection(stationId)` whenever `version.stationId != null`, regardless of `version.corrected`, so an uncorrected transmission's revision cards wore a correction marker. Now gated on the shared `correctedAttributionOrNull`, given a new `(stationId, corrected)` overload so this screen - which holds a view state rather than a full entity - can reuse the one rule instead of becoming the fifth reconstruction of it. Two cases, discriminated. Awaiting the capture: the lead needs a scenario with an **uncorrected, stationed** transmission's revisions, where the attribution row should now be absent, beside the existing corrected case. | fixed |
| R-1143 | Three screens gained a share kebab that no artboard draws | constitution VIII; FR-EXP-7; R-1096 | Flagged by the R-1096 builder rather than assumed acceptable, which was right. Fixing the share subject meant putting a share action on Digest, Thread Detail and Transmission Detail, using the `DrillInHeader` kebab those screens already carry - but **no artboard draws a kebab on any of the three**, so the next capture will deviate from its board by construction and constitution VIII has nothing to judge against. The affordance itself looks right (it reuses an existing pattern rather than introducing an icon), so the likely answer is that the three boards are out of date and should gain it - but that is a design decision to make deliberately and re-draw, not something a capture should silently absorb. | design **CLOSED, and the row's premise was wrong** (lead, 2026-09-21). Filed on a builder's report that no artboard drew the kebab on any of the three screens; the boards unit checked each one and found **Digest and Transmission Detail already draw it**, byte-identical to `Station.dc.html`'s reference pattern and present since the canvas's first commit. The tour capture agrees for Digest. Only `Thread-Detail.dc.html` lacks it, and that board does not draw the shared drill-in header at all - it draws a back icon with a live dot and an elapsed readout - so there is no kebab-shaped slot to add without redrawing the header. Split out as R-1145 rather than forced. | closed |
| R-1144 | Detail-Why is missing the per-candidate reasons and the bottom action bar its artboard draws | FR-UI-8; constitution VIII; R-1117 | Split from R-1117 once the screen was finally captured, so the remaining gap is recorded against the picture rather than a prediction. `overnight/D05-why.png` renders sections 1-4 correctly, but against `Detail-Why.dc.html` two things the board draws are absent: the per-candidate **reasons** (the board's own example is *needs an A the lattice did not hear*), which need derived data no current API returns, and the **bottom action bar**, which needs the same correction callbacks `MainDestination` builds inline. The R-1127 builder reported both rather than bodging them, which was right - the reasons in particular are the sentence that makes this screen worth opening, and a fabricated one would be worse than none. | design **PARTLY FIXED, and the unbuilt half is the important one** (`0e99eab1`, merged 2026-09-21). The **bottom action bar** is built: `DetailWhyScreen` takes nullable `onNotRight`/`onConfirm` and renders the board's own `ActionBar` only when both are supplied, wired only when the polled state is `Inferred` - the same gate `TransmissionDetailScreen`'s own bar uses, and the only case `Detail-Why.dc.html` ever draws it for. **The per-candidate reasons were deliberately not built, and the investigation is why.** The builder traced the board's own example - *needs an A the lattice did not hear* - to its source and found that `CallsignGrammar.slotDetailsFor` computes one `SlotDetail` list **per lattice, not per candidate** (its own doc comment says so), and `DataPassBResultSink.persistCandidates` stores that identical shared list under every candidate's row. So `CandidateInspectionViewState.slots` is byte-identical for K7LWH, KA7LWH and K7LVH, and a reason rendered from it **would be true by accident for the winner and fabricated for every runner-up** - on the one screen in the app whose entire purpose is explaining why a conclusion was reached. Declining to build it was the right call. Split out as R-1148. Awaiting the capture of the new action bar. | fixed |
| R-1145 | Thread Detail's artboard draws a header the screen does not have | constitution VIII; R-1096, R-1143 | Found while checking whether the three share-kebab screens had boards to match. `Thread-Detail.dc.html` draws a back icon styled near-white with a live dot and an elapsed-time readout - a `ScreenHeader`-like shape - while `ThreadDetailScreen.kt:72-78` renders the shared accent-green `DrillInHeader` with a back label and now a share kebab. These are different headers, not a missing detail, so no capture of this screen can be judged against this board until one of them changes. The board is the likelier candidate, since the code uses the pattern every other drill-in uses, but redrawing a header is a design decision and was deliberately not taken by a builder fixing colours. | design **CLOSED** (`784bcbb5`, merged 2026-09-21): `Thread-Detail.dc.html`'s header redrawn against `Station.dc.html`/`Digest.dc.html`'s shared pattern - 20 px accent-green chevron, *Threads* label, three-dot kebab - justified element by element against `ThreadDetailScreen.kt:72-78`, which renders `DrillInHeader(parentLabel = "Threads", onBack, onKebab = onShare, ...)` and nothing else. **The live dot and elapsed readout were dropped rather than relocated**, and the builder checked before deciding: a grep of `ThreadDetailScreen.kt` for `live|elapsed` matches nothing anywhere in the file, so the board was promising a fact the screen never shows - the same defect as R-1130, in the other direction. Verified by rendering the board and comparing it against the two it now follows. | closed |
| R-1146 | Four tour steps still assert the playback contract R-1006 deliberately changed | FR-UI-7; R-1006; constitution VIII | Found by the 2026-09-21 full tour, which failed four `overnight-live/C10-*` steps: *never found a node tagged `transport-bar-playback`* (three of them) and *never found a node tagged `waveform-glyph-pause`* (the fourth). These steps encode the pre-R-1006 behaviour, where playback continued after navigating away from an over. R-1006 fixed exactly that - a `DisposableEffect` at the transport-bar layer stops playback when the operator leaves the transmission - and its own closing note records the judgement that even a same-screen switch to a different over stops it. So `C10-playing-then-log` describes a state the app can no longer be in, and the steps are stale rather than the code being wrong. Decide what the tour should capture instead: the honest replacement is probably a capture of the transport bar *absent* after navigation, which is the new contract and currently has no capture at all. | process **CLOSED** (`8c36cc46`, merged 2026-09-21: `overnight-live/C10*` steps 4, ok 4, errors 0), **and the builder was right to reject this row's own suggested replacement.** The row proposed capturing the transport bar *absent* after navigation. That is false for this scenario: `overnight-live` is a genuinely capturing session, so once playback stops on leaving the transmission, `resolveTransportBarState` falls through to `TransportBarViewState.Live` rather than `Hidden` - the bar is still there, showing the live state instead of the playback one. The four steps now wait on the ordinary `live-bar` tag, confirmed on a device by a real screenshot of the Log screen showing the plain green *Live* bar after navigating back from playback. A capture asserting absence would have been a new false claim in place of the stale one. **2026-09-21, re-investigated after three steps still failed in the canonical run, and the lead's hypothesis was wrong.** The lead suspected the pause glyph never flipped back - a Principle I defect on a control the operator is actively using. It flips. Three independent proofs against unmodified production code: a scoped `-Only "overnight-live/C10*"` run passing 4/4 on a device, a new Robolectric case exercising a genuine second manual tap, and the observation that three of the four failures never reached the post-pause wait at all. **Every failure hit the *first* wait**, the initial play-glyph render - including `C10-playing-then-log`, whose path contains only one such wait. See R-1157 for what actually causes it. | closed |

And `ImproveScreens.kt` offers a checkbox reading **Match voices to stations heard here** which is inert - a control that appears to do something and does nothing, which is worse than its absence. Both are wording-and-gating fixes, not features: say *overs with no callsign* and remove or disable the checkbox until `:identity` exists. | spec **FIXED, awaiting the batch capture** (`acb0101d`, merged 2026-09-21). And it drew a finer distinction than the row asked for - the Now count mixes `UNKNOWN` and `AMBIGUOUS` overs, so *no callsign heard* would overclaim for the ambiguous half, where a candidate was heard and merely not confirmed; *not attributed to a station* is the only phrase true of both. | R-1149 | Captures from two emulators are not comparable, and nothing says so | constitution VIII; R-1116, R-1083 | Found by the R-1116 builder and diagnosed by the lead. Comparing a live scoped run on `emulator-5560` against the committed reference captured on `emulator-5554` reported **every screen changed by 7-19%**, from a constant ~48 px vertical shift - reproduced even on a screen with no seeded date or time content, so not a text difference. The cause is that the two emulators were not in the same state: `wm size` on 5554 reports *Override size: 1260x2772* while 5560 reports only its physical *1080x2400*. The canonical reference is captured at the override; a run on an emulator without it is a different geometry, and the comparison is meaningless rather than merely noisy. **Why this matters beyond one run:** `diff.py` was just made able to see a one-line text change, which is exactly the sensitivity that turns a geometry mismatch into 19% noise and buries the real signal. Any future reviewer comparing on a second emulator - which is the normal way to parallelise this work - would get a wall of false positives and learn to ignore them. The tooling should refuse rather than report: the manifest already carries `width` and `height` per entry, so `diff.py` can compare those and fail loudly when two runs disagree, the same shape as its existing `runId`/`apkHash` guard. `install.ps1`/`tour.ps1` should also assert the override is set rather than assuming it. Note the same builder separately observed the *then-committed* manifest carrying three `apkHash` values and no `runId`s across 219 entries - that was the pre-tour state, and the lead verified the manifest at HEAD is coherent: 319 entries, one `runId`, one `apkHash` (`3e4cb83`), 7 `ok=false` matching the 7 recorded failures. | tooling **CLOSED** (`784bcbb5`, merged 2026-09-21), and the fix rests on a sharper observation than the row made. `diff.py` already had a `DimensionMismatch` check, and it could never have caught this: a `wm size` override shifts content **within a same-sized canvas** rather than resizing it, so the 5554-override-vs-5560-physical pair produces screenshots of **identical pixel dimensions**. The manifest's own `width`/`height` is the only signal there is. Two guards added, both refusing before a single pixel is compared (exit 4): `check_manifest_geometry` for self-consistency within a run, excluding failed entries since a failure stamps `width=0, height=0`; and `check_geometry_agreement` across runs - deliberately a different shape from the `runId`/`apkHash` guard, because two runs are *expected* to disagree on those and never allowed to disagree on geometry. The discriminating test is the one that matters: before and after agreeing on run id and apk hash, with identical-pixel-dimension screenshots, differing only in manifest geometry - which returned `rc==0` and *unchanged: 1* before the fix. `tour.ps1` also now throws if `adb shell wm size` reports no override line, which is the upstream half. 30 tests pass. Scope stated rather than glossed: a `--before` git ref is not covered, only a directory with its own manifest, which is the shape the real defect took. | closed |
