| R-1161 | Setup loops forever on Running overnight, and the exit needs a capture the loop prevents | AC-189; constitution IV; R-1104; NFR-8 | **Device field report, 2026-09-22, operator, verbatim:** *"after finishing the setup, i get dropped back into the running overnight screen and it loops when i click skip for now, i cant exit it. any button i press just loops back to the running overnight page."* Reproduced by reading the code; the trace is exact and the operator's *any button* is literally true. **Root cause: `SetupActivity.reconcileOvernightSurvival` (`SetupActivity.kt:396`) resets the very flag that is the step's only exit, and the condition that would stop it resetting can only become true after a capture that this loop prevents.** The cycle: `onStartCapture` (`SetupActivity.kt:983`) sets `setupComplete` and hands back; `MainActivity.route` (`MainActivity.kt:81`) sees setup complete but `overnightSurvivalStillUnproven` (`MainActivity.kt:110`) true, so it relaunches `SetupActivity` and finishes itself; `SetupActivity.onCreate` calls `reconcileOvernightSurvival` (`:310`), which - survival still unproven - sets `overnightStepSeen = false`; `stepFor` (`SetupStateMachine.kt:123`) therefore returns `OVERNIGHT`; *both* of that screen's buttons (`onSkipOvernight` `:677`, `onOpenBatterySetting` `:673`) set the flag back to `true` and call `refreshStep`, which now finds every gate clear, returns `null`, and hands back to `MainActivity` - step 2 again, forever. **`hasProvenSurvival()` requires a real, cleanly ended session of at least `OVERNIGHT_SURVIVAL_THRESHOLD_MILLIS`, and the only way to start one is `MainActivity.startCaptureAndShowStatus()`, which the loop never reaches.** There is no escape from inside the app on a fresh install. **Why it was not caught, and it is worth being precise about this.** The reset is not careless - `reconcileOvernightSurvival`'s own doc comment (`SetupActivity.kt:376-380`) argues the case explicitly: resetting the flag rather than the gate means the two buttons *"still only need to fire once per launch ... with no risk of looping back to `SetupStep.OVERNIGHT` again within the same launch"*. That reasoning is **true within a launch and false across launches**, and `handBackToMainActivity` (`:462`) plus `MainActivity`'s own `finish()` make every trip a new launch. R-1104 is what closed the circuit: before it, only an operator who happened to re-enter Setup hit the reset, so it read as a nag; R-1104 added the identical check to `MainActivity`'s fast path - correctly, for the gap it names - and thereby made the nag unconditional and the loop total. Neither change was wrong on its own; the defect is in their composition, which is why no single unit test sees it. **The deeper design fault, which the fix must address rather than patch.** `OVERNIGHT` is a setup gate whose exit condition is evidence that by construction cannot exist during setup. AC-189 is right that only heartbeat evidence may prove survival and that `isIgnoringBatteryOptimizations()` must never be trusted (constitution IV, ColorOS) - but that makes overnight survival a **post-capture** fact, not a pre-capture gate. Independent external research run for this session agrees and is worth recording: no consumer app that depends on background survival asks for the battery exemption during onboarding - Life360 and Strava both surface it as an after-the-fact remedy once tracking has actually failed, and the dontkillmyapp position (Urbandroid, who ship Sleep as Android) is that on OEM skins the generic exemption does not cover auto-start or the vendor sleep list at all, so asking for it up front buys a yes that does not fix the problem. **Fix direction: remove `OVERNIGHT` from the setup gate ladder entirely and surface it as a *Keep capture running* prompt triggered by the first missed heartbeat**, which both ends the deadlock structurally and matches what AC-189's own evidence rule implies. Any fix that merely stops the reset leaves a step that asks for something before it can be verified. **Discriminating test** (this is the shape that fails before and passes after, and its absence is the coverage gap): `SetupStateMachineTest` today tests `stepFor` as a pure function over a snapshot and never exercises the `MainActivity` -> `SetupActivity` -> `MainActivity` round trip, so no test can see a two-activity cycle. The test must assert that an operator with `setupComplete = true` and `overnightSurvivalProven = false` reaches capture - i.e. that routing terminates - rather than asserting any individual `stepFor` result. **Two findings added after the row was first filed, from an independent trace that reproduced the same cycle.** First, and it changes what the fix is allowed to do: **AC-189 (`spec/functional-spec.md:3250-3252`) only requires that the step *"reappears on every relevant subsequent launch"* - it never says capture is blocked until survival is proven.** R-1104 over-implements its own acceptance criterion, so removing the refusal costs no AC coverage; the nag may stay, the block may not. Second, the trapped screen has **no exit of any kind**: `OvernightScreen` passes `onBack = null` (`OvernightScreen.kt:37`), and because a cold resume calls `refreshStep(pushCurrent = false)` the `backStack` is empty, so `SetupActivity.onBack` (`:472-475`) returns without doing anything - and both `SetupActivity` and `ReaderActivity` are `exported="false"` (`AndroidManifest.xml:56-80`), so `MainActivity` is the only entry point and there is genuinely no way out but uninstalling. That is contributory rather than independent - fixing the deadlock ends the report - but see R-1162. Also pinned down: the exit evidence needs a session of at least 15 minutes ending in `TerminationReason.USER` (`OvernightSurvival.kt:40`, `:45`, `:52-56`), and the cycle became total in `2026-09-20 (m-a11y-manifest: R-1108/R-1106/R-1104)` (`CHANGELOG.md:2380-2429`) - the reset at `:396` was pre-existing and benign until R-1104 closed the circuit, so **neither change was wrong alone and the defect lives only in their composition**. **CLOSED structurally, which is what this row asked for** (P39 `6a105634` + the homes branch `ee1a58d8`). The earlier fix stopped the loop; this removes the shape that allowed it. `stepFor` never returns `OVERNIGHT` any more - the battery ask is not a setup gate at all - and its new home is a *Keep capture running* banner raised by two heartbeat-trail rows more than 35s apart. That trigger cannot fire before a capture has run, because the trail cannot hold two rows before one has, which is the property that makes the deadlock unreconstructable rather than merely fixed. `isIgnoringBatteryOptimizations()` is not the trigger, not a guard and not a suppressor. AC-199 is pinned two ways: structurally, the presentation is absent from `TAKEOVER_PRESENTATIONS`; behaviourally, the lead read `Start capture` fully reachable beneath the banner in `scratchpad/p39-captures/keep-running/F24-keep-running-now.png`. | app | closed |
| R-1162 | A setup step that can reappear after setup is complete offers no way back | R-1161; constitution IV; AC-189 | Split out of R-1161 so the deadlock fix is not confused with the affordance it exposed. `OvernightScreen` is rendered with `onBack = null` (`OvernightScreen.kt:37`), which is defensible for a step walked once mid-sequence - there is nowhere sensible to go back *to* on a fresh install. It stops being defensible the moment the same step can be shown again **after** setup has completed, which is exactly what `reconcileOvernightSurvival`'s reset and R-1104's fast-path re-ask both do: the operator is then standing on a screen they reached from a working app, with no back, no skip that sticks, and no exit. On a cold resume `refreshStep(pushCurrent = false)` deliberately pushes nothing (`SetupActivity.kt:445`, and its kdoc `:439-444` is right that it must not fabricate a back-stack entry the operator never navigated through), so `onBack` (`:472-475`) finds an empty `backStack` and no-ops. The rule this row asserts: **any step reachable once `setupComplete` is true must render an exit that returns to the app, not only a forward action.** Note this generalises past `OVERNIGHT` - it applies to every step `SetupStateMachine.stepFor` can return on a post-completion re-entry, and that set should be enumerated rather than assumed to be one screen. **Fix merged (`53365f99`, merge `0751bbe8`); row stays open on evidence.** The exit shipped as a third bottom action, *Back to the app*, rendered only when `store.setupComplete` - correct, because on a genuine first run there is nowhere to return to and an exit there would itself loop. **That is exactly why the scoped run of 2026-09-22 could not capture it**: `setup-verified/S08-battery` seeds a first run, so the frame shows the two original actions and the new one is legitimately absent. The lead confirmed from that capture what it *can* show - R-1172 is fixed, the hardcoded *Not yet exempt* assertion is gone and the row now reads *Whatever you choose here, it never blocks capture* - and confirmed that the exit itself has **no tour coverage at any font scale**. Needed before this row closes: a step that seeds `setupComplete` and re-enters at OVERNIGHT, captured at 1.0 and 2.0. Note the scaffold pins the action block, so a third action at 2.0 is the growth R-123/R-341/R-360 are scarred by and is the specific thing to look at. A Robolectric bounds test asserting all three actions sit inside the screen at `w390dp-h844dp-420dpi` exists and is not that evidence. | app | open |
| R-1163 | Two test suites each seeded past the deadlock and wrote down why | R-1161; constitution II, VIII | The reason R-1161 shipped is not that it was subtle in the code - it is that **both suites that could have caught it had already met the mechanism, worked around it in a fixture, and documented the workaround as an inconvenience.** `MainActivityTest.kt:109-116`: `markSetupComplete` seeds `KEY_OVERNIGHT_SURVIVAL_PROVEN = true`, commented *"without this, `overnightSurvivalStillUnproven` would now route every one of them to SetupActivity"*. `SetupActivityTest.kt:388-395`: the identical seed, commented *"without this, `reconcileOvernightSurvival()` would reset `KEY_OVERNIGHT_SEEN` back to false"*. Each comment describes one half of the cycle accurately. Neither author was in a position to see the other half, and no test drives `MainActivity -> SetupActivity -> MainActivity` as one sequence, so the composition was never executed anywhere. Worse, `SetupActivityTest.kt:1508-1527` is named `AC_189 skipping Overnight again within the same launch hands back to MainActivity, no loop` - its kdoc (`:1497-1507`) correctly scopes *no loop* to the intra-activity case, but the name reads as a general guarantee, and **a green test asserting "no loop" is precisely what made the inter-activity loop invisible**. This is the same class of finding as R-1140 and R-1160: evidence that proves something narrower than it appears to. The standing lesson worth extracting: **when a fixture has to seed a flag to stop the app routing somewhere, that is a finding about the app, not a property of the fixture** - the seed should carry a register id or a comment saying which real-world state it is standing in for, and if no real operator can reach that state, the test is asserting over a state that does not exist. | process | open |
| R-1164 | The Welcome screen makes the one privacy claim the spec forbids, and it ships today | FR-ANL-14; constitution I, V; D42; AC-180 | Found while auditing onboarding copy; it is live in the build the operator is testing right now, which is why it leads this batch. `WelcomeScreen.kt:143-144` reads *"Transcription, callsign resolution and the log all run here. No account, no upload, no network in the capture path - ever."* **FR-ANL-14 (`spec/functional-spec.md:1841-1844`) says a bare no-upload claim SHALL NOT be made**, and this build contradicts the sentence three ways: a contribution channel, a field-report channel, and analytics tier 3 - which this very flow offers the operator six screens later at `AnalyticsConsentScreen.kt:96`. The screen promises never, and then asks. The constitution names the one permitted wording verbatim and it must replace this line rather than simply deleting it, because Welcome is where the promise belongs: *Your audio is processed only on your phone and is never uploaded unless you choose to share it.* Note the defect is the absolute *ever*, not the sentiment - *no network in the capture path* is true and enforced by `dependencyRules`. Keep that half. | app | open |
| R-1165 | Two screens still promise voiceprints never leave the device, which D38 made false | FR-SPK-20; FR-OBS-9; FR-OBS-10; D38; constitution I, V; R-1164 | Same audit as R-1164 and the same severity: shipping now. `WelcomeScreen.kt:254-256` (inside the *What is captured* sheet) and `SettingsAboutScreen.kt:64` both state that *"Voiceprints, the names you give stations, what this phone has learned about who is around when, and your location never leave it - not in an export, a contribution, a diagnostic bundle or a backup."* **The voiceprint half stopped being true when the field-report channel landed** (D37/D38, constitution 1.3.0, now 2.0.0 at `constitution.md:302-306`): voiceprints and embeddings may leave by that one channel - per-category, defaulting off, named by file and real size before every upload, and refused against a public destination unless a visible Settings switch has been explicitly turned off. The rest of the sentence is still exactly true and must survive: user-supplied names, station knowledge and precise location leave in no tier and no channel. So this is a **surgical correction, not a deletion** - split the absolute claim from the one carved-out exception rather than weakening the whole line. This is the second-order cost of a constitution amendment: the prose that quoted the old principle was not swept, and two screens have been repeating a superseded promise since. | app | open |
| R-1166 | First-run setup is eleven to twenty screens where four would do | AC-166; AC-180; AC-189; FR-CAP-8; constitution I; R-1161, R-1162 | The operator's own words after running it on the device: *"the setup feels to complicated as well, simplify it to a smaller number of pages. dont ask the frequency for onboarding, drop the user into the capture mode dialog after askin gbasic questions. simplify the language across onboarding to just focus on what the app is asking for, dont use all the mumbo jumbo on 'on this device only etc'."* Measured rather than estimated: a brand-new user on API 33+ with everything bundled sees **12 screens** (Welcome, Jurisdiction, Mode, Microphone, Notifications, Input, Verify, Level, Overnight, Radio, Analytics consent, Ready); the worst case reaches **20**, every member of `SetupStep` (`SetupStep.kt:44-65`) being reachable in one run. Five have no skip predicate at all - Mode, Input, Verify, Level, Overnight. **The step audit, with the test being: can the app start capturing correctly in the next minute without this answer?** MUST ASK NOW: microphone permission (`stepFor` genuinely gates it, `SetupStateMachine.kt:117`); input and verify together (constitution IV - *a route that is not the selected device halts capture* - this is the product); level; models, but only when something is actually missing, which `stepFor:131` already scopes correctly. CAN DEFER to a contextual prompt or Settings: jurisdiction (one bool nothing else reads), notifications (skipping gates nothing, `SetupActivity.kt:570-574` - ask at first capture start), overnight (see R-1161 - it cannot be verified before capture, so it cannot be a pre-capture gate), the whole radio/rig branch (no rig module is built at all, `RadioUsbScreen.kt:22-27`), analytics consent (never gates, `SetupStep.kt:30-32`; tier 1 is on regardless). **Target shape**, which is also what the external research independently converged on: capture mode as a dialog over the empty log rather than a screen - that is the operator's own *drop the user into the capture mode dialog* - then the microphone ask fired directly from that choice with no explainer screen, then one full screen carrying input, the verify checklist expanding in place, and the level meter beneath it, then words on screen. Everything else becomes contextual. Published guidance puts the drop-off at roughly 15% per screen past five and measures best-in-class by time-to-value, not completeness. **This row does not stand alone**: R-1161 (the loop), R-1162 (no exits), R-1164/R-1165 (false copy), R-1167 (frequency), R-1170 (disabled buttons and dead ends) are all findings inside this flow, and the redesign should close them together rather than one at a time. Note the redesign needs spec amendments, not just code: AC-166, AC-180 and AC-189 each pin a step to first-run setup, and deferring them changes what those ACs assert. **CLOSED on captures the lead judged** (P39, `6a105634`, merged `e839084e`). A first run is now **Welcome -> Mode -> Listen -> Ready**, four screens, with Models appearing only when a download is owed. Verified from `scratchpad/p39-captures` (emulator-5558, 1260x2772 @ 420dpi, 43 steps, 43 ok): Welcome carries one title, the FR-ANL-14 sentence once and two acknowledgement rows; Mode reads `1 of 3` with three plain-language rows and no Continue; Listen reads `2 of 3` and carries the route list, the route check and the level in one screen. The mode jargon the critique named is gone - *"a handheld near the phone - room audio - frequency by hand"* is now *"the phone listens to a handheld or speaker nearby"*. **What this closure does NOT cover, stated rather than glossed:** the artboards were redrawn by the same builder in the same change, so a capture agreeing with its board proves the two agree with each other, not that either is right. What the captures independently establish is the screen *count*, the copy, and that each screen renders coherently at 1.0 and 2.0. See R-1185 and R-1186 for what they showed that is still wrong. | app | closed |
| R-1167 | The frequency is demanded on one path and never asked on the path right beside it | R-344; FR-RIG-*; constitution I; R-1166 | The operator asked for the frequency question to leave onboarding. Checking whether its answer is even used turned up something worse than a redundant question: **the flow is internally inconsistent about whether it is required at all.** `SetupStep.RADIO_USB` (`RadioUsbScreen.kt:48`) asks for MHz with `Continue` disabled until the text parses (`:50`, `:60`) - a hard requirement. The bottom action on the *previous* screen, *Not now - you can connect one later from Settings* (`RadioScreen.kt:75` -> `onRadioNotNow`, `SetupActivity.kt:746-753`), sets `radioChoice = NONE` and advances with `manualFrequencyHz` still null, and capture then runs perfectly well - S12 simply reads *No radio, frequency by hand*. So one tap demands a number the adjacent tap proves optional. R-344 fixed this for one path and not the other. **The answer is genuinely used** - do not delete the field, only the onboarding prompt: `SetupCaptureConfigurationAdapter.kt:46` -> `CaptureConfiguration.manualFrequencyHz` -> `SharedPreferencesCaptureConfigurationStore` -> read by `RealCaptureService` at session start (proven by `RealCaptureServiceManualFrequencyTest.kt:73`), and read back for display at `SettingsPolling.kt:425` and `ReadyScreen.kt:480`. It belongs in the log header, editable in place, where the operator can see what it is labelling. **And it has no visual evidence at all**: S10 is the one setup step with no tour step at any font scale, so whatever it looks like now has never been captured or compared with `Setup-Rig-Usb.dc.html`. **CLOSED** (P39 `6a105634` + the homes branch `ee1a58d8`, merges `e839084e`/`7fb8900d`). The prompt is out of onboarding and the value lives in the log header, editable in place, reading the store capture actually consumes. **The far more dangerous half of this row was found only because the two halves were built in parallel**: `SetupCaptureConfigurationAdapter` sourced `manualFrequencyHz` from `SetupStore`, so once setup stopped writing that field, any later trip through setup would have overwritten a log-header frequency with `null` - silent, no error, every subsequent over unlabelled. Fixed properly rather than patched: `SetupStore.manualFrequencyHz` is **deleted** (one fact in two stores is how they drifted), and the adapter now takes the value as a parameter **with no default**, so a caller who forgets it fails to compile instead of silently reintroducing the loss. Pinned by `AC_202 re-entering setup after a log-header frequency was set leaves that value intact`. See R-1181 for what the capture of the new header showed. | app | closed |
| R-1168 | There is no gain control, the design intent promised one, and three screens promise there never will be | FR-CAP-*; constitution I; R-1166 | The operator asked to *"allow the user to change the gain of the mic"* during onboarding. The honest state of the code, because this needs deciding rather than just building: **no gain, AGC or input-level control exists anywhere in any module** - no `setVolume`, no multiply, no `AutomaticGainControl`, `NoiseSuppressor` or `AcousticEchoCanceler` reference at all. Measurement, by contrast, is thorough and good: `LevelMeter.kt:41-163` computes peak and RMS dBFS, a 10 s noise floor, clip flags and a 60-entry history. Three things pull in different directions and the decision must face all three. (1) **`design/design-intent.md:77` already specifies S07 as "Level slider. `Test` arms the meter"** - so a control was designed and never built, and `Setup-Level.dc.html` has no slider either, meaning the artboard silently agreed with the code rather than the intent. (2) **Shipped copy in three places promises the opposite**: `LevelScreen.kt:104` and, most explicitly, `SettingsCaptureScreen.kt:141` - *"it never adjusts gain on the way in, so what is retained is what the radio put out"*. Any gain control makes that sentence false and it must be rewritten in the same change, not left to rot (constitution I). (3) **The engineering case is real but narrow.** Android exposes no input-gain API and a USB adapter's ADC gain is not reachable, so this can only ever be a software multiply on PCM the ADC already quantised: noise rises with signal, the `LevelMeter` noise floor tracks it exactly, and it cannot un-clip an input already pinned at full scale. It genuinely helps in one case - an adapter so quiet that speech peaks near -45 dBFS, wasting half the word - but that is an input S07 already refuses at `TOO_QUIET_BELOW_DBFS = -24.0`. **Judged: build it, at one specific seam, and nowhere else.** `AndroidAudioIo.read()` (`AndroidAudioIo.kt:185-188`), saturating, is the only point both the capture path and the onboarding meter cross - `RealLevelCheck` opens the device itself (`SetupActivity.kt:1315-1322`) and never touches `AudioRecordSource`, so a multiply applied at `AudioRecordSource.kt:174` or `:194` would leave the slider the operator is watching completely dead during first run. Applying it in `read()` also means `LevelMeter.onFrame` measures post-gain audio, so gain-induced clipping is reported honestly instead of hidden. It must NOT go through `CaptureConfigurationStore`, which freezes configuration at session start by design (AC-131) and would produce a slider that moves while the meter does not. See R-1169 for the change that is likely to matter more. | app | open |
| R-1169 | The spec picked an unprocessed audio source years ago and the code still asks for the processed one | technical-design §5.1; constitution I; R-1168 | Found while assessing R-1168, and it is probably the real fix behind the operator's complaint rather than the slider. `spec/technical-design.md:360-364` specifies: `MediaRecorder.AudioSource.UNPROCESSED` when `AudioManager.getProperty(PROPERTY_SUPPORT_AUDIO_SOURCE_UNPROCESSED)` reports it, falling back to `VOICE_RECOGNITION`, and *explicitly not* `MIC` with effects, *"which applies AGC and noise suppression that fight the ASR"*. **`AndroidAudioIo.kt:112` hardcodes `VOICE_RECOGNITION` unconditionally.** Grepping `UNPROCESSED` across the whole repository returns two hits, both inside that spec paragraph, none in code. The spec also requires that which source was actually obtained be recorded on the session; that is not recorded either, so there is currently no way to tell from a capture which processing the OEM applied to it. **Why this outranks the gain slider**: `VOICE_RECOGNITION` is an OEM-tuned path that on many skins applies its own automatic gain and noise suppression - on ColorOS in particular - so the operator may be fighting a vendor AGC that is itself the cause of a level that will not sit still. A multiply stacked on top of a hidden AGC is two controls fighting. This costs one method, is already specified, needs no copy retraction, and makes the recorded audio genuinely different rather than merely louder. Doing this first also gives R-1168 a clean signal to work against. | app | open |
| R-1170 | Five setup screens disable Continue for validation, and two of them have no way out at all | constitution I, IV; FR-A11Y-*; R-1161, R-1162, R-1166 | The operator asked to *"keep the continue button lit up at all times"*, and the published argument is on their side rather than merely being a preference. A disabled button gives no feedback about what is wrong, is often below contrast minimums, and - the accessibility point that settles it - `disabled` removes the control from the focus order, so a screen-reader user cannot find the thing they are blocked by (Adam Silver; Adrian Roselli, *Don't Disable Form Controls*; GOV.UK's own practice is to submit and then explain). The defensible exception is narrow and worth keeping: an **in-flight action**, to prevent double submission - not validation state. Current `enabled =` gates: `InputScreen.kt:61` (`selectedId != null`), `VerifyScreen.kt:163` (route check passed), `LevelScreen.kt:52` (`band == IN_BAND`), `RigTransportScreen.kt:86` (`selected != null`), `RigBluetoothScreen.kt:98` (link verified), `ModelsSetupScreen.kt:66` (every download finished), `RadioUsbScreen.kt:60` (frequency parses). **Rule to apply: never disable for validation - keep the button lit, validate on tap, and say what is missing. Disable only while an action is actually running.** **Two of these are worse than annoying, they are dead ends.** `LevelScreen` disables Continue unless the band is green *and* passes `onBack = null` (`:47`) - a quiet band at two in the morning strands the operator with no forward, no back and no later. `ModelsSetupScreen` disables Continue until every download completes, also with no back (`:60`) - with no network that is terminal. Both need an explicit escape (*Set this later*) that records the unresolved state on READY rather than blocking. Seven steps in the back half of the flow pass `onBack = null` in total - MODE, JURISDICTION, LEVEL, OVERNIGHT, RADIO, MODELS, ANALYTICS, READY - which is the structural reason R-1161's loop had no manual escape. **First half landed** (`41f7f664`, merged `a3df0e4d`): six screens now keep the primary lit and refuse on tap with a specific assertive live-region message, and ten existing `assertIsNotEnabled()` assertions were **inverted rather than deleted** - each replaced with the strictly stronger form of clicking the live button and asserting the callback never fired. The one disabled state kept is the genuinely in-flight download, which is the exception this row always allowed. Constitution IV is intact and pinned: an unverified route and an unverified rig link both still refuse. **Second half - the lead's ruling, asked for by the builder and recorded here rather than left to the next reader.** The escapes are NOT a *Skip*. On `LevelScreen` the action is **Set the level later**, recording an unresolved level that READY already renders amber and that should also stand as a warning in the log header - because a quiet band at two in the morning is indistinguishable from a dead route, and the operator is the only one who can tell those apart. On `ModelsSetupScreen` it is **Continue without these models**, landing on a tier-reduced configuration that is *named on every session it produces* rather than silently degraded (constitution I: a weaker phone knows less, it is never more wrong). Both need `SetupStateMachine` to carry an **unresolved-but-acknowledged** state distinct from not-yet-reached, which is exactly why they were correctly out of the first unit. **And the models escape needs AC-188 amended, not merely implemented**: that criterion today asserts setup cannot reach READY with a model missing, and an acknowledged-degraded path is a different assertion, not a relaxation of the same one. Route this with P39. | app | open |
| R-1171 | Three capture switches in Settings are persisted, rendered, and read by nobody | constitution I; R-1132, R-1139, R-1141 | Found while searching for an existing gain control. `SettingsStore.kt:72-74` declares `noiseReductionEnabled` (default true), `bandPassFilterEnabled` and `levelWarnEnabled`; all three are persisted with real keys (`:141-143`), rendered as switches (`SettingsCaptureScreen.kt:121-132`) and written on toggle (`SettingsContent.kt:567-575`) - and **nothing in `:pipeline`, `:capture-*`, `:segment` or `:asr-*` ever reads any of them.** The operator can turn noise reduction off and nothing happens; it was never on. This is the built-but-never-connected pattern this register has now recorded eight times (R-1132, R-1139, R-1141, R-1137, R-1133 among them), and it matters here beyond its own severity: it is the **existing precedent for exactly the failure R-1168 must avoid**, so a gain slider added without a consumer would be the fourth switch on the same screen that does nothing. Either wire them or delete them, and say which in the changelog - never leave a third state where the UI asserts a capability the pipeline does not have. | app | open |
| R-1172 | Two setup screens show invented state as though it were real | constitution I; R-1166 | Two separate instances of a screen presenting fabricated specifics with the authority of live data, which is the defect class Principle I exists for. **First**, `OvernightScreen.kt:78` renders a status row reading *"Not yet exempt - this never blocks capture"* that is **hardcoded and never recomputed**: it says *not yet exempt* on a device that is already exempt, which is a false report about the exact fact the screen exists to establish. (Reading the exemption flag honestly is its own problem - constitution IV says `isIgnoringBatteryOptimizations()` lies on ColorOS - so the fix may well be to state nothing rather than to state it live; what is not defensible is asserting a specific wrong value.) **Second**, `NotificationsScreen.kt:55-64` draws a preview notification populated with invented particulars: *"6:42"*, *"412 overs"*, *"145.230 and 146.960 tier 3 38.2 GB used"*. It is a screenshot of a session that never happened, shown to an operator who has not captured anything, and the 38.2 GB figure in particular is the sort of number a reader will take as a real measurement of their own phone. A preview is defensible; fabricated readings inside it are not - use obviously generic placeholders or draw the chrome empty. **CLOSED, both instances** (the 2026-09-22 merge and P39). The hardcoded *Not yet exempt* row was replaced with a clause that is actually true whatever the OS reports; the fabricated notification preview - *"6:42"*, *"412 overs"*, *"145.230 and 146.960 - tier 3 - 38.2 GB used"* - is gone with `NotificationsScreen` itself, which P39 deleted when the notification ask moved to first capture start. Worth recording that the second instance was resolved by **deleting the screen for an unrelated reason**, not by fixing the fabricated figures: had the notifications step survived the restructure, that invented 38.2 GB would still be shipping. | app | closed |
| R-1173 | The superseded privacy claim is repeated in seven more places, including the repo headline | FR-ANL-14; FR-SPK-20; FR-PLT-5; D37; D38; D42; constitution I, V; R-1164, R-1165 | Reported by the R-1164/R-1165 builder as deliberately outside its ownership, and the list is worth keeping whole rather than rediscovering one surface at a time. Each of these still carries a claim that D37/D38/D42 superseded. **`README.md:4` - *entirely offline* - is the repo's own headline and by far the most public of them.** In the app: `StationIdentityScreen.kt:103` and `:483-485` (the voiceprint absolute, a third and fourth time), `SettingsExportScreen.kt:73-74` (*Never exported, by any option: voiceprints...*), and `SettingsViewData.kt:362-363`, whose kdoc calls its list *Constitution V's closed list, verbatim* - V no longer reads that way, so the comment now misdescribes the very thing it points at. In `:data`: `CatalogEntities.kt:183`, `/** Never leaves the device (D28, FR-SPK-20). */` on the embedding field. In the record: `results/audit-2026-09-07.md:539` has *embeddings never leave* written down as settled. Artboards carrying the same words: `Station-Identity.dc.html:33` and `:88`, `Settings-Export.dc.html:99`, `Settings.dc.html:65`. **The sharp detail, and it says where the drift is**: `docs/privacy-policy.md` and `docs/play-data-safety.md` **already state the correct qualified form**. So this is not a product that has not decided what is true - the outward-facing legal documents are right and the app's own UI and README are the ones out of line with them, which is the worse direction for the claim to be wrong in. **Correcting these is not a find-and-replace.** R-1164/R-1165's own fix established the shape: FR-SPK-20 permits a voiceprint to leave for *the operator's own device-to-device transfer* and requires the app to say so, and `SettingsBackupScreen.kt:112-114` already tells the operator voiceprints travel in a backup - so a replacement reading *only by field report* would ship a third false claim and leave two screens contradicting each other. The excluded case is a **cloud** backup (FR-PLT-5), not a backup. Reuse `OfflinePromiseCopy.kt`, the single shared source R-1165's fix introduced, rather than writing an eighth independent wording. | app | open |
| R-1174 | A smoke-test class fails once and passes alone, and nobody can say why | R-1140; constitution VIII | Reported by the R-1161 builder **against a suite it had no reason to touch**, which is the only reason it is on the record at all. Its first `:app:smokeTestFullDebugUnitTest` run failed on `NavSeedTest.openThreadId lands on the thread detail drill-in` with a Robolectric `ActivityController.windowFocusChanged` NPE. Re-run alone: passes. Re-run with `--rerun-tasks`: passes. The change under test touches `MainActivity`, `SetupActivity`, `OvernightScreen` and `OvernightSurvival.kt` - no navigation file - so the builder's read that this is an isolation flake in that suite rather than its own defect is well supported. **Why this is worth a row rather than a shrug.** These 18 classes were isolated into `smokeTest*` tasks in the first place because they poison Compose's idle state for whatever runs after them (R-1140), i.e. this suite's defining property is already that its members interfere with each other. A test in that set failing once and passing on retry is therefore exactly what an unfixed instance of the original problem would look like, and "it passed the second time" is the reasoning that would keep it invisible forever. Someone should run `NavSeedTest` repeatedly in its task's real ordering and find out whether the NPE is ordering-dependent - and whichever way that comes out, record it, because the next builder to hit this will otherwise spend the same hour deciding it was not theirs. | process | open |
| R-1175 | The build log carried an unresolved merge for two days and every guard missed it | constitution I, VI; R-1163; spec-check rule 8 | Found by the lead on 2026-09-22 while resolving an ordinary `CHANGELOG.md` merge: the file already contained a `=======` and a `>>>>>>> n-capture-provenance` at lines 2836 and 2913, left from a merge two days earlier. Both entries' content was fully present - only the `<<<<<<<` and its header had been resolved away - so nothing was lost and nothing looked obviously wrong, which is exactly why it survived. Fixed by deleting the two lines and nothing else (`git diff --stat`: 2 deletions). **Three guards should have caught this and each missed it for its own reason, which is the finding.** (1) `spec-check` rule 8 does check for unresolved markers but only across `spec/` - `CHANGELOG.md` is not a spec file, and it is the one file AGENTS.md requires every single commit to touch. (2) Nothing in the gate reads the changelog at all; it is prose, so no compiler, test or linter has an opinion about it. (3) **Four separate past entries record `git grep -n -E "^(<<<<<<<|>>>>>>>)" - no matches, repo-wide` as their verification line.** That command is correct and would have matched line 2913 the moment this landed; the entries predate it, so the claim was true when written and quietly stopped being true afterwards - a verification with no re-run is a verification with a shelf life. **Worth fixing at the source rather than by vigilance**: the cheapest real guard is to extend the existing marker check to the whole repository and run it in the gate, since it costs one `git grep` and the project already believes in it enough to have written it into four changelog entries by hand. Note the naive check is wrong here and the fix must not be naive either: several entries legitimately quote that grep pattern inside backticks while recording that they ran it, so the check must anchor to line start - a substring search reports those and gets itself disabled as noisy, which is the failure mode after this one. | tooling | open |
| R-1176 | A touch-target constraint was present in the source, silently discarded at runtime, and invisible to every test | FR-A11Y-*; WCAG 2.2 AA 2.5.8; constitution VIII; R-1090 | Found by the R-1168 builder from a `uiautomator dump`, not from reasoning, and it generalises well past the control it was found on. The new setup gain `Slider` carried `Modifier.heightIn(min = 44.dp)` in its source. The real node measured **`[65,1530][1195,1608]` - 24 dp tall**, well under the floor. Material 3 1.3 applies `requiredSizeIn` internally, and `requiredSizeIn` **overrides an incoming `heightIn` rather than being bounded by it**, so the modifier was present, looked correct to any reader, and did nothing. Fixed by sizing the thumb's own box (24 dp x 44 dp); the re-dump reads `[65,1530][1195,1672]` - 44.0 dp. **Why this is a register row and not a line in a changelog.** Every ordinary check passes a control in this state. The source review passes - the modifier is right there. Robolectric passes. A screenshot passes, because the control looks fine; the defect is in the *touch* bounds, not the pixels. Only an accessibility-node dump on a real window sees it, which is exactly the class of evidence R-1090 already says this project cannot get from Robolectric. **`heightIn` present and ineffective is strictly worse than `heightIn` absent**, because the absent case is findable by grep and the present case reads as already handled. **Scope, and this is the open half:** 25 files under `app/src/main/kotlin/org/ort/app/ui/` currently constrain a 44 dp target. Any of them wrapping an M3 component that applies its own `requiredSizeIn` - `Slider`, `Switch`, `Checkbox`, `RadioButton`, the icon buttons - may be in the identical state right now, and no test in this repository would say so. The audit is a dump per control against the 44 dp floor, on a device, and it wants doing once and then encoding: the honest permanent guard is a `uiautomator`-driven assertion in the tour rather than a modifier anyone can re-add believing it works. Note the fix must size a box the component does not override, not simply re-state the minimum a second time. | app | open |

The step is right and its scoped run is green (the builder's own captures show the paragraph fully reachable with nothing clipped). But `results/ui-audit/tour-manifest.json` holds 325 entries from run `05120d5d` and will not contain the new step **until the lead runs the full canonical tour**, which is the only run permitted to replace `results/ui-audit/`. | R-1179 | The Bluetooth-permission step has never once captured the Bluetooth-permission screen | R-817; R-940; R-800; R-1005a; R-810, R-811, R-820, R-830; constitution VIII; R-1160, R-1021 | Found by the lead on 2026-09-22 while checking the scoped setup run after four merges: `setup-bt-permission/S02c-bluetooth-permission` came back **changed at all three scales, and no builder had touched `BluetoothPermissionScreen.kt`**. It was not a stale capture. Looking at the pixels, the frame shows *Input* - title *Input*, indicator *4 of 10*, the route list, primary *Verify this input* - and the committed reference PNG shows the identical screen. **Every S02c capture in `results/ui-audit/`, at every font scale, is the Input screen.** The change the diff correctly detected was R-1170's grey-to-green primary on `InputScreen`, appearing on a step named for a screen it has never shown. **Cause, proven by experiment rather than asserted.** `install.ps1:95` runs `pm grant android.permission.BLUETOOTH_CONNECT`. `SetupStateMachine.needsBluetoothPermission` requires `!permissions.bluetoothConnectGranted`, so with the grant in place that gate can never fire and the step the tour asks for via `EXTRA_STEP` is replaced by the natural next one. Verified directly: `pm revoke android.permission.BLUETOOTH_CONNECT`, re-run the three `setup-bt-permission` steps, and the capture is the real screen - *Nearby devices*, *2 of 10*, *Allow nearby devices*, *Not now - use USB instead*, the degraded banner - exactly as `Setup-Bluetooth-Permission.dc.html` draws it. **Nobody was careless.** That grant was added deliberately for R-810/R-811/R-820/R-830, whose Bluetooth rig screens genuinely need the permission, and it fixed them. It simply also made the one screen whose entire purpose is to *request* that permission unreachable - a fix whose blast radius nobody had reason to check, which is the same shape as R-1161 (two correct changes, one defect in their composition). **What this invalidates, stated carefully rather than reflexively.** `Setup-Bluetooth-Permission.dc.html` has never been compared against its screen at any scale. **R-817** is *S02c `@2x` coverage*, closed on *run 3 capture confirmed by reviewer A2* - that confirmation was of the Input screen and is void; reopen it. **R-940** is *S02c at 2.0*, closed on a V11 device confirmation and reviewer A4 - **its conclusion probably still holds** and this row does not claim otherwise, because its actual fix was a trailing spacer added to `SetupScaffold` for *every* setup screen, so the invariant is not S02c-specific; what is void is the S02c evidence, so re-confirm it rather than reopening the finding. R-800 lists S02c among its redrawn boards on the same footing. R-1005a (*S02c / S10b permissions*, still `building`) should be read with this in mind before it closes. **Fix direction, and the precedent is already in the package.** Do not simply stop granting the permission in `install.ps1` - that trades this screen for the four rig-Bluetooth rows it was added for. `DebugMicPermissionOverride` (`app/src/main/kotlin/org/ort/app/ui/setup/`) already exists to let a debug run present a permission state the device does not actually have; a `DebugBluetoothPermissionOverride` of the same shape, set by the tour for these three steps only, is the honest fix and needs no change to `stepFor`. **And the general guard this wants:** a tour step names the screen it intends to capture, and nothing asserts the screen it captured is that one. `SetupActivity.currentStepForTest` is already exposed and already used by the settle - having the runner compare it against the requested step and fail loudly on mismatch would have caught this on the first run, and would catch every future instance of it. **CLOSED, and verified by the lead rather than accepted on report** (P39). `setup-bt-permission/S02c-bluetooth-permission` now captures **its own screen** - the lead rendered the new PNG and read *"Nearby devices"*, *"Bluetooth mode needs one more permission"*, the paired-device bullets and the degraded banner, which is the first time in this program that step has photographed the thing it is named for. Two parts to the fix: `DebugBluetoothPermissionOverride` (the shape `DebugMicPermissionOverride` already established) lets the gate fire honestly despite `install.ps1` granting the permission, and - the part that generalises - `ScreenshotTourActivity.assertStillOnRequestedStep` re-checks `currentStepForTest` **immediately before `drawToBitmap`, not only at the settle**. That placement is the whole point: the settle passed, then the step changed, and the gap between them is exactly why this was invisible for the life of the program. Every future instance now fails loudly. | tooling | closed |
| R-1180 | A test raced virtual time against a real dispatcher and called the result a timeout | R-1139; R-1174; constitution II, VIII | Release `35827213621` went red on `ad5c4924` while CI on the identical commit went green and the lead's own full gate had passed: `2905 tests completed, 1 failed` - `PushedToastChannelTest.R_1139 undoScope is not cancelled by an unrelated coroutine scope completing`, `TimeoutCancellationException: Timed out after 5s of **_virtual_** (kotlinx.coroutines.test) time`. **Nothing in that night's four merges touched this file or anything it imports** - confirmed by diffing the whole merge range - so the first job was to establish it was not collateral, and it was not. **The defect.** Both R-1139 tests ran under `runTest` and launched work on `PushedToastChannel.undoScope`, which is deliberately a **real** `SupervisorJob() + Dispatchers.IO` scope - deliberately, because a scope that can be faked away is not the thing R-1139 needs proven. Under `runTest`, `withTimeout(5_000)` counts **virtual** time, and the virtual clock leaps forward the instant the test scheduler has nothing left to run - which is immediately, because the real work is on `Dispatchers.IO` where the scheduler cannot see it. So the timeout was not bounding the work, it was **racing** it: green whenever `Dispatchers.IO` happened to win, red on a loaded hosted runner. A test that fails for a reason unrelated to the behaviour it asserts, and - worse for this register's purposes - one that would have gone green on a re-run, teaching everyone to re-run it. **Fixed** by giving those two tests `runBlocking` instead of `runTest`, so the five seconds are real wall-clock seconds. Every other test in the file keeps `runTest`, correctly: they drive a plain `Channel` and nothing else, where a virtual clock is exactly right. The kdoc records the distinction in place so the next reader does not 'tidy' it back. **Discrimination run both ways**: with `undoScope` temporarily cancelled at construction both tests fail (and the run takes the real 5s each, which is itself the proof the timeout now bounds rather than races); restored, both pass. `:app:detekt` and `:app:ktlintCheck` green. **The general lesson, and it is the second flake in one night after R-1174**: `runTest` and a real dispatcher must never appear in the same test. The repository should be swept for the pattern - a `runTest` body that touches a production object holding its own `Dispatchers.IO`/`Default` scope - because every instance is the same latent race, and each will present as an unrelated intermittent failure somewhere else. That sweep is this row's open half. **The sweep this row asked for is DONE, and found nothing** (2026-09-24). Every production-owned scope in every module was enumerated and every virtual-time construct in a `runTest` body checked against it: **`PushedToastChannelTest` was the only instance.** That is a trustworthy negative rather than a shallow pass, because it explains its own coverage - `:rig` and `:pipeline` were already swept for exactly this shape under **R-808**, with `UnconfinedTestDispatcher(testScheduler)` injected and kdocs saying why, and `PushedToastChannel` post-dates that sweep and sat outside it. The inventory is in the row: 8 hard-wired production scopes (`DiagnosticsLog.kt:100`, `FieldReportRecorder.kt:74-75`, `RealCaptureService.kt:247`, `OrtApplication.kt:63`, `OrtDatabase.kt:923`, `ScreenFrameCapturer.kt:164`, `AndroidBluetoothLink.kt:129`, plus the fixed one) and nine injectable ones. **The sweep did turn up three latent traps that are not this defect but become it easily** - R-1189, R-1190, R-1191 - and one unrelated finding, R-1192. Row closed; those carry the remainder. | process | closed |
| R-1181 | The new log-header frequency row is contradicted by the overs directly beneath it | R-1167; AC-202; constitution I, VIII | Judged by the lead from the R-1167 builder's own capture (`p39-captures-v2/overnight/L01-log.png`), and it is the kind of thing only a screenshot shows. The header renders **`Frequency / not set`** with the note *"No radio is reporting a frequency. Overs are logged without one until you enter it."* - while the quick-filter chips immediately below it read **`145.230`** and **`146.960`**, and every over in the list carries a real value in its `FREQ` column. A reader sees the screen assert something the same screen disproves. **The production logic is right and is not what this row is about.** The mapper reads `CaptureConfigurationStore.current().manualFrequencyHz` - what `RealCaptureService` actually consumes at session start - which is the correct source, and *not set* is the honest answer for it. The per-over `FREQ` column is a different fact: what each transmission was recorded against. Both are true; shown together without qualification they read as a contradiction. **Two things to fix, and they are separable.** (1) **The scenario, most urgently**, because this is about to be baked into canonical evidence: the `overnight` fixture seeds per-over frequencies and `SetupStore` but leaves `CaptureConfigurationStore` empty, so the tour's permanent capture of this header will forever show a state no real operator reaches. Seed the configuration store to match, and add a fixture that genuinely has no frequency anywhere if the empty state needs its own frame. (2) **The copy's scope.** *Overs are logged without one until you enter it* is a claim about every over; it is only true of overs from here on. An operator who set a frequency, captured, then cleared it would see the same contradiction with a correctly seeded store, so this is not solely a fixture problem. Scope the sentence to what is actually being claimed. **Why no test caught it:** the 23 tests the builder wrote are good and all pass - they exercise the header against its own facts object, in isolation. Nothing composed it above a list whose rows carry frequencies, which is the only arrangement in which the sentence is false. That is the difference between a unit test and a screenshot, and it is why constitution VIII says the screenshot wins until explained. | app | open |
| R-1182 | A second frequency field in Settings is written, read back, and never reaches capture | R-1167; R-1171; R-1132, R-1139, R-1141; constitution I | Reported by the R-1167 builder as outside its ownership. `SettingsStore.manualFrequencyMhz` is a **second, entirely separate** manual-frequency value from the one capture actually consumes. `SettingsContent`'s edit action writes it and `SettingsPolling` reads it back to render CF02 - so it round-trips convincingly and looks wired - but `RealCaptureService` reads `CaptureConfiguration.manualFrequencyHz` from `CaptureConfigurationStore`, which this field never reaches. **Editing the frequency in Settings today changes a label and nothing else**, and because the label updates, it confirms itself. This is the same built-but-never-connected pattern as R-1171's three capture switches and the six rows before it, with one aggravating difference: those switches do nothing and look like they do nothing on close inspection, whereas this one **displays the operator's own input back to them**, which is the most persuasive possible evidence that it worked. Now that R-1167 has given the value a real home in the log header, the resolution is probably deletion rather than wiring - two fields for one fact is how they drifted apart - but that is a judgement about which surface should own it, so decide it deliberately and say so. | app | open |
| R-1183 | The tour navigation helper replays taps at a resolution the tour never uses | R-1149; constitution VIII | Reported by the R-1167 builder, which lost time to it and worked around it rather than fixing someone else's tooling. `tools/ui-audit/nav.ps1` replays recorded taps at **1080x2400**, the emulator's *physical* size, while `install.ps1`/`tour.ps1` deliberately leave the device at the **1260x2772** override every canonical capture is taken at (R-1149 made that override mandatory and made `tour.ps1` refuse without it). Every coordinate is therefore off by the ratio between the two, so the script taps the wrong places - and because a wrong tap near an edge can hit the system back gesture, it can navigate out of the app entirely, which presents as the script mysteriously failing rather than as a coordinate bug. The builder's workaround was to drive `ScenarioReaderActivity` directly instead. That works and is arguably better practice anyway, but the script is still there and still wrong, and it will cost the next person an hour for exactly the same reason. | R-1189 | A production timeout wraps caller-supplied code, so every caller decides whether it is a race | R-1180; R-808; AC-131 | The sharpest finding of the R-1180 sweep, and the one worth acting on. `WorkQueue.kt:205` runs `withTimeoutOrNull(remaining) { execute() }` where `execute` is **supplied by the caller**. If any caller passes a lambda that touches Room, the work dispatches onto `OrtDatabase.kt:923`'s real `Executors.newCachedThreadPool`, the test scheduler sees itself idle, and the virtual clock fires the deadline instantly - R-1180 exactly, but **created by the caller rather than visible at the timeout**. No lexical rule can see it: `PassDrainRunner.kt:26` passes `execute` as a parameter, so nothing at `WorkQueue.kt:205` knows what it wraps. **It has already bitten once**: `ReprocessRunnerTest.kt:41-51` is `runBlocking` specifically because of it and documents the reproduction. Today's other callers are safe **by accident of their fixtures** - `WorkQueueTest.kt:134`/`:496` pass `delay(Long.MAX_VALUE/2)` (pure virtual, deterministic) and `WorkQueueTest.kt:143`/`PassDrainRunnerTest.kt:50,80` pass non-suspending lambdas. Nothing enforces any of that, and the next test to pass a Room-touching lambda inherits an intermittent failure whose cause is two modules away from its symptom. **Fix direction:** give `WorkQueue` a timeout/dispatcher seam so the bound is injectable, or make `runLeased` assert loudly when it is running on a `TestCoroutineScheduler` without explicit permission. Either converts a silent trap into a loud one, which is the whole of what is wanted here. | data | open |
| R-1190 | One of sixteen flush call sites breaks the convention the other fifteen keep | R-1180; R-1189 | `ProseDigestRunnerTest.kt:102` is the **only** one of 16 `DiagnosticsLog.flush()` call sites in the repository not wrapped in `runBlocking`. It awaits a `CompletableDeferred` that is completed on `DiagnosticsLog.kt:100`'s real `Dispatchers.IO` consumer, from inside a `runTest` body. **It is not a race today** and the row should not pretend otherwise: the await is a plain suspension, bounded by `runTest`'s own 60-second **wall-clock** timeout rather than by virtual time, so it behaves. It is filed because a single added `withTimeout` inside that body turns it into R-1180 precisely, and because a convention kept by 15 of 16 sites is one nobody will notice breaking at the 16th. Cheap to close and cheap to guard: a grep-level check that `DiagnosticsLog.flush` and `FieldReportRecorder.flush` are only called inside `runBlocking` has zero false positives and catches every future copy. | pipeline | open |
| R-1191 | A test waits a fixed 200 ms for a coroutine to reach a state, and calls it a margin | R-1180; R-1174; constitution II | From the R-1180 sweep's inverse list - tests that wait in **real** time - and this is the one that will actually fail rather than merely be slow. `ReprocessWorkerTest.kt:421` runs `delay(200)` inside `runBlocking`, commented *"a real margin for the coroutine to actually reach the frozen wait loop"*, while racing an `async(Dispatchers.Default)`. **That is a margin, not a bound**: on a saturated hosted runner 200 ms is not reliably enough for a coroutine on a contended dispatcher to reach a particular line, and when it is not, the test fails for a reason having nothing to do with what it asserts - then passes on a re-run, which is how R-1174 and R-1180 both nearly escaped. Replace the sleep with a real synchronisation point the test can await (a latch, a `CompletableDeferred` the production code completes, or a state the test can poll with a generous bound). Two more from the same list, lower priority and both defensible: the nine `RealCaptureService*Test` files each carry their own copy of `waitUntil(timeoutMillis, intervalMillis = 100) { Thread.sleep(...) }` with budgets up to 20 s (correct - that service's scope has no seam - but this is where the suite's wall-clock cost and load sensitivity live, and nine copies of one helper is its own smell), and `LevelMeterTest.kt:157-162` asserts throughput with `measureTimeMillis` across a real thread, which is a performance assertion wearing a correctness assertion's clothes. | pipeline | open |
| R-1192 | Every network test does real blocking socket I/O on the test thread, and so does production | R-1180; FR-NET-*; constitution VII | Turned up by the R-1180 sweep while establishing what was *not* a virtual-time race. `:net`'s clients - `RealAnalyticsUploadClient.kt:116`, `RealFieldReportUploadClient.kt:249` - use blocking `HttpURLConnection` with socket timeouts, and there is **no `Dispatchers.IO` hop anywhere in `net/src/main`**. So the module's ~31 `runTest` tests perform real blocking socket I/O on the test thread. Correctly, that is not a virtual-time race and the sweep was right to classify it out. **But the same fact is true of production**, which is the part that wants a decision: whether every caller of these clients wraps them off the main thread was outside that audit's scope and is not established anywhere. Given constitution IV (capture never blocks) and VII (only `:net` links an HTTP client), a blocking client with no dispatcher of its own is a contract that every caller must honour and none is checked against. Establish whether the callers do, and if the answer is that they all must, consider moving the hop inside `:net` where it can be guaranteed once instead of remembered everywhere. | net | open |
| R-1193 | The app asks permission to share audio and has no code that could ever share it | FR-ANL-4; AC-174; D42; constitution I; R-1171, R-1182 | The highest-harm finding of the dead-path census, and it is on the screen P39 shipped hours ago. **Analytics tier 3 has no producer anywhere.** `AnalyticsEvent.kt:36` declares `tier3` and `AnalyticsPayload.kt:106` declares its payload; the toggle is offered on Welcome (`SetupActivity.kt:1369,1382`) and in Settings (`SettingsContent.kt:314`), the choice is persisted (`AnalyticsTierPreferences.kt:31`) - and **the sole construction of a tier-3 payload in the entire repository is `telemetry/src/test/.../AnalyticsControllerTest.kt:74`**. Settings tells the operator it means *"Audio (tier 3) - Retained over audio together with its corrected transcript"* (`SettingsAnalyticsScreen.kt:84-87`). **Tier 2 is half-dead in the same way**: `Tier2Payload.Transcript` (`AnalyticsPayload.kt:90`) has no producer either; the only production tier-2 path is `CorrectionAnalytics.kt:23`, which builds `.Correction`. Settings says tier 2 is *"Transcript text and callsigns"*. **Why this is worse than an ordinary dead switch, and the reason it leads this batch.** Nothing leaks - the failure is safe in the privacy direction - but the app **asks the operator for consent to something it cannot do**, on the first screen of a brand-new install, under a heading about sharing their own recorded audio. A person who turns it on has been asked to make a real decision about third parties' speech and been given a false account of what follows. Under constitution I that is the same defect class as a confident wrong callsign, and it is squarely FR-ANL-14's neighbourhood: we corrected the claim that nothing is ever uploaded while shipping a toggle implying something is. **The honest fix is a judgement, not a build.** Either implement the producers, or say plainly on both surfaces that these tiers are not yet collected in this build - the pattern `SettingsContributeScreen.kt:100-102` already uses for the contribution channel (*"no upload client exists in :net yet"*) and that `SettingsAnalyticsScreen.kt:55-57` already uses for the missing endpoint. **The disclosure exists for the destination and not for the payload**, which is why this survived. Do not leave a consent control whose behaviour is unimplemented and undisclosed. | app | open |
| R-1194 | Two crash-recovery paths are specified, written, and never run | FR-RUN-8; AC-47; constitution IV; R-1132 | Two independent recovery mechanisms exist in `:data` and nothing calls either. Both are data-integrity, not cosmetics. **(1) `TransmissionDao.kt:56` `findAllProcessing()` - zero callers, zero tests, anywhere in the repository.** Its own KDoc states the contract: *"Crash recovery (FR-RUN-8 -> AC-47): every transmission left `PROCESSING` at launch."* The launch sweep that does exist (`OrtApplication.kt:187`) recovers **work-queue leases by run id**, which is a different thing: a transmission row stranded in `PROCESSING` by a crash is stranded **permanently**, with no surface that will ever revisit it. **(2) `WorkQueueDao.kt:46` `selectExpired(now)` - zero callers, and the one `WorkQueueDao` method `WorkQueue.kt` never calls.** `lease()` writes a `deadlineAt` (`WorkQueueDao.kt:41`) that nothing reads. So a pass that hangs **within the current run** is never reclaimed; recovery happens only on a process restart, because that mints a new run id. The deadline column is the mechanism for the in-run case and it is inert. **Together they mean the recovery story is a restart, not a sweep** - and a device that never fully restarts, which is exactly the overnight-capture case this product is for, never recovers at all. Constitution IV: a route that is not verified never silently proceeds; the same discipline should apply to work that silently never resumes. Note AC-47 is presently scored as covered - see R-1196 for why that is not evidence. | data | open |
| R-1195 | The Meter action is wired to a parameter marked unused, under a comment asserting it works | R-1182; R-1021; constitution I | `CaptureStatusContent.kt:82-84` declares `openLevelMeter`/`openLiveMonitor` with `@Suppress("UNUSED_PARAMETER")`, and production still passes real lambdas into them from `OrtNavHost.kt:1953-1954` (set at `:508` and `:841`). Tapping **Settings > Capture > Meter** therefore does nothing that the parameter describes. **What makes this a register row rather than a tidy-up is the comment four lines above it.** `OrtNavHost.kt:1944-1947` asserts the tap *"lands here directly on `LiveMonitorScreen`"*. A reviewer reading that file is told the wire works by the file itself; the `@Suppress` that contradicts it is in a different file. This is the R-1182 shape - a live-looking wire into nothing - with the aggravating feature that the codebase actively vouches for it. Note the screens themselves (`LevelMeterScreen`, `LiveMonitorScreen`, `CaptureStatusScreen`) are deliberately superseded and kept, documented at `CaptureStatusContent.kt:46-52` under P9's never-delete-quietly rule - **that part is fine and is not the defect**. The defect is an action that still offers to reach them. | app | open |
| R-1196 | The coverage matrix scores a dead feature as fully covered, and the asymmetry that proves it | R-1193; R-1194; constitution VI, VIII; R-1140, R-1160, R-1163 | The dead-path census's sharpest structural finding, and it explains how ten instances of built-but-never-called got past every gate. **`buildSrc/.../CoverageMatrix.kt:18-29` maps requirement ids to test-function names.** A feature with no production caller but a well-named test therefore scores 100%. **The worked example is AC-174 (analytics tier 3), listed as covered at `results/coverage-matrix.md:112`** by `AC_174_tier_3_is_never_queued_while_disabled` - **a negative assertion that a dead producer satisfies perfectly**, and which would go on passing if every line of tier 3 were deleted. The tell is an asymmetry: tier 2 has an `AC_173_tier_2_queues_once_enabled` counterpart and **tier 3 has no positive test at all**. A requirement covered only by negatives is a requirement nothing proves. **The general blind spot, stated once so it stops being rediscovered:** every gate this project has asks *is this declared and consistent?* and none asks *does production reach it?* `dependencyRules` validates the module graph, and a dead symbol sits on a permitted edge. `platformGuards` checks artifacts. `spec_check.py` never opens a `.kt` file - its whole function list is ids, refs, traceability, encoding. detekt runs only `UnusedPrivateMember`, and this repo is `explicitApi()` with hand-rolled DI, so nearly everything is `public` and the rule cannot fire. **And constitution VIII's visual verification is the deepest blind spot of all: a dead switch renders pixel-identically to a live one, so the capture matches the artboard - the evidence is the camouflage.** Two things worth doing, in order: (1) audit the coverage matrix for **other requirements carried only by negative assertions**, which is a mechanical query over the existing mapping and probably a short list; (2) treat a requirement's coverage as unproven unless at least one test constructs the thing in production shape. See R-1197 for the reachability guard the census recommends. | tooling | open |
| R-1197 | A reachability guard is worth building for three closed inventories, and not for anything else | R-1196; R-1171; R-1182; R-1188; R-1193; R-1194 | The dead-path census's recommendation, recorded with its limits intact because the limits are what make it shippable. Build a `reachability` task in `buildSrc`, following the existing `PlatformGuardsTask` precedent, covering exactly **three closed inventories**: **(1) every `SharedPreferences` key constant** (~50, all in `KEY_*` companions) must have at least one write and one read in `src/main`, **and at least one reader outside the package that writes it** - that last clause is the non-obvious part, and it is precisely what distinguishes a live setting from R-1171's render-yourself loop. **(2) every `@Dao` method** (145 of them) must have a `src/main` reference outside its own declaration file, or - where the only one is a same-file `@Transaction` default - that wrapper must have one. The census resolved all 145 by hand and reports this rule catches **24 of 24** dead ones with no false positives. **(3) every destination/step enum member and every `*Screen(` composable under `ui/`** must have one `src/main` invocation; this one has real false positives (previews, deliberately superseded screens) and needs an allowlist. Escapes go in one `reachability-allowlist.md` with a **reason column and a register row id** - the shape the design inventory already uses for accepted deviations - and never a silent `@Suppress`, because the allowlist entry *is* the disclosure. **What not to build, on the census's own advice:** a general unused-public-API gate across 21 `explicitApi()` modules. Interface implementations, Compose, `kotlinx.serialization`, Room codegen and the project's own behavioural fakes will all trip it, and a guard that cries wolf is `-x`'d within a week. If broader coverage is wanted, make it a **ratchet rather than a gate** - emit a count to a committed baseline and fail only when it grows. Keep the manual census too, scoped to these ~400 symbols, once per release rather than per push. | tooling | open |
| R-1198 | Twenty-four of one hundred and forty-five DAO methods are unreachable, and one is a trap | R-1194; R-1196; R-1197 | The census resolved every `@Dao` method in `:data` by hand: **24 of 145 (16%) have no production caller.** R-1194 covers the two that are recovery paths; this row holds the rest so the list is not lost. `CatalogDao.kt:223,226` (contribution items), `:241,244` (calibration), `:247,250` (asset), `:253,256` (operator location); `TransmissionDao.kt:22` `observeById` - **the only `Flow` DAO method in `:data`, and nothing observes it**; `TransmissionLabelDao.kt:23`; `TranscriptDao.kt:48,56` (`searchAll`/`searchCurrent`, superseded by `SearchDao.search`); `ProseSummaryDao.kt:24` `listAll`, whose only `src/main` caller is `FakeProseSummaryStore` - a test double that lives in `src/main`, which is its own small finding. **The one that is actively dangerous is `CatalogDao.kt:235,238`: a duplicate `CorrectionEntity` insert and read.** Production corrections go through `CorrectionDao`, which maintains invariants; this second path compiles, is the obvious API to reach for from `CatalogDao`, and **writes to the same table** without them. It is not dead code waiting to be deleted - it is a loaded footgun waiting to be called. Delete it first, separately, and say so. Also filed here rather than separately: **`station_summary` is an entire table with no consumer anywhere** - entity `CatalogEntities.kt:352`, DAO `CatalogDao.kt:259,262`, zero references in main, debug **or** test - superseded by the live `ProseSummaryEntity`/`RoomProseSummaryStore`. Two per-station summary tables is R-1182 at the data layer, and the schema currently implies the LLM summary is persisted in the dead one. | data | open |
| R-1199 | Provenance and history are recorded where nothing can ever read them | constitution III, VI; R-1194 | Two findings with the same shape: a guarantee satisfied at the write and unavailable at every read. **`SetupStore.verifiedResamplerIdentity` (`SetupStore.kt:42`)** is written at `SetupActivity.kt:775` and cleared at `SetupStore.kt:193`, and **read by nothing outside tests**. It is a *provenance* field - constitution VI makes determinism bounded by resampler identity - so its presence implies the identity of the resampler that produced a session's audio is recoverable. It is recorded and discarded: no screen, no debug dump, no diagnostic bundle reads it. Contrast `verifiedAudioSource` (`:53`), added last night, which **is** read at `ReadyScreen.kt:418` - the difference is instructive and makes the fix obvious. **`StationIdentityDao.kt:87` `voiceprintBindingHistoryFor` and `:124` `priorWeightHistoryFor`** are written on the real production path (via `bindVoiceprintToStation` / `updatePriorWeight`) and read only from `StationIdentityDaoTest` and `CorrectionPollingTest`. Constitution III's *"nothing is deleted quietly"* is honoured in storage and unreachable in practice: **the guarantee is storable, not inspectable.** A history no surface can show is indistinguishable, to the operator, from one that was never kept - which is the exact belief the principle exists to prevent. | app | open |
| R-1200 | Three dead UI subtrees, and an enum that looks like the failure registry and is not | R-1188; constitution I | The remainder of the census's UI findings, filed together because the resolution is the same judgement for all of them - delete, or route to them, and say which. **`FailureId` (`FailureViewState.kt:18-37`) is an 18-member enum with zero references in the entire repository** - main, test and debug alike; the only two occurrences are its own declaration. It is documented as *"one `FailureId` per artboard"*, so it reads exactly like the registry tying F1..F23 to their screens, while the real dispatch is the sealed `FailurePresentation` (`FailureMapper.kt:103-130`). **Anyone adding a failure mode will wire the wrong type**, and the doc comment will confirm their mistake. That is the harm; the dead members are incidental. **`TransmissionListScreen.kt:28`** (plus its private `TransmissionRowContent` at `:46`) is the app's former reader, referenced nowhere - and `AndroidManifest.xml:74` still names it in the comment describing the reader. **`PlaceholderScreen.kt:21`** exists for a `hasScreen == false` branch (`ReaderDestination.kt:9-11`) that no longer exists, since every destination now has a screen. Also worth a line: **`FailurePresentation.StorageAudioPaused` (`FailureMapper.kt:117`) is constructed only in `LiveBarPollingTest.kt:269`** - not even a debug scenario builds it, so unlike its five siblings it is not covered by `DebugFailureOverride`'s documented deferral and has no way to be seen at all. | app | open |
